## 2026-09-22 (Opus 5) — THE FIRESTARTER "DETERIORATION" IS A RECOGNITION FAILURE, NOT A BUILD ONE — AND I ANSWERED IT OFF THREE DIFFERENT POPULATIONS

- **THE ONE THING TO CARRY: the user said *"I think you already mixed different cases (firestarter and cli) again"* — no clitarget row had entered the numbers, and the mixing was real anyway, one level in.** Census of the run dirs: 75 firestarter, 24 clitarget, 3 flightsim; every filter was `target.txt == firestarter` and every cited run verified individually. **What was actually mixed was three of MY OWN populations for one claim:** outcome windows from the 54 firestarter runs that have a ledger row; the productivity table from a date cutoff that silently gave 19 and 21 runs under a "last 20" header; the residue analysis from all 71 firestarter dirs with a `GoalStack.json`, **17 of which have no ledger row and are precisely the 2026-08-24..08-31 runs carrying the single-boot-gate-leaf shape I then generalised from**. A denominator is part of a claim. **And my `sorted(x)[len//2]` is not a median for even n** — that alone moved window A's duration from 44.4 to 50.0 min and put two numbers for one quantity in consecutive messages. Extends [[feedback-dont-evaluate-a-build-with-older-runs]] from targets to populations.
- **CANONICAL NUMBERS — one population, stated so nothing has to be re-derived: firestarter only, ledger rows, strict last-20 (B: `2026-09-02`..`2026-09-21`) against previous-20 (A: `2026-08-26`..`2026-09-02`), real medians.**
  - duration **44.4 -> 27.5 min** · cycles **36 -> 23** · merges/cycle **0.27 -> 0.31** · rejection rate **51% -> 56%** · compile-probe share of rejections **80% -> 71%**
  - **23/23: 10/20 -> 9/20. `GOAL_FULFILLED`: 10/20 -> 4/20.** Conditional on a 23/23 artifact, fulfilment **9/10 -> 4/9** (Fisher p=0.057).
  - **The loop's ability to BUILD did not measurably change. Its ability to RECOGNISE that it is finished halved, and the runs got about 40% shorter — a cap, not the loop.** Deadline runs end at their cap, so the caps went from roughly 90 min in August to 25-45 min recently.
- **RESIDUE, same 40 runs:** zero-residue runs **11/20 -> 5/19** · root COMPLETED **10 -> 4** · ended `SMOKE: 23/23` with an uncompleted root **1 -> 5** · runs that reached the closure edge at all **11/20 -> 7/20**. ACTIVE-leaf origins: **boot-gate 4 -> 17**, coverage-retro 16 -> 17, planner 3 -> 14, analysis 19 -> 5, divided-criterion 13 -> 3.
- **WHY ONE LEAF IS FATAL RATHER THAN SLOW:** `GoalPlannerService.autoCompleteRootIfSatisfied()` early-returns while ANY subgoal is ACTIVE, and every closure gate (`T4-CV`, `T2-DR`, `T4-AN-8`, `T4-DO`, `T4-BV`, `T4-BW`, `T4-BP-3`) sits past that line. The guard is silent — no log line, no event — so a run that never asked looks identical to one where nothing happened.
- **AND THE INSTRUMENT FOR THAT QUESTION LIES: `closure_attempts` in the run summary is `grep -c 'root closure blocked'` — it counts BLOCKS, not attempts.** A fulfilling run and a never-asked run both read 0. I built a claim on it before reading what it counts, and had to withdraw "the closure edge was never reached" for the older group, where it had in fact been reached and blocked 18 to 34 times per run ([[feedback-grep-the-behaviour-not-the-name]]).
- **BOTH "IS IT ACTUALLY DONE?" PATHS ARE WIRED TO HALT EDGES THE WALL-CLOCK KILL BYPASSES.** `T2-EH`/`T4-BP` verify-before-pause re-verifies every ACTIVE leaf against the current output and then attempts root completion — its only caller is `RootConvergenceTracker`, the loop's own brake. `T2-EQ`'s `clearPackagingBuildFixSubgoals()` has one caller, `PreHaltPackagingCheck`. **`deadline` — now the dominant terminal — is preflight's external kill and reaches neither.** The loop asks whether it is finished when it decides to stop, never when it is stopped.
- **`T4-DW` FILED (Tier 4, `roadmap.md`, overview line added):** a boot-gate work item has no observation that can retire it. `clearActiveBuildFixSubgoals()` retires only unstamped items on COMPILE_OK and `T2-EQ` only package-gate ones; `GATE_BOOT`/`GATE_TEST`/`GATE_CRITERIA` have no mirror, though `BlockerSubgoalInjector`'s own javadoc states the principle for every gate. The observation already exists — `EvaluationAgent.runOriginatingGate()` re-runs the stamped gate and `T4-CC` spends the answer on accepting a mutation. Field evidence: `2026-09-13_22-45-39` (4 later boot-OKs) and `2026-09-19_23-56-29` (1 after each of 3 leaves) kept their leaves. **The ticket says plainly it explains 2 of the 5**, and carries the abstention guard: boot `result=OK` coexists with **1360 of 2693 (A) and 775 of 1544 (B) criteria never judged**, so an OK that judged nothing must not retire anything ([[project-false-completion-2]]).
- **THE PROBE-DISAGREEMENT STORY IS REAL AND ITS EVIDENCE SITS OUTSIDE THE WINDOW — recorded here so it is not re-derived as if it were current.** In five runs of `2026-08-22`..`2026-08-24` the LAST root-closure block came AFTER the last merge, four of them naming the same criterion (*"POST /loans returns HTTP 422 — if exceeded"*, application answered 201) on the very tree that later scored 23/23. Mechanism, read off one run's final closure attempt: `probed=26 skipped=24 contradicted=5`, with setup requests refused by the running application — `POST /books {"title":"value","isbn":"value"}` refused 400 by the spec's own validation, `alice@example.com` refused 409 because an earlier probe created her, loans refused 409 because a book was still on loan. **All probes share one running instance whose state accumulates, and the fixtures use placeholder values the spec rejects.** Those runs are in neither 20-run window; what IS inside is the never-judged rate above, roughly unchanged at ~50%.
- **🔴 THE INSIGHT THIS SESSION KEPT WALKING PAST, and the user had to name it: the loop can prove a goal UNFINISHED in microseconds and has no path that proves one FINISHED.** `EndpointSurfaceQuery.endpointFromWorkItem` + `isBuilt` is deterministic, reads the in-memory file map, and costs no build, boot or model. It is consumed in exactly two places and both are negative — `isOpenStructuralRepairLeaf` (true when NOT built) and `reconcileCriteriaAgainstStructure` (un-banks a criterion when NOT built, *"structure wins over the banked read"*). **Nothing ever concludes "this leaf IS built, so it is already solved."**
  - **Recognising that a goal is already solved is a PRIORITY in its own right, not a tidy-up at the edge of death.** I filed `T2-FL` with a "do NOT make it periodic" constraint justified by `T2-FH` — which measured BRAKING decisions, not re-verification. A category error, and the cheap check has no cost argument against it. Corrected in place: two tiers, structural promotion run regularly, LLM criteria verification once at the budget edge.
  - **The boundary that keeps it honest:** a mapping existing is not the behaviour being right. Structural promotion may answer a structural demand (*"Implement POST /loans"*) and must never answer a behavioural criterion — that distinction is what [[project-false-completion-2]] and [[project-false-completion-3]] were built from.
- **`T4-DX` BUILT IN FULL AND READ OVER THREE FIELD RUNS; `T2-FL` FILED FOR WHAT THE LAST RUN EXPOSED.** The probe could not establish the state its criteria require: `FieldConstraintDiscovery` was REACHED AND BLIND (it found `ISBN_PATTERN` and could not bind `.matcher(book.getIsbn())`), one of two body paths was never wired (`candidateBodies` passed `Map.of()` constraints, and FOUR criterion shapes reach their setup body through it), and a constrained field sampled to the same value for every criterion. All three fixed; suite 3153/0/5.
  - **The measure that survived is PER-RUN, and the cross-run one did not.** `unparsed` moved 16 -> 27 -> 30, so each run authored a different criteria set and the judged share is not comparable — the DoD's unchanged-counters clause caught that, which is what it was for. **Placeholder bodies on the wire: 17 -> 4 -> 0 -> 0**, predicate verified against each log so the zero is an absence. Invalid-fixture refusals gone (zero 400s), collisions 7 -> 4 with the remaining four UNATTRIBUTED.
  - **Each of the three defects was found by a FIELD RUN and none by the suite** — both body paths were unit-tested and only one was wired. A green suite tells you a mechanism works; only a run tells you which mechanism the loop actually calls.
  - **Run `2026-09-22_15-59-33` then showed the OTHER problem in its purest form: `deadline` at 23/23 final and preserved, peaked at cycle 33 of 69, 36-cycle drift tail, 4 ACTIVE leaves all naming endpoints the artifact SERVES, none gate-stamped** (so `T4-DW` fired zero times, correctly). **`T2-EH` already built the verification that answers this and it is closed, wired only to the convergence brake; `T2-FH` already computes the remaining budget at the run's own pace and spends it only on braking.** Both halves exist, nothing joins them, and `deadline` is the terminal runs end on — filed as `T2-FL`. **Fifth capability-outruns-routing instance of the day.**
- **`T4-DW` BUILT AND FIELD-RUN THE SAME DAY. Suite 3142/0/5; run `2026-09-22_05-11-32` (firestarter, 75-min cap) ended `GOAL_FULFILLED` at 23/23 final and preserved, 32 cycles, 7 merges, 27.8 min, peaked at the FINAL cycle, drift tail 0, ZERO ACTIVE leaves — the first firestarter fulfilment on a perfect artifact since `2026-09-12_22-44-28`.** The retirement fired once, six seconds after the merge that wrote the entry point. **The causal claim is NOT made and must not be inherited as one:** the run's two `serves` items closed by ordinary score-based closure, the single closure block was `T4-BP-3`, and n=1.
  - **The run's real yield is a routing limit in the build itself — fourth instance of [[project-capability-outruns-routing]], in a ticket that cites that note.** The `serves` retirement is wired only to `BehaviouralSelfCheck`, which ran **3 times in 32 cycles** (17 intervals abstained on `COMPILE_ERROR`, exactly `T2-FK`'s measurement), while 5 of the run's 6 boot-gate OKs arrived through the staged-candidate re-run or the closure edge. **And that self-correction was ITSELF wrong, retracted the same day:** the closure edge reaches its gates only past `if (anyActive) return;`, so when its boot gate runs **zero subgoals are ACTIVE and there is nothing to retire** — a call site there would fire on an empty set forever. Provenance of the run's 6 OKs: 1 self-check, **5 closure-edge**, 0 staged-candidate. **There is no slice 2 of that shape.** The real constraint is that a `serves` retirement needs an on-disk observation COINCIDING with an open item, which only the self-check provides, and it abstained 17 of 20 intervals — `T2-FK`'s trigger half, which its own body says not to build on those numbers.
  - **The pattern to carry, and it now has two layers: a mechanism can be correct, tested, reachable by its own test, and still routed to a link that fires 3 times in 32 cycles — AND the obvious fix for that can be void.** I proposed a closure-edge call site from an OK COUNT without asking what state the loop was in when those OKs were taken; the guard that made the edge unreachable for closure also makes it empty for retirement. Ask which state produced an observation before routing anything to it. The unit test proved the call site exists on the per-cycle path; only the field showed how often that path ABSTAINS. A reachability assertion answers "is it called", never "is it called when it matters".
- **Open and cheap, if picked up:** replay a frozen tree with a fresh application instance per criterion and see whether `contradicted` goes to zero — the probe-isolation question, answerable without a live run.

## 2026-09-21 evening (Opus 5) — T6-M CLOSED `[x]`, AND BOTH T6 INSTRUMENTS WERE WRONG BEFORE THEY WERE READ

- **THE ONE THING TO CARRY: a guard is only proven for the target its tests were written on, and this project now has three targets.** `failure_family.py`'s `outcome()` exists precisely to keep a HEALTHY run's mid-run compile noise out of the failure distribution — its docstring says exactly that — and it parsed the score with `(\d+)/23`. That is firestarter's suite; clitarget scores out of 19 and an earlier yardstick out of 12. **23 of 96 archived runs that SCORED ABOVE ZERO were being classified as failures, seven of them perfect scores**, including the GOAL_FULFILLED run from earlier the same day. All 21 existing tests used `23/23`. The guard was correct and its denominator was not.
  - **It is the ticket's OWN filed defect recurring inside the tool built to answer it.** T6-M's body already records the baseline being computed by regexing `\d+/23` out of `smoke` and dropping non-numeric rows. Same regex, same field, one axis over: that one dropped rows with no number, this one dropped rows with a different denominator.
  - **A second defect in the same tool, found by the evening's own run:** `classify_boot` never checked the packaging signature, so `no main manifest attribute` — a LAUNCH-time symptom whose build log is empty by definition — fell through to the weaker run-log fallback. Both fixes known-negative checked (revert, exactly the new tests fail). 26 tests.
- **`T6-M` CLOSED `[x]` — distribution over 96 archived runs, 18 failing, `unknown=0`: 5 compile-missing-type · 4 wiring · 3 dependency-missing · 3 compile-other · 1 compile-missing-member · 2 fallback-healthy. NO FAMILY DOMINATES, which is the finding** — the ticket assumed the 24 runs that never reached a working artifact were one class that would name an owning ticket. **Report it SPLIT BY PROVENANCE:** only 7 of 18 come from the scorer's own logs, and the two halves disagree about which family leads — on authoritative evidence wiring leads (4 · 2 · 2); the fallback's top family groups five members by diagnostic shape that do not share a mechanism.
- **The wiring family traced to a live blind spot in `T3-CA`, and the base rate says leave it alone.** 3 of 4 wiring rows carry an identical mechanism (same missing `BookRepository` bean); two predate T3-CA's ship dates, **`2026-09-03_19-05-02` does not** — zero firings four days after the field proof. Cause, read off the runs' own rendered sources: T3-CA needs same-package peers to carry the registration UNANIMOUSLY. Working run 13/13 and 13/13 with one outlier; failing run `MemberRepository` 5/5 but `LoanRepository` **1/5** and `BookRepository` **1/5** — two of three unannotated, **no unanimous norm left to be an outlier from**. Second instance of [[project-detector-went-silent-as-the-fault-spread]], in a different detector; a unanimity rule fails the same way a majority rule does, one step earlier.
  - **Base rate measured before deciding anything: across 51 post-actuator runs T3-CA fired in 4 and ALL FOUR avoided the failure; exactly 1 run hit it, with no firing.** So the spread case is n=1 of 1 opportunity — recorded on the ticket and deliberately NOT built, with a cheap re-entry watch (`No qualifying bean` in artifact-boot.log plus zero `T3-CA:` lines).
- **`T6-L` re-measured and STAYS OPEN. all-time 56% · newer half 68% · last 12 62%** (was 37/48/62). **Two things matter more than the headline:** the combined figure is a mean of shares of DIFFERENT instruments (firestarter 23, clitarget 19) while the 75% was calibrated on firestarter — split, **firestarter last-10 is 77%**; and that is the **SECOND** time the number has been reached, which the ticket's own body already says does not close it (clause 3, the originated-failure profile, is untouched). The window also spans three builds, so it is not a rate.
- **🔴 FIELD RUN `2026-09-21_14-29-16` (firestarter, 60-min cap, halted at 29.4 min): T4-DO's repair fired correctly and T4-DQ's halt-edge rewind undid it 300ms later.** Repair at `15:00:13.045` naming `LibraryApplication`, build OK `15:00:19.962`, stall `15:00:19.965`, **`REWOUND to cycle-1789993952159` at `15:00:20.322`**. Proven with artifacts, not timestamps: post-repair snapshot pom has `mainClass`, the rewind target does not, and **`output/pom.xml` is byte-identical to the rewind target**. **The invariant: at the halt edge the restored state IS the deliverable, and a deterministic repair applied after the rewind target has no later cycle in which to re-derive itself.** Recorded on `T4-DO`, NOT filed — the disposition is the user's.
  - **COST ON THIS RUN WAS ZERO and I said so rather than banking the finding as a save** ([[feedback-activity-not-progress]]). The preserve path scored the post-repair snapshot and `preserved-boot.log` shows that artifact STARTING and printing its menu. Both scores are `boot-failed` for an unrelated reason.
  - **The run is not a packaging story: the loop built a COMMAND-LINE program for a target whose yardstick serves HTTP.** `start_mode=NEW_PROJECT`, 24 cycles, 3 merges, three files total (`LibraryApplication` as an interactive CLI, `Book`, `BookRepository`), **zero controllers**, its own criteria check reading `0 passed` of 4 against a `BookController` that does not exist. The archive was runnable and the PROGRAM was the wrong shape. One run is not a claim; nothing filed.
- **Watcher discipline held and earned nothing new.** Poll-based on the launch log rather than a `tail | awk` pipeline, so the mawk and self-match families could not apply; predicate proven both ways before arming (the present case had to be SYNTHESISED — no prior launch log existed — rather than assumed), and a companion JVM-liveness signal caught the halt 60 seconds before the terminal line appeared.
- **🔴 THE USER'S TWO QUESTIONS RELOCATED THE DIAGNOSIS TWICE, AND BOTH TIMES I HAD BUILT A STORY THE DATA DID NOT SUPPORT.** Asked *"why does a rewind take place after a repair in the first place?"* — it does not, causally. Both are downstream of the same idle state: the loop stopped producing compilable mutations, the brake counted 20 cycles without a merge, and the self-check only got through because a cycle produced NO mutation at all (the abstention fires on a cached `COMPILE_ERROR`; an ABSENT result still attempts). **The repair arrived at the first moment it was allowed to, which was also the run's last.** My preserve-selector fix was treating a symptom two steps removed and was dropped.
- **Asked *"why is there a fixed interval, either?"* — the constant's own docstring names the variable it proxies for** (*"the artifact needs time to change"*), and the loop measures change directly. Two tells were already in the file: the 8->4 retune justified as *"four samples is too few to land in a healthy window"* (sampling and hoping) and a counter that deliberately does not reset on abstention (*"waits for a moment it can actually use"* — reaching for a readiness trigger and patching the counter instead). **Filed `T2-FK`.**
- **🔴 AND THE USER'S THIRD QUESTION — *"is it ok to compare all of the 92 runs?"* — CAUGHT ME POOLING ACROSS TARGETS.** clitarget and flightsim entered mid-window with different look rates (0.711 vs 0.398/cycle), so my pooled figures were a blend. Firestarter-only the effect is STRONGER (0.105 vs 0.458, **4.4x**). Checked four axes: targets bit, the T4-DQ rewind partly bit (it captures the very counter being measured), the model swap landed 27 minutes before the earliest run (luck), BootGate changed on the 3 newest. Recorded in [[feedback-dont-evaluate-a-build-with-older-runs]] — **the TARGET must be constant too, and that is the harder axis because a target changes what a measurement MEANS.** Second time in one session the same multi-target blind spot cost me.
- **KEPT PULLING AND THE CONFOUND WENT AGAINST MY OWN TICKET, one hour after filing it.** Never-booted runs failed **75%** of compile probes against **36%** for runs that saw it boot; the cached verdict is re-measured every 1.7 cycles (not stale); a tail-blindness hypothesis was REFUTED (12 of 17 probe to within 0-4% of their end — I had generalised from the one run I started with); and **49 boot attempts across the 17 runs were ALL BLOCKED**. So more looks would mostly have returned BLOCKED again and the trigger redesign must not be justified by this population.
  - **What survives is the clean strand, and it is worth more than the ticket's headline.** On `14-29-16` the boot gate diagnosed *"the application exits at startup (exit 1) — no main manifest attribute"* at **14:33:22, 2m33s in**, and the run repeated that phrase **51 times over 27 minutes** while the deterministic one-file-write repair stayed unreachable. **5 of 17 never-booted runs carry that exact defect** (one 78 times); 4 predate `T4-DO` so only one is evidence of suppression. `T2-FK` re-scoped in place to say so.
- **Not committed** — working tree carries the changes; no authorization given ([[feedback-no-auto-commit]]).

## 2026-09-21 later (Opus 5) — T4-DO CLOSED `[x]`: THE ROUTING FIX HAD ITSELF BEEN MIS-ROUTED FOR THREE WEEKS

- **THE ONE THING TO CARRY: a code comment asserting that a call site is REACHABLE is a claim about the ENCLOSING METHOD, and it is checkable in one command.** `T4-DO`'s August entry diagnosed the reachability defect correctly, wrote the correct reason into a comment, and then satisfied it with a second call **inside the same unreachable method** — both `applyRunnablePackagingRepair` sites sit in `autoCompleteRootIfSatisfied` (`GoalPlannerService:3422-3961`, brace-matched). Its comment read *"many cycles before any closure attempt"* while sitting at the closure edge. **The ticket carried `[x]` on that for three weeks**, and nothing re-read the placement because the comment asserting reachability sat beside the call that lacked it. First member of [[project-capability-outruns-routing]] where the ROUTING FIX was itself mis-routed.
- **T4-DO CLOSED `[x]`, field-proven** — run `2026-09-21_13-26-31` (clitarget, **GOAL_FULFILLED in 10.3 min**, 10 cycles, 5 merges, **19/19 final and preserved**, 0 `ERROR c.w.a.` lines). Repair fires **13:29:37** -> package gate builds **13:29:40** -> boot gate `probed=3 skipped=0`, `result=OK` **13:29:41**. **Refused to observed in 65 seconds, with `closure_attempts=0` when it fired.** Before/after on one seed: entry point at **17m12s** (model found it unprompted), **never in 30 min**, then **2m33s** deterministic.
- **🔴 THE USER'S QUESTION MOVED THE FIX, AND MY FIRST PLACEMENT WAS WRONG FOR A REASON I HAD NOT DERIVED.** Asked *"why are we repairing the next package instead of the current one"*, I found `BehaviouralSelfCheck` packages and THEN boots, every 4 cycles — so a pom written at the boot gate lands **seconds after** a build and waits a whole interval. **I had preserved "write the pom, block the cycle, next package rebuilds" from the closure edge without re-deriving it for a call site with different economics.** Moved to `PackageGate`, immediately before `buildExecutor.execute`: one home, every packaging path, consumed by the build on the next line. **Deliberately before the build and not inside a gate that builds for itself** — `verifyDeclaredEntryPointExists` then checks the repair against the build output, which is [[project-false-completion-2]]'s own guard.
- **🔴 AND MY FIRST FIELD WATCHER WAS BLIND — pointed at `logs/autobuilder.log`, which does not exist.** The live log is under the TARGET dir, `data/clitarget/logs/`. It would have reported `repair=0` for the whole run and I would have had a clean "mechanism never fired" reading that was purely the wrong path. **Caught only because I checked the file before trusting its zero**; the re-armed watcher refuses to arm unless the log's first line is this run's. **Thirteenth watcher variant** ([[project-run-lifecycle-runbook]]) and a second instance of [[feedback-grep-the-behaviour-not-the-name]]'s "a zero over the wrong surface". The `grep -c … || echo 0` two-line-string trap fired again in the same session, harmlessly.
- **A hazard the single call site had hidden:** between writing the pom and the next package the JAR still carries no `Main-Class`, so every caller in that window gets the same non-empty answer — the closure edge never saw it because it returned immediately. On the per-cycle route it would have appended the plugin block **once per firing**, and freshness does not stop it (`artifactOlderThanSources` walks `src/` and never reads `pom.xml`). Field-confirmed fixed: one `<mainClass>`, one plugin block, one firing.
- **Score discipline held:** the baseline band (clitarget 19-check population **10/19-19/19**, 19/19 already six times) and the revert rule were **written down before the result landed**. 19/19 + GOAL_FULFILLED is ONE sample from a variant distribution — **the attributable claim is the mechanism and its timing, traced line by line; the score claim is only "in band, no evidence of harm"**. Three watches flagged "opportunity without firing" all appear identically in run 6, so none is this change's.
- **Not committed** — working tree carries the change; no authorization given ([[feedback-no-auto-commit]]).

## 2026-09-21 (Opus 5) — T1-GD CLOSED `[x]` IN ONE SESSION, FIELD-PROVEN, AND THE GUARD OUTRAN ITS REPAIR

- **THE ONE THING TO CARRY: the defect was filed as a false PASS and the field had been producing false FAILURES the whole time — 36 of them.** Re-reading run `2026-09-20_09-55-09`'s own log rather than the filing: from **09:57:12 to 10:12:19, twelve boot-gate sweeps, every one `probed=3 skipped=0 contradicted=3`** against a jar with no entry point. `<mainClass>` first appears at **10:12:54**; the next sweep at **10:16:03 reports `contradicted=0`** — the same three criteria passing the instant the program could start. **The filing was not wrong, it was the smaller half**, and one guard covers both because it stops any criterion-level verdict.
- **T1-GD BUILT AND CLOSED `[x]`** (suite **3119/0/5**, +3 tests). `BootGate.launchAndObserve`, on the `declared.isEmpty()` branch only, asks `RunnableJarGuarantee.declaresEntryPoint` before `probeCommandCriteria` and returns `ClaimRefutation.unattributed`. **The first caller that class has gained since T4-DO shipped it** — routing, as the ticket said, not a second check. `PackageGate.verifyArchiveLaunchable` repointed at the same method, so the question has one home.
  - **Refuted rather than abstained-from, deliberately:** the criterion PROBE abstains, which is what the DoD asks, but an empty gate return means *"nothing contradicted the claim"* and the closure would proceed on the unearned pass. **Known-negative checked: 2 of 3 new tests fail with the guard reverted, and the failing assertion is `isPresent()`** — the gate returns EMPTY, the defect reproduced rather than described. The third passes both ways BY DESIGN: it is the over-reach guard.
- **✅ FIELD CLAUSE MET the same night** — run `2026-09-21_00-32-59` (clitarget, 30 min, 30 cycles, 3 merges, deadline, PASS). `T1-GD:` fired **5 times, first at 00:35:27**, two minutes in. Chain in one millisecond: abstention -> boot gate BLOCKED -> `T4-CU … (criteria named: 0)` -> refusing to close -> not promoting. **`exit_status_probe_runs=0` for the whole run against `18` on run 6, same target and seed.** Not a stall: 4 distinct subgoals of 15 worked, a 17-minute gap mid-run, `boot-gate=1361ms/5`, and **`T4-CC` fired 0 times** so no merge was ever held back by it.
- **🔴 THE GUARD AS FIRST SHIPPED REFUTED A LIBRARY, AND THE USER'S QUESTION FOUND IT, NOT ME.** Asked why I was hesitating to commit and whether *"fixing an exit state isn't the only option"*, I tested the case I had never tested: **a library declares no entry point and owes none.** The guard refuted it and told a correct artifact to "declare the entry point the sources provide" — **`T4-DD` item 3's own defect rebuilt one level up**, demanding a completion model the specification never asked for. **I had wired half of `T4-DO`**: `repairedPom` asks the archive AND the sources (*"A library, or several candidates. Neither is this check's business"*), and I used only the archive half. **The rule was written four lines above my insertion point** — *"This gate holds no expectation to compare such a status against, so it has no opinion."* Fixed via `RunnableJarGuarantee.sourcesNameSoleEntryPoint`, known-negative checked on the new half alone (remove it, exactly the two library tests fail). Suite 3121/0/5.
  - **THE LESSON, which is not "test more": I proved the mechanism FIRED and never asked who else it fires ON.** Every check I ran — 3 tests, a known-negative, a field run, a both-ways watch — interrogated the positive case. A guard is defined as much by what it declines to judge, and nothing in my evidence touched that side.
- **🔴 AND THE CORRECTION WAS ITSELF INCOMPLETE — the user asked "what about `t1gd-unlaunchable-abstention`" and that found the rest.** Declining to DEMAND an entry point is not declining to JUDGE: a library fell through and was probed against an archive that never ran, so the defect was narrowed into the carve-out rather than closed. **And it fell through silently**, so the watch read `OPPORTUNITY-NO-FIRE` — which its own check text defines as "the artifact was launchable". **I had written the T4-CU silence lesson into the runbook earlier in this same session and then rebuilt it.** Fix: the probe abstains in both cases, only the demand is conditional, and the declination logs its own line. Suite 3122/0/5.
  - **THE PATTERN ACROSS BOTH CORRECTIONS: I kept verifying the case the mechanism was built for.** Firing, known-negative, field run, both-ways watch — every instrument aimed at the positive. Both defects lived in what the guard declined to touch, and both were found by a question rather than by my own checks. **Ask a new guard who it must NOT judge, and require that case to log.**
  - **A known-negative can also be wrong, and this one was.** Deleting the `return` let control fall into the refute branch, so three tests failed for a reason unrelated to the path under test. The honest form was HEAD's own file against the current tests: exactly one failure.
- **⚠ AND A FIXTURE THAT PASSED FOR THE WRONG REASON, caught only by an intermittent failure.** The helper wrote the archive BEFORE the sources, so the artifact predated them and the gate abstained on FRESHNESS rather than on entry points; the two-entry-point test was green on that path the whole time. Visible only when two writes landed in the same millisecond. **Sources first, archive last, as a real build does it.** [[feedback-verify-diff-is-substantive]] applies to fixtures, not just diffs.
- **🔴 THE GUARD REFUSES AND NOTHING REPAIRS — the residual, noted on `T4-DO` rather than filed.** `mainClass` was never written in 30 minutes and **`T4-DO` fired 0 times**: both `applyRunnablePackagingRepair` call sites live inside `autoCompleteRootIfSatisfied` and `closure_attempts=0`. **`T4-DO`'s own body predicted exactly this** — *"a run whose artifact cannot run is precisely the run that stalls before closure"* — so this promotes a prediction to an observation. On run 6 the entry point arrived because the MODEL found it unprompted, not because a gate asked. The guard is safe either way (unearned verdict -> refusal, never a pass), but the deterministic answer still is not routed to the edge that needs it.
- **⚠ THE SMOKE SCORES DO NOT COMPARE AND I NEARLY REPORTED THEM AS IF THEY DID.** 2/19 against run 6's 19/19 — but run 6 spanned **45m23s** and found its entry point at **17m12s**, while I capped this one at **30 minutes** because I sized it to catch the abstention, not to produce a comparable artifact. Before saying anything about it I checked the one mechanism by which the guard could have suppressed evolution (`T4-CC` decisive-gate re-run) and it was **zero in both runs**.
- **Field watch `t1gd-unlaunchable-abstention` added and verified BOTH ways: `FIRED(5)` on this run, `OPPORTUNITY-NO-FIRE(18)` on run 6.** Pointed at the gate's OWN line, because the refutation prose travels into the injected work item and re-matches on every echo — the defect `t4cu-criterion-contradicted` carried twice.
- **TWELFTH WATCHER VARIANT, recorded in [[project-run-lifecycle-runbook]]: a counter-delta monitor armed 90 seconds before the JVM rotated the log, so it BASELINED on run 6 (`prev_probe=18`) and was inert for the new run.** Not self-matching, not mawk — a race between arming and rotation. The absent-and-present check passed because it exercised the PREDICATE, never the BASELINE. Fix: pin the file and refuse to arm unless its first line is this run's.
- **And two guessed spellings caught by the same reflex:** my merge and cycle greps both returned 0 on a live, cycling run. The real markers are `post-merge build result=` and `CycleExecutor - T6-J: session`, confirmed by reproducing run 6's recorded `merges=16` / 47 cycles before trusting them ([[feedback-grep-the-behaviour-not-the-name]]).

## 2026-09-20 later (Opus 5) — T4-CU CLOSED `[x]`: THE FIELD DoD IS MET, AND THE ROUTE WAS A TARGET CHANGE

- **THE ONE THING TO CARRY: the mechanism was never broken and the artifact was never the problem — I kept pointing it at the one target where its own guard bites.** `BootGate`'s command branch attributes UNCONDITIONALLY (a non-serving target has no endpoints to suppress on); the serving branch withholds whenever an endpoint fails. Corpus: **firestarter 132 refusals / 0 command-shape; clitarget 57 / all 57.** Four firestarter runs never reached the clause for that reason alone. Fourth routing answer of the session.
- **✅ T4-CU's FIELD DoD MET and the ticket CLOSED `[x]`** (run `2026-09-20_09-55-09`, clitarget, deadline, **smoke 19/19**, 47 cycles / 16 merges). Chain: evaluator claim → execution refutation 09:57:12 (attributed, narrow drop `3 of 4`, repair item naming 3 criteria, current work same cycle) → loop works it (13 refusals / 12 merges, ONE item, 11 twins refused) → model writes the message code 10:00:11 → **10:12:58 it finds the REAL blocker itself, a missing `Main-Class` nothing had told it about** → runnable jar → `closure claim not contradicted` 10:16:03 → COMPLETED. **Confirmed by RUNNING the artifact, not by trusting the probe:** exit 1 + `no such file: <path>` on stderr.
- **🔴 A DEFECT MY OWN CHANGE INTRODUCED, caught in the field and fixed the same hour.** A refused repair item CLONED itself — 3 identical items in 100 seconds in run 5 — because the item's description embedded the probe's temp fixture path, so every injection was textually unique and dedup never matched. Fixed two ways: description built from the CRITERIA (stable, and already the done-when), and a criteria-gate item no longer injects at all. Run 6: **1 item, 11 twins refused**. Suite 3116/0/5.
- **⚠ AND THE TEST FOR IT PINNED NOTHING UNTIL THE KNOWN-NEGATIVE CAUGHT IT.** On the first revert only ONE of two new tests failed: the self-cloning test used a CONSTANT refutation text, so dedup rescued the tree and it passed with the guard removed. Varying the text isolates the guard from the dedup. **A test that passes with the mechanism reverted is worth nothing.**
- **🔴 RESIDUAL FOR `T4-DO`/`PackageGate`, filed as a note not a ticket: an unlaunchable jar exits 1.** For ~19 minutes the probe exercised the JVM launcher's failure, not the program, and **a criterion asserting ONLY an exit status would have passed against code that never ran a line** — the message assertion is the sole reason it was caught. Sharpens `T4-DT` from *"a status cannot see a wrong answer"* to *"a status cannot distinguish a working program from one that never started."* The invariant — *when the artifact never executed the code under test, no criterion's failure is its own* — already has two partial homes (the not-packageable abstention, the endpoint withholding) and a gap on the non-serving path.
- **FILED `T1-GD` (open): an artifact that never launches exits 1, and "exits 1" is a criterion specs routinely state — so a STATUS-ONLY criterion can PASS against code that ran not one line.** Measured for 19 minutes in run `2026-09-20_09-55-09`; the exit status MATCHED and only the message assertion caught it. **Routing, not a missing check:** `T4-DO` already built `RunnableJarGuarantee` and it has exactly ONE caller (the closure edge's boot-failure branch) while `BootGate`/`BehaviouralClosureVerifier`/`PackageGate` reference it zero times — and those three are the criterion-probe path. Fix must not name an ecosystem's launcher text.
- **Narration question answered (user asked):** goals and criteria are **NOT** truncated in storage — root descriptions run 2,115 chars, criteria complete at 89/96/66/42. Two caps exist and both are on the SPEECH path: `graph.html cropForSpeech` ~240 chars (boundary-aware) and `TtsController.MAX_CHARS` 600 (blunt `substring`). The spoken text is the templated prefix, so the criteria — the part worth hearing — fall off the end. That is `T5-BI`/`T5-BO`, and the fix is speaking the criteria rather than a bigger cap.

## 2026-09-19/20 (Opus 5) — T4-CU FIRED IN THE FIELD, T1-GC FILED AND FIXED, AND I CORRECTED MYSELF TWICE

- **THE ONE THING TO CARRY: a command returning nothing is a SPELLING of absence, not a proof of it — four instances in one session.** `docker ps` listing nothing became "the daemon crashed and took the container with it" (`docker inspect` says `RestartCount: 0`, `OOMKilled: false` — the mechanism was never evidenced, and the user corrected me); `grep -c … || echo 0` assigned the two-line string `"0\n0"` and broke a verification predicate — **the exact trap this runbook documents**; a `grep` for "prompt diff" read as missing when the file says "diffing the first rendered prompt"; and a `${p%%:*}` split on a colon INSIDE my own pattern reported 3 attributed refusals when all 3 read `criteria named: 0`. **Every one was caught only by a contradiction with evidence already in hand.**
- **T4-CU FIRED IN THE FIELD, first time on this build** (`2026-09-19_23-56-29`, firestarter, deadline, **smoke 23/23**, 29 cycles / 9 merges, clean box). Verifier refused on its own line -> closure blocked -> bank cleared -> `closure_refused` event (`gate=T4-CU`) -> `closure_refusal_report.py` read it back from the field log. **NOT a livelock: every refusal followed by a merge within seconds** (the spin T2-FB broke had 78 early exits and zero merges). **Repair clause still unfired** — all 3 refusals were ENDPOINT-level, which the design deliberately leaves unattributed; **164 of 171 archived refusals (96%) are criterion-only and attributable**, so this run drew the 4% three times.
- **🔴 I ALSO REFUTED MY OWN 2026-09-17 CLAIM that T4-CU's precondition is "rare by construction".** Across 88 runs / 685 completed subgoals: **313 (45.7%) carry a SPEC-stated criterion** (67 of 87 runs, median 3/run), the verifier is **reached in 82 of 88 runs** and **refutes in 24**, 171 refusals total. I had generalised from ONE run's six closures. Waiting is not futile; those 171 refusals never produced the DoD's second half only because, before this session, **a refusal injected nothing**.
- **T1-GC filed and fixed the same day: the loop spun 29,500 aborted cycles in 230s (128/sec, 231 MB of log) when the graph went unreachable.** Mechanism established rather than assumed — both log lines appear exactly 29,500 times, so it is one abort per SCHEDULER TICK, not a retry inside one call. The `fast` profile's `inter-cycle-delay-ms: 1` is safe only while cycles are slow; a cycle that aborts in microseconds voids that premise. **Invariant: a cycle that FAILS must not retry faster than one that SUCCEEDS.** Doubling backoff, floor 1s and cap 60s (the project's own cadences), skipping BEFORE the lock so the offending code is not re-entered and its stack does not re-log. `LlmCircuitBreaker` was this protection for the model; the graph had none. **NOT field-verified — `backoff-fired 0` on run 3, no outage occurred.**
- **T4-CU shipped in full this session:** port widened (`ClaimRefutation`), all TEN closure gates countable (census corrected from eleven — `T4-BN-3` is a quarantine visit counter, not a refusal), repair clause proven as a SEQUENCE by `ClosureRefutationRepairScenarioTest` (known-negative checked: 4 of 6 fail with the mechanism reverted), and **residual ① closed** — the model fallback was designed on and wired off for seven weeks (zero production callers passed `true`), now `autobuilder.goal.closure-probe-model-fallback: true` behind a real off-switch.
- **🔴 THE TICKET'S OWN WATCH WAS LYING and is fixed.** `t4cu-criterion-contradicted` reported **FIRED(10)** on a run where the gate ran ZERO times: it matched `BootGate`'s caller-agnostic summary phrase, which `BehaviouralSelfCheck` also emits and which then travels into `T4-BV`'s injected work item and re-matches on every echo. Repointed at the verifier's own refusal line, verified BOTH ways (FIRED(7) on a known-positive run, NO-OPPORTUNITY on the misreporting one). **Second defect found in that one watch.**
- **Open, not diagnosed, and not T4-CU's:** preserve shipped **9/23** while the run's FINAL artifact scored **23/23** (`preserved_source=loop-preserved`; strict 4/23 vs 23/23 discounted=none). Compared as failing SETS: final empty, preserved 14 members. `peaked_at_cycle=7/29` disagrees with the final being best. Belongs to `T3-BY`/`T4-CA`. Also: **`closure_attempts=0` on that 23/23 artifact** — [[project-first-perfect-smoke-score]] recurring.
- **Operational:** `MEMORY.md` had been over its size limit and **three rules were silently not loading** (`feedback_no_invented_labels`, `feedback_no_co_mention_pairing`, `feedback_tts_marytts`) — trimmed the three longest index entries back toward the ~200-char contract, detail verified present in each target file first. Run 2 (`2026-09-19_00-42-58`) is labelled in the ledger as an ENVIRONMENT COLLAPSE, not a loop outcome; do not count it in terminal-rate statistics.

## 2026-09-17 (Opus 5) — T4-CU'S PORT WIDENED AND SHIPPED, AND ITS OWN WATCH WAS LYING

- **THE ONE THING TO CARRY: an instrument that names a ticket is not measuring that ticket.** `t4cu-criterion-contradicted` reported **FIRED(10)** on a run where T4-CU's verifier ran **zero** times. Its pattern was `BootGate`'s summary phrase, which **every caller of the shared criterion probe emits** (here `BehaviouralSelfCheck`/T4-CY, whose mismatches go to the evolution prompt) and which then **travels into the work item `T4-BV` injects**, so every echo of that subgoal re-matched. Ten lines, five cycles, two real gate events, neither this gate's. Repointed at the verifier's own refusal line and verified BOTH ways: FIRED(7) on `2026-08-22_06-19-55` (exactly its seven refusals) and NO-OPPORTUNITY on the run that misreported. **Second defect found in this one watch** — `watch_report.py`'s own comment records the first. And the shared probe's line is prefixed `T4-CU:` whatever the caller, so it never was evidence this gate ran.
- **T2-FH CLOSED `[n]` DECLINED on measured evidence, and the oracle question was tested rather than asserted.** The proposed own-record brake rule is 3-6x more harmful than the constant it would replace (`scripts/drought_replay.py`, 19 tests, 67 replayable runs); a PERFECT brake saves only 314 of 2,523 cycles (12.4%), mostly in runs that were failing anyway. **Combining heuristic values, merges, file counts and completions does NOT approximate the oracle**: 5-fold held out BY RUN, the full combination reaches AUC 0.623 against elapsed-cycles-alone at **0.688**, the heuristic family alone is 0.487 (chance), and as a halt rule every threshold that fires kills runs while every harmless one never fires. Structural reason: **nothing in the loop is comparative across cycles** (T4-DS's gap). Re-entry: a comparative in-loop signal exists.
- **T4-CU port widened in one scope (suite 3101/0/5).** `ClaimRefutation(description, refutedCriteria)`; the flattening was one level DEEPER than the port — `BootGate` joined a list whose entries already embedded their criterion. Repair item injects with the refuted criteria as its done-when, `T2-FB` drops only the refuted entries, and `closure_refused` now fires from **all ten** closure gates (as first shipped it wired one of ten and the claim was corrected). Census corrected too: **ten** gates, not eleven — `T4-BN-3` reads like a refusal in its own message but is a quarantine visit counter.
- **🔴 THE FIELD DoD GOT NO OPPORTUNITY, AND THE REASON IS STRUCTURAL RATHER THAN BAD LUCK.** Run `2026-09-17_23-01-39`: verifier wired, own lines fired 0 times across **6 closures** — 4 declared no criteria, and **both criteria-bearing closures were 100% loop-AUTHORED**, so `T3-CC` filtered them before the verifier was called. **`T3-CC` narrows this gate's reachability and is right to** (asserting an authored criterion accuses a correct artifact). So the field clause needs a closing subgoal with a **specification-stated** criterion — **not more run length**, which is how it has been planned until now.
- **Third silence promoted DEBUG->INFO.** A closure declaring no criteria exited invisibly; it is the MAJORITY case (4 of 6), and the bean-absent case had already got a presence line for exactly this reason. That ambiguity is what made the ticket's own *"5 runs, never executed"* unreadable.
- **Readouts shipped:** `scripts/closure_refusal_report.py` (9 tests, absence reported as absence, known-positive asserted first) and `scripts/drought_replay.py` (19 tests). **Eleventh watcher variant recorded:** the app log's mtime answers *alive*, the behaviour log's only *progressing* — it writes at CYCLE boundaries, so it sat 10 minutes stale at load 175 on a healthy run.
- **Sample quality, stated so the run is not over-read:** load average **175** against a qemu VM, Docker Desktop and Firefox; one suppressed `ServiceUnavailableException`; smoke 11/23, preserved boot-failed. The no-opportunity finding stands only because it was traced per closure in the goal tree rather than inferred from the score.
- **Open and NOT decided by me:** residual ① — probe synthesis has never met the local model because **zero production callers enable it** (`BootGate` reserves the model fallback for the completion edge; the completion edge passes `false`). One boolean, real cost (29.5 and 14.9 minutes measured by T4-CY), left to the user.

## 2026-09-16 (Opus 5) — TWO FIELD RUNS, BOTH PASS, AND THE CLI TARGET STILL WORKS

- **Both targets ran clean on the session's build.** `firestarter` (25 min cap, 11 cycles, 3 merges, `terminal=deadline`, smoke **23/23** final and preserved, `preserved_source=reconstructed:cycle-1789507940640`, run dir `/tmp/preflight_runs/2026-09-15_23-07-04`) and `clitarget` (25 min cap, 23 cycles, 6 merges, `terminal=deadline`, smoke **10/19** final against **17/19** preserved, `preserved_source=loop-preserved`, failing checks 6 and 7, run dir `/tmp/preflight_runs/2026-09-15_23-37-21`). **Zero `ERROR c.w.a.` lines in either run.**
  - **The CLI target's final-versus-preserved gap is the known pattern, not a new fault:** the loop held a 17/19 artifact and ended on a 10/19 one, with the in-process preserve hook firing correctly ([[project-first-perfect-smoke-score]]).
  - **CORRECTION, from the summary rather than my assumption: firestarter's 23/23 IS this run's.** `start_mode=NEW_PROJECT` with `start_tree=LOOP_AUTHORED` means it cleared `output/` and built the artifact itself in 25 minutes. I had reported it as "a resumed tree [that] inherited most of it" on the strength of a small early file count and a low cycle number, without reading the two summary fields that say otherwise ([[feedback-grep-the-behaviour-not-the-name]]: the field was there and I inferred instead).
  - **Both terminals are `deadline` because I gave them 25 minutes against preflight's 90-minute default, and the two runs mean opposite things by it.** firestarter `peaked_at_cycle=11/11` with `drift_tail_min=0.0` — it peaked on its FINAL cycle and was cut off while still improving; more time was the binding constraint. clitarget `peaked_at_cycle=7/23` with `drift_tail_min=17.8` — it found its best answer eight minutes in and spent the next eighteen getting worse, so more time buys more drift, not a better artifact. **One label, two situations; read `peaked_at_cycle` and `drift_tail_min` before treating `deadline` as "needed longer".**
  - **`deadline` is the normal terminal here, not a fault:** 66 of 136 recorded runs (48%), against `GOAL_FULFILLED` 35 (25%) and root-convergence stall 14 (10%).
  - **firestarter closed 23/23 with `closure_attempts=0`** — a flawless artifact it never tried to close on, which is [[project-first-perfect-smoke-score]] recurring rather than a new finding.
- **T1-GB's deletion path is FIELD-EXERCISED, which is the one piece of this session's code the runs could reach.** Four probe lines across the two runs stage deletions, including `T1-EC: probe result=COMPILE_OK (0 files written, 1 deleted)` — **a delete-only mutation being probed**, which before the fix returned "no changed files in evolution outcome" and was never probed at all. The merge-time half (`merge: deleted N path(s)`) did not fire: no accepted mutation carried a deletion.
- **T2-ES markers: zero in both runs**, as expected. No refused `#NoDiff` promotion, no escalation. Consistent with the extinction measurement.
- **A latent inefficiency I had added, found by the run and removed.** One `ConnectionReadTimeoutException` hit `/autobuilder/checkpoints` reading a checkpoint from the graph. **The message class predates today** (95 occurrences in the 2026-09-14 log), so it is not a regression — **but `hasCarriedContext`, which I added to the listing yesterday, called `archivePathOf`, whose fallback is a graph query per row.** On a listing that walks every restorable point that was several hundred Neo4j round trips per request. Now disk-only; the restore path keeps the full lookup, where it is one query for the one point. The listing understates rather than blocks. Suite 3091/0/5.
  - **Worth carrying:** a per-row helper on a listing endpoint is a place to check for an N+1 before shipping it. The unit tests could not see this and neither could the suite.

## 2026-09-15 later (Opus 5) — T2-ES CLOSED `[s]` SUBSUMED INTO T4-CU, AFTER I NEARLY RETIRED A LIVE PROBLEM ON A DEAD SYMPTOM

- **THE ONE THING TO CARRY: a symptom disappearing because the diagnosis moved UPSTREAM is not a premise dissolving.** I closed this `[o]` obsoleted on the measurement that refused `#NoDiff` promotions occur zero times in 967 cycles. **The phenomenon itself occurs in 36 runs** — `"the application runs, but spec-named endpoint(s) fail at runtime: GET /loans answers 404 — the mapping is not reachable in the running application"` is this ticket's case in its own words. I measured a SPELLING and claimed a BEHAVIOUR, the third instance of that error class in one session. **Corrected to `[s]`: `T4-CU` owns the capability** — `BootGate` boots the artifact and exercises the criteria against it, so the loop OBSERVES the existence/behaviour gap instead of inferring it from a completion claim. That is why the `#NoDiff` route withered: the loop stopped waiting to be told and went and looked.
- **AND THE TICKET I SUBSUMED INTO WAS ITSELF MIS-MARKED: `T4-CU` was `[s]` subsumed into NOTHING.** 61 lines of body name no target, no other ticket claims the capability, and siblings quote its mechanism from run logs as live. Its own body says the original DoD is **"STILL NOT met"** while the refutal half is **"MET"** — which is `[~]`, not `[s]`. Corrected. **The one line mentioning the marker was declining an upgrade to `[x]`, not affirming subsumption**, and a wrong KIND survived because nobody re-read it. **`[s]` told every reader that a live, load-bearing mechanism with an outstanding field DoD was somebody else's problem** — and `BootGate`'s criterion execution is what now produces the existence-versus-behaviour diagnosis in 36 runs. **Check the ticket you point AT before you point at it.**
- **What T2-ES built REMAINS and is complementary, which is what rescues the work.** `BootGate` sees only what a running artifact reveals; a parallel store compiles, boots and answers requests while silently losing writes. `StoreCoherenceHeuristic` plus the structural-defect escalation catch that at evaluation time, before merge — and that half fired in the field on 2026-09-14. **Observable-at-runtime is T4-CU's half; compiles-and-boots-but-wrong is this one's.**
- **(superseded framing, kept for the reasoning) a capability can be built correctly for a premise that appears to dissolve while you build it.** T2-ES shipped both triggers and the structural one fired in the field. The originating one — the model reporting work done while a criterion stays unmet — has occurred **0 times in 967 cycles across 48 runs**, against 27 times in one July run. `EVOLUTION_NOOP` fires 5 times in that whole corpus and the refusal never follows, so when the model claims completion the banked criteria now agree. Other work closed it: `T4-BP` verification, `T2-CC`, `T1-DN`, `T1-EE`. **Closed `[o]`, not `[x]`** — built is not the same as verified, and the originating half cannot be verified because the phenomenon is gone.
- **Clause-by-clause beats a tick.** No domain vocabulary (a test over twelve banned tokens). Escalates in single digits as mechanism, as written ("demonstrated on a replay of this run's shape" — no such run is archived, five staging routes failed). Root brake stops ending such runs — unassessable, such runs do not happen. **Two clauses retired as obsolete rather than satisfied.**
- **AND THE SESSION'S SHARPEST LESSON WAS A RETRACTION OF MY OWN.** I explained the extinction by claiming the `#NoDiff` permission block was structurally unreachable on spec-shaped work, because key terms are camelCase/PascalCase only. **It also extracts HTTP PATHS**, so `/books` matches `@RequestMapping("/books")`; three of four real criteria match, and the block had rendered 4 times in a run I had already measured. I reasoned about regexes instead of running them and built a causal story on one run's zero. [[feedback-grep-the-behaviour-not-the-name]], and it cost a committed claim that had to be retracted the same day.
- **Watch item carried past closure, owned by nobody until it fires:** an injected work item has never changed a next proposal in the field, for EITHER trigger. Markers `structural_defect_escalated` and `nodiff_disagreement_escalated`, read the `outcome` field. Re-entry if either fires and the next proposal re-sends the rejected shape — the falsifier stated in advance. Deletion candidate if 1000 more cycles pass silent.
- **Method note worth keeping: five staging routes failed and each named a real property.** Frozen tree cannot express a sequence; a rewind gives a fresh roll; an injected subgoal is pruned because `setAsCurrent` requests the replan that kills it; an injected `EVOLUTION_NOOP` is wiped by `T1-EE`'s stale-signal guard. **When the fifth instrument fails, the phenomenon is the finding.**

## 2026-09-15 (Opus 5) — THE TICKET'S ORIGINATING CASE WAS NEVER BUILT, AND NOW IS

- **THE ONE THING TO CARRY: a ticket can be advanced, field-proven and still not be the ticket.** `T2-ES` was filed from a run that lost 27 of 110 cycles to one subgoal emitting the identical pair of statements — evolution saying the work is already implemented, the promotion gate saying a criterion is unmet. What shipped on 09-13 and fired in the field on 09-14 escalates on a repeated CRITICAL heuristic finding with a structural subject. **The two triggers do not overlap**: a `#NoDiff` cycle proposes no mutation, so it produces no heuristic opinion and no streak. I wrote a DoD line calling the clause field-proven without noticing the clause names a different trigger. **Read the clause against what shipped before calling it met.**
- **BUILT 2026-09-15 (suite 3085/0/5).** `noDiffRefusalStreak` on the cycle context counts consecutive refused `#NoDiff` promotions per subgoal, with the same per-subgoal lifecycle the heuristic streaks use; at 3 (`autobuilder.tuning.escalation.nodiff-disagreement-cycles`) `BlockerSubgoalInjector.injectNoDiffDisagreementRedirection` raises a work item whose every word is derived — the planner's criterion text, the observed count, and `WiringFactsAnalyzer.peerAnnotationAnomalies` computed against the project's own peers. Stamped `GATE_NODIFF_DISAGREEMENT` because the defect compiles, the third door onto `T2-EQ`'s cause B. Refuses to inject with no unmet criterion on record, because an item saying only "investigate" is re-asking through a costlier channel. Event `nodiff_disagreement_escalated`, carrying `injected` or `skipped: <why>`.
  - **The user's 2026-07-27 constraint is asserted as a test, not honoured by intention:** given inputs with no ecosystem or domain words, the generated item contains none. Twelve banned tokens checked.
  - **The wiring is NOT covered.** The call sits inside the existing `refusalReason != null` branch and no test drives that branch; reaching it needs the whole tick. The branch is field-proven by the ticket's own 27 logged refusals and the call adds no condition of its own, which is an argument, not a test.
- **FIELD VERIFICATION ATTEMPTED AND THE PRECONDITION IS EXTINCT (2026-09-15).** Across 48 behaviour logs and 967 cycles there are **ZERO refused `#NoDiff` promotions with a real subgoal id**, and `EVOLUTION_NOOP` appears in 5 events total. The originating run lost 27 cycles to this in July. **So this half's field clause cannot be met by waiting.** Two runs tried; run 2 forced the threshold to 1 and staged a subgoal whose key terms are all present but whose claim is false, which is a faithful reproduction because the loop grants its `#NoDiff` permission on shallow TERM matching while the verifier judges MEANING. **The planner pruned the injected subgoal as "superseded by replanning" before evolution reached it** — operator-injected subgoals have no protection, gate-raised ones do. Remaining lever: inject with a gate stamp or pin it. Decide deliberately; the other half of this ticket already recorded "stop trying to stage it".
  - **My first base-rate measurement was wrong in the OTHER direction:** the log directory also holds TEST output, so grepping it returned fixture subgoals (`criteria-sub`, `bf-sub`) in multiples of three and looked like a healthy rate. Filter to `subGoalId=goal-`, and validate the predicate against a known positive before trusting a zero.
  - **TWO MORE STAGING ROUTES TRIED AND BLOCKED, both by the system working correctly.** Operator-injected subgoal: `setAsCurrent:true` emits `REPLAN_REQUESTED` and the planner pruned it — only `isFromGate` metadata survives a replan and no REST surface sets it; `pin` emits the same replan. Operator-injected `EVOLUTION_NOOP`: accepted by the signal endpoint, then consumed mid-cycle by `GoalPlannerService:1343`, the `T1-EE` guard that stops a stale noop outliving its subgoal.
  - **AND I THEN GOT THE EXPLANATION WRONG AND RETRACTED IT THE SAME DAY.** I claimed the `#NoDiff` permission block was structurally unreachable because key terms are camelCase/PascalCase only. **It also extracts HTTP PATHS**, so `/books` matches `@RequestMapping("/books")` — measured against the real criteria and the real tree, three of four match. The block had also rendered 4 times in an earlier run I had already measured. **I reasoned about the regexes instead of running them, and built a causal story on a single run's zero.** The honest account is narrower: the model claims completion occasionally, the refusal never happens, so the two judgements now concur. That is the loop working, not a defect.
  - **STANDING QUESTION: this half is insurance against a condition that has not occurred in 967 cycles.** The premise dates from July and other work (`T4-BP`, `T2-CC`, `T1-DN`, `T1-EE`) appears to have closed it. Deletion candidate if another 1000 cycles pass silent; not a ticket today.
  - **The attempt earned two real defects, found in PREPARATION not by running:** the banked criteria map is keyed from ONE and the escalation read it from zero (would have quoted the wrong criterion or stayed silent; 3 tests fail against the old indexing), and the counter took `build-failed` refusals too (would have masked a real disagreement). Both fixed. **The preparation was worth more than the run.**
- **BOTH halves now share one gap: the injection actuator has never fired in the field.** On 09-14 its one opportunity was skipped because the planner had already written a `[BUILD_FIX]` naming the same defect. For both triggers, detect-and-decide is proven and "an injected item changes the next proposal" is not. Watch markers: `nodiff_disagreement_escalated` and `structural_defect_escalated`, and read the `outcome` on each.

## 2026-09-14 latest (Fable 5.1) — T2-ES FIELD-PROVEN BY A STAGED REWIND, AND THE DEFECT UNDER IT WAS A DROPPED `#Delete`

- **THE ONE THING TO CARRY: a rewind reproduces a state only if it restores what the next tick READS, and until today no rewind ever did.** The carried cycle context (streaks, persistent warnings, relayed rejection reasons, probe and build observations) is not a holder; `meta.json` is written mid-cycle and cleared at the run boundary; `settleAfterRewind` settled the CURRENT context. So every rewind restarted every streak at zero, and a cut point staged one cycle short of an escalation could never reach it. Fixed: `checkpoints/<cycle>/context.json`, archived as `__context.json`, restored and settled from; absence and supplied-not-captured reported ([[project-state-time-travel-design-note]]).
- **THE EXPERIMENT THE USER ASKED FOR, DONE AS ASKED.** Rewind to the 09-13 defect run's second-firing cut point (`cycle-1789286226879`, streak 2) with its carried context RECONSTRUCTED from that run's logs (its `mutations/` were gone), re-keyed to the new identity, provenance-labelled, verified by a block-by-block prompt diff against the original run's next prompt. **`structural_defect_escalated` fired one cycle after the resume** (2 min 20 s in): heuristic `StoreCoherenceHeuristic`, subject `Book`, streak 3, both paths. Two cycles after the cut the grind was over (merge with a single store). The watch item that had waited two weeks for a natural grind is closed by one staged rewind.
- **AND THE MODEL HAD ALREADY TAKEN A PATH THE LOOP COULD NOT SEE.** That same proposal carried `#Delete [.../BookRepository.java]` — one of the two offered ways out — and was REJECTED for the parallel store it had just removed: the delete directive was parsed, logged, and then dropped by the diff (told nothing), the heuristics (overlay without subtraction), the probe (writes only) and the merge (mutation-directory walk). **`T1-GB`, filed and closed the same session:** `ProjectDiffService.computeDiff(current, previous, deletions)`, `ProjectDiffResult.overlay()` as the one home, probe stages deletions with rollback, merge applies them; five tests fail with the fix reverted. Third member of the "the loop threw the work away" family on this ticket (T3-BW, T4-CY before it). Not field-verified after the fix: the run merged by the other route.
- **THE INJECTOR SKIPPED AND THE EVENT SAID "ESCALATED".** The planner ran first, read the restored warning and interpretation, and replaced the backlog with a `[BUILD_FIX]` subgoal naming the store unification; `hasActiveBuildFixSubgoal()` made the injector skip at DEBUG after the event had gone out. The injector now returns its outcome and the event records `injected` / `skipped: <why>`. Residual, unfiled: whether a planner-written BUILD_FIX that names the defect should count as the escalation (it did here; one instance).
- **THEN THE USER ASKED WHETHER WE NOW GATHER ENOUGH TO REWIND ANYWHERE, AND THE ANSWER EXPOSED ONE MORE HOLE — CLOSED.** Capture is unconditional: tree, context, holders, goal cut point and cache every cycle, with exactly one early return, the automatic rewind-on-grind, which is correct because that cycle's tail describes an abandoned path. Retention is 40 local checkpoints and 2000 archived, contexts included. **But `StateFingerprint` digested four components and the carried context was added as a fifth without extending it**, so a partial or corrupted context would have restored silently. Now a fifth `context` digest, recorded at capture, recompared at restore, compared only when both sides have one. **Rule: a new state component and its instrument ship in the same change.** Round-trip fixed point measured on all 15 contexts before shipping and pinned in the suite; known-negative checked; field-verified both ways, including the first `clean:true` rewind with no failures at all. Suite 3073/0/5.
  - **My own scan for early returns reported a false zero twice**, using `\s` and then `\b`, neither of which mawk supports. Only the known-positive check caught it. [[feedback-grep-the-behaviour-not-the-name]] again, and the lesson is that the guard has to be run against a line you have already seen, not merely written.
- **Found on the way: `RequestChannel` map keys did not round-trip through JSON** (record-keyed map, `toString` out, single-String constructor in); every `meta.json` on disk carries triple-wrapped keys. Only mattered once a context was READ back. `SignalTypeKey` + round-trip test. **And the archive's reserved-prefix rule**: the first supplied context planted a third `__` file into `output/`; strip by prefix now.
- **Cost, honestly:** T4-DQ fixes (context capture/restore, key round-trip, prefix rule) + the reconstruction (a 165 KB context from logs, ~100 fields with provenance) + one 9-minute live run + T1-GB (four homes, six tests). Runs: `2026-09-14_21-34` (firestarter, stopped by hand at 21:43, 3 real cycles after the rewind then the planner-only loop). Full suite green (3056 before T1-GB, then 3069/0/5 with the deletion tests added).
- **Operational:** the evolution prompt of a rewound cycle is 345 lines on this build against 949 on the 09-13 build for the same state — the CURRENT SUBGOAL / REMAINING / ARTIFACT HISTORY blocks are gone and the T4-BX relay renders; compare prompts by carried BLOCK, not by length. `ContextCheck.java`-style validation (deserialize with the app's classes, assert the carried fields) is the cheap pre-flight for any supplied context.

## 2026-09-13 latest (Opus 5) — T2-ES SHIPPED, AND THE MEASUREMENT CORRECTED THE TICKET BEFORE ANY CODE WAS WRITTEN

- **➡ THE ONE THING TO CARRY: A COUNTER THAT COUNTS CORRECTLY AND FEEDS ONLY A PROMPT IS NOT AN ESCALATION MECHANISM.** `T2-ES` was filed saying the loop "cannot see the repeat". It could: `T2-AT`'s `criticalHeuristicStreaks` counted the field run's repeats perfectly, warning *"critical for 2 consecutive cycles"* up to *"critical for 6"*. **What reported `repeat_proposals=0` was a DIFFERENT mechanism** — `T2-EY`'s proposal ledger, keyed on proposal CONTENT, which was right to say the proposals differed. The real gap was one grep away: every reader of the streak (`EvolutionProtocolContract:511`, `PlannerProtocolContract:583`) is a prompt renderer, so the integer's only destination was the warning text. **Before building the capability a ticket says is missing, grep who READS the thing that allegedly cannot see — the counting may be fine and the consumption may be the whole defect.** ([[feedback-grep-the-behaviour-not-the-name]], [[project-capability-outruns-routing]] — assume UNREACHED, not unbuilt.)
- **✅ THE IDENTITY MEASUREMENT, which the design did rest on and which held.** Run `2026-09-13_09-44-03`, the seven `StoreCoherenceHeuristic` rejections: **one subject, three distinct rationale strings** — `BookStore:14` five times, then `:11`, then `:13`, because the model rewrote the file each cycle and kept the defect. Content-keying splits one defect into three; name-keying alone conflates different subjects; **`name + subject` is 1 across all seven**. Taken from the app log, not reasoned about.
- **✅ SHIPPED (suite 3039/0/5).** `HeuristicOpinion.StructuralDefect(subject, resolutionPaths)` — stated by the finding heuristic, because only it knows the shape of its own defect — plus `identityKey()`, falling back to the bare name when there is no subject. The streak keys on that. At **3** consecutive cycles (`autobuilder.tuning.escalation.structural-defect-repeat-cycles`) `BlockerSubgoalInjector.injectStructuralDefectRedirection` injects a divergence work item naming the mutually sufficient ways out, **each path its own acceptance criterion**. Behaviour event `structural_defect_escalated`.
  - **Three, because the warning surface already spends the first two cycles** — no warning on the first firing, the persistent block from the second — so escalating at three gives re-asking exactly one chance before concluding it will not work.
  - **🔴 THE HAZARD WORTH REMEMBERING: this is the first divergence whose defect the COMPILER CANNOT SEE.** `T2-DD` keys on a failed file path, `T2-EF` on a missing symbol; both are build failures. A parallel store compiles perfectly, so without a gate stamp the COMPILE_OK cleanup would have retired the work item on the very next merge — **`T2-EQ`'s Cause B arriving through a new door**. `BlockerTypes.GATE_STRUCTURAL_DEFECT` closes it by construction. Ask of any new work item: what observation is allowed to retire this, and can that observation actually attest to it?
  - **Two producers wired, the third deliberately not.** `StoreCoherenceHeuristic` (one path per declaring type) and `ManufacturedDependencyHeuristic` (one per introduced manufacture, remedies its rationale already carried as trailing advice). **`MergeRegressionHeuristic` stays name-keyed on purpose** — its finding is about the mutation just proposed, not a standing defect in the tree, so there is no choice to offer; the injector's refusal to escalate a finding with no resolution paths gives the right behaviour by construction rather than by a special case.
- **📋 METHOD: the tests were checked against a known-negative.** Reverting the keying to `op.name()` fails two of the four escalation tests. The rewrite-stability test asserts the **PAIR** — the rationale must DIFFER across the two cycles while the identity holds — so it cannot pass vacuously, which is the failure mode `EvaluationAgentRepeatRefusalTest`'s own header warns about.
- **⚠ NOT FIELD-VERIFIED — no run has executed since.** Field DoD and the falsifier, stated in advance: a run where one heuristic reports the same subject three times running emits `structural_defect_escalated`, injects the work item, and **the NEXT proposal implements one of the named paths** rather than re-proposing the rejected shape. Watch marker: `structural_defect_escalated`. **Inconclusive is a real outcome** — a run with no three-peat confirms nothing. **The falsifier that matters: if the model reads the two paths and still re-proposes the same shape, the identity and the actuator are both right and the missing move is elsewhere — do not answer that with another prompt tweak.**
- **🔴 AND THE REWIND EXPERIMENT DID NOT ANSWER IT EITHER — three instruments, still no verdict.** The idea was right and the operator's: restore the defect run by the T4-DQ time machine and continue it on the new build. It took FOUR fixes to make a cross-lineage rewind faithful (see [[project-state-time-travel-design-note]]), and the fourth — restoring the ROOT — turned `clean:false` into `clean:true` and incidentally explained `holder:fixAttempts`, which I had reported twice as an independent unexplained divergence and which was downstream of the broken root all along.
  - **The defect DID reproduce** (`parallel store introduced — entity 'Book' declared in BookRepository:12, BookService:11`) — **but with a different partner class than the original run's `BookStore`, and exactly ONCE.** The loop then moved to the next subgoal, and the streak resets on subgoal change by design, so the escalation could never reach its threshold of 3.
  - **➡ A REWIND REPRODUCES A STATE, NOT A TRAJECTORY.** The design note already predicted the mechanism — rewound-out goals stay in the graph's description corpus that the planner's duplicate check reads, so the plan diverges even from identical restored state. Temperature-0 determinism does NOT survive a rewind. **Do not plan an experiment that needs the ORIGINAL SEQUENCE back; a rewind gives you the starting position and a fresh roll.**

- **📋 Session shape, for honesty about cost:** one capability shipped (T2-ES identity + escalation), four restorability defects found and fixed as a side effect of trying to TEST it, and the thing under test is still unproven in the field. The restorability work is committed and verified; the escalation claim is not, and the actuator is verified only as far as goal state.

The run was HEALTHIER than the one that motivated the ticket (10 merges vs 3), which is exactly why no parallel store appeared. 23/23 on a `deadline` terminal with `closure_attempts=0` is [[project-first-perfect-smoke-score]] recurring, not a new result.

The candidate source does survive as log text, so a RECONSTRUCTED scenario is possible and must be labelled as such. **Before proposing a harness, check that the artifact it consumes actually contains the thing** ([[project-scenario-replay-harness]], [[feedback-grep-the-behaviour-not-the-name]] — I asserted the tree existed without looking).

A mutation that triggered NO finding therefore landed exactly on the cut, on the accepting side, at full confidence — **140 of 140 such mutations in the archive were ACCEPTED**. Sharing one number between "where the scale starts" and "what clears it" turned *neutral by default* into *accept by default*. Available wherever a computed score has a threshold: ask what the scale's zero point is, and whether anything else is standing on it.

I grepped a token I had invented and read its absence as evidence of absence. The dependency-grind retraction still stands on the timestamp evidence; the compile-count sentence I offered in support of it does not.

Edge tolerance added.

  - **➡ THE RULE: a build failure means the COMPILE OUTPUT is redundant, never that everything observed that cycle is.** The relay now carries the dissenting heuristics' own findings and drops only the outcome message the compile block already covers.

Its DoD clause 3 is recorded as UNMEETABLE AS WRITTEN — it specified a reproduction of the wrong shape, because **a DoD written from a hypothesis inherits that hypothesis**.
