## 2026-09-13 (Opus 5) — THE YARDSTICK'S SPLIT BECAME A DOCUMENT, AND TWO RUNS PROVED BOTH HALVES

- **➡ THE ONE THING TO CARRY: A DERIVED FILE THAT CAN BE COMPARED AGAINST ITS SOURCE IS A SECOND OPINION, NOT A CACHE.** `InputFormatExample` had a latent defect — a sentence-final period was only stripped when something followed the line — and it surfaced because the seed and the obligations file parsed the SAME sentence differently (`[a, b,c, d"e]` against `[a, b,c, d"e.]`). Neither was checked against a spec; they were checked against **each other**, and the divergence was the whole signal. Available wherever a derived file and its source can both be parsed.
- **✅ `T6-S`: the split is now `bootstrap/seed.obligations.txt`, tracked, generated by the local model, hand-editable — the OPERATOR's design.** Two hashes answering different questions: the seed's says whether the source moved, the body's says whose split it is. **Provenance, never a tamper gate** — rejecting an edited file is the same as not supporting editing. Their combination makes the fourth state safe (seed moved AND hand-edited → keep and warn). **Not mtime**, which a `git checkout` resets.
  - **📋 AUDITED against all four seeds, anchor by anchor: NO INVENTIONS ANYWHERE.** One real deficit — clitarget omitted the worked example, the sentence three oracles read — fixed by hand, which then reads back `EDITED` and closed anchor coverage 10/13 → 12/13. **Two audit limits recorded**: flightsim's seed has zero anchors so the check is blind there, and rung1's nine "uncovered" anchors are a FALSE ALARM because its seed describes an ADOPTED project, where routes are context rather than obligations.
  - **📋 The model's split beat the rule on recall AND granularity.** It recovers *"two consecutive double quotes mean one literal double quote"* and *"Quotes are removed from the value"*, which the rule **dropped entirely** — the two obligations behind both of this month's false completions. First prompt produced fragments (`404 if absent` with no owning endpoint); one rule — *each line must stand alone* — took fragments 12 → 0 on firestarter without losing recall.
  - **✅ WIRED, transition measured per target first:** closure edge 10→21 clitarget, 7→18 firestarter, 1→7 flightsim, 2→2 rung1. **Two defects the suite caught during wiring**, both mine: falling back to `statedIn` for both consumers silently narrowed coverage from 35 clauses to 12, and passing the text via a settable field made the analyzer stateful, which `T4-DQ`'s registration contract failed the build for.
- **✅ FIELD-PROVEN ON BOTH HALVES, one run each.**
  - **clitarget (`2026-09-12_19-42-02`, EDITED file, deadline, 17/19, failing 10,11): THE FALSE COMPLETION DID NOT RECUR.** Run `2026-09-11_16-27-02` closed **GOAL_FULFILLED** on the identical score and identical failing checks; this one refused. **Same defect, no longer declared done.** An obligation the OPERATOR wrote drove a correct execution refusal (`column` printing `b` for `b,c`).
  - **firestarter (`2026-09-12_22-44-28`, PRISTINE file, GOAL_FULFILLED, 23/23, 25 min): `criteria_executable_pct=79`** where the seed was **0-of-7 exercisable** two days earlier. 8 uncovered obligations reached the root edge and **3 were offered to the execution probe**, where none could be before. **⚠ `closure_attempts=0`, so only the PERMISSIVE direction was exercised** — the refusal path on a REST target with the new split is untested.
- **🔴 `T1-FZ`, found by the run and fixed: the probe WALLED a correct artifact.** *"exits 0 (no malformed rows)"* staged a MALFORMED file and demanded 0; the artifact exited 2, which the spec requires. Neither `T1-FY` guard reached it — one status, one shape, the shape NEGATED. **Fixed by ONE structural check**: the specification states which status each COMMAND yields for each shape, and an exercise is validated against that. Subsumes `T1-FY`'s route too, and sits at the single exit so no branch bypasses it.
  - **⚠ AND MEASURING CAUGHT ME BUILDING THE TRAP `T1-FY` WARNED ABOUT.** Applied globally the check removed **eight legitimate** `column <well_formed_file> <absent-name> → exit 4` exercises — the same eight `T1-FY` named when it recorded that such a guard *"would have traded one false refusal for eight lost checks"*. Scoped per command: 130 → 130.
- **🔴 `T2-FJ` REOPENED — my "no threshold compares against that number" finding was WRONG.** I audited the DOCUMENTED knob, found it dead, and generalised. The real boundary is **0.75, hardcoded in four places**, and `EvaluationAgent:458` does `(0.75 - score) / 0.20` — arithmetic on the second decimal of a 29-value quantity. **Method note: a dead knob is evidence about that knob, never about whether the behaviour exists elsewhere. Grep the VALUE, not the name.**
- **⚠ A MODEL FALSE NEGATIVE ON COMPLETE EVIDENCE, seen in the field.** clitarget's closure was blocked on three banked criteria the artifact demonstrably satisfied, with `evidence=complete` and zero croppings. `T2-FI` removed one cause of wrong verdicts; it never removed the model. **Two greps saved the attribution** — none of the three came from the obligations file, so the wiring was not implicated.
- **⚠ TEST BLEED NEARLY MADE ME MISREPORT A CLEAN RUN AS STAGNATION.** A full `mvn test` wrote 1034 `[main]`-thread lines into `data/firestarter/logs/autobuilder.log` **42 minutes after** that run's summary was written, including *"Root goal exhausted"* and *"Stagnation runaway"* from test fixtures (`goal-1`, `root-1`, `POST /orders`). Only the timestamps and thread names distinguished them. [[project-tests-rotate-live-logs]] again, from the log-reading side.
- **📋 Also filed, open: `T5-BN`** (`AnnotationVocabularies` bypassed at five sites, one of them in a file that imports it) and **`T5-BO`** (six interface observations; `llm unknown?` is not actionable until it can be located).

## 2026-09-11 later (Opus 5) — SIX CLOSED, AND EVERY ONE OF THEM CORRECTED A DESIGN I HAD ALREADY ARGUED FOR

- **➡ THE ONE THING TO CARRY: I VALIDATED THREE MECHANISMS AGAINST INPUT I CHOSE, AND THE FIELD SUPPLIES DIFFERENT INPUT.** `T4-DU` was built, green, and validated against three real artifacts — then went **10 opportunities and 0 probes** live, because its test criterion (*"…and exits 2"*) is the one shape that synthesizes and the loop never banks it. The verdict was validated; the REACHABILITY never was. **Before building a probe, ask what the corpus actually produces** — `scripts`-free, offline, two minutes. Every fix tonight was then measured against 357 real banked criteria, and that measurement refuted a design twice more.
- **✅ `T1-FY` — WALLING BY THE ORACLE, which is the failure the whole probe chain exists to prevent.** Run `2026-09-11_19-58-35` ended `deadline` at **19/19 with zero failing checks** after the closure edge refused it four times across 24 minutes: a compound criterion (*"exits with code 2 … or 0 if all rows are well-formed"*) made the probe demand exit 2 from a WELL-FORMED file. **Three guards existed for exactly this and each was defeated by where the words sat.** Fixed by counting the elided status, not by adding a fourth pattern. Corpus: 125 → 124 exercises, exactly one changed.
  - **The apparent signature was REFUTED before it became the fix.** Filtering for "well-formed fixture, non-zero status" returns nine, and **eight are correct** (an absent COLUMN in a good file). Building on the obvious shape would have traded one false refusal for eight lost checks.
- **✅ `T2-FI` — the verifier CROPPED its own authority and trusted the answer.** 150-line cap, `Main.java` is 215, and `countPhysicalLines` sits at 200. **Controlled ablation, same model, temperature 0, 5 samples per arm: cropped it answers the SPEC's value, whole it answers the CODE's.** Fixed with a budget plus an asymmetric guard — an all-clear on incomplete evidence defers, a BLOCKED verdict is untouched, because the severe failure is rare and the mild one is frequent.
  - **🔴 A SECOND HOME FOUND HOURS LATER at the EVALUATOR**, cropping at 150 lines *or 4000 characters*, and its answer is `llmScore`. Same fix. **And the knob had been there all along, unbound**: `EvaluationConfigProperties` was referenced by nothing, while `CLAUDE.md` documented its dead `heuristic-threshold` as the acceptance control.
- **✅ `T2-FJ` CLOSED NEGATIVE, and it refuted MY OWN numbers from two hours earlier.** I paired scores to decisions by PROXIMITY in the behaviour file; `mutation_scored` and `mutation_negotiated` share a `mutationId` and a structural join was available all along. **2,264 pairs instead of ~950 guesses: 0.71 is 86% not 30%, 0.72 is 89% not 56%.** So "a 26-point cliff" and "four ordinal bands" are FALSE; the shape is near-binary with the boundary between 0.65 and 0.68.
  - **The inversion is EXPLAINED and benign:** 0.92 accepts at 79% against 0.82's 92% because `DeterministicScoreHeuristic` overrides the model in **24 of 29 high-band flips (83%)** against 49% mid-band. That band is where the model is overconfident and the guard for it fires. **A redesign that smoothed it would have deleted a working correction.**
  - **Re-entry, so it reopens on evidence not impression:** a decision shown to hinge on a distinction the score cannot express.
- **✅ `T5-BM` — the graph refused the filed design.** *"Actions rendered around the node"* meets a goal carrying **216 actions**; its type/status breakdown is eleven rows. Summary first, verified end-to-end against the live store. **The legibility half of its DoD is explicitly NOT certified — no browser was involved.**
- **⚠ WHAT IS STILL UNPROVEN, so nobody reads six ticks as six settled things:** `T4-DU` and both `T2-FI` homes have had NO field run; `T4-DT`'s `injectExecutionRefutation` still needs a ROOT-level refutation; and `T2-FI` rests on one claim, one artifact, one model — re-run the two-arm ablation on another archived closure before treating cropping as the general explanation.

## 2026-09-11 (Opus 5) — T4-DT PROVEN IN THE FIELD BY A REGRESSION THE LOOP CAUSED AND REPAIRED, AND THE SAME RUN IS A FALSE COMPLETION

- **➡ THE ONE THING TO CARRY: THE INSTRUMENT COULD NOT ANSWER THE TICKET'S OWN DoD, AND THAT WAS FIXED BEFORE SPENDING A RUN ON IT.** `BootGate` counted every probe shape in one `probed=` figure, so a run could report `probed=8` without a single CONTENT criterion among them — and the DoD clause was precisely *"a content criterion is probed rather than abstained on"*. A run launched against that instrument would have come back unreadable. **Ask what the run will be ABLE to say before launching it, not after.**
- **✅ `T4-DT`'s LAST DoD CLAUSE IS MET, and both sides of it landed in one 16.5-minute run** (`2026-09-11_16-27-02`, clitarget from seed, 22 cycles, 10 merges, `GOAL_FULFILLED`). The ticket said a run that goes right cannot test this. It got both because **the loop broke its own field splitter mid-run**: `16:31` correct → `16:38/16:39/16:41` refuted → `16:44` repaired and silent. Both endpoints verified by running the jar by hand (`b,c` → `b` → `b,c`), never off the log line.
  - **Detect → refuse promotion → repair → the probe goes quiet on its own.** That is the actuator proven, not just a detector firing.
  - **A direct before/after on IDENTICAL input, which is worth more than the counts:** the refused sentence is the seed's own *"column <file> <name> — print every value…"*, and that exact text sits in `goal-107c93b2`'s criteria in run `2026-09-10_21-46-44` where the probe reported *"none can be executed"*. Same seed, same target, same sentence, abstained then, refutes now.
  - **⚠ `injectExecutionRefutation` (the `GATE_CRITERIA` item) IS STILL UNPROVEN.** Every refutation came from the SUBGOAL path; the root closure probe passed on all three visits. It needs a root-level refutation.
- **🔴 THE SAME RUN IS A FALSE COMPLETION — 17/19, checks 10,11 — AND THE SEED WAS AUDITED BEFORE THE LABEL WAS APPLIED.** Every malformed row is reported as `line 1` where the seed states `line 2` and `line 3`; the seed states the rule verbatim, so the yardstick is not the guilty party this time. At the closing moment: **4 seed requirements at the edge, 2 exercisable, `value criteria probed=0`** — the line-number requirement fell through to the model, which passed it. The new zero line is what makes that readable at a glance.
  - **The successor lever is narrower than "format synthesis" and is a GROUNDING problem, not a synthesis one:** the claim is a quoted message with THREE placeholders whose values depend on row position, while `groundedMessage` grounds one. **The probe stages the fixture itself, so it already knows which physical line each malformed row is on.**
- **📋 COST, against the 8-and-8 baseline:** `package_gate_runs` and `boot_gate_runs` both **14** (+75%), and the run still converged in 16.5 min. `criteria_executable_pct=31 count=8`.
- **⚠ TWO READINGS I HAD TO CORRECT MID-SESSION, both caught by measuring rather than reasoning.** (a) Three subgoal abstentions looked like one new mechanism ("the criterion names no operation"); the archive's abstentions turned out to be a DIFFERENT mechanism (injected `[BUILD_FIX]` items carrying seed prose), and my own tally's `criteria-not-found` counter is what exposed it rather than a silent zero. (b) I called the regression "not repaired, carried forward" six minutes before the repair landed — a negative read taken too early on a loop that was still working the item.
- **📋 A LIMIT OF THE ABSTENTION TALLY, for whoever measures this next:** counting goals where *"none of its N criterion(s) can be executed"* misses the commoner PARTIAL case — one goal stored 5 criteria and offered 1. The informative denominator is criteria-offered over criteria-stored, not goals-abstained over goals.

## 2026-09-10 later (Opus 5) — THE CLOSURE EDGE WAS BLIND TO EVERY NON-HTTP TARGET, AND THE SMOKE DENOMINATOR IS FIXED AT SOURCE

- **Asked whether to invent a fifth toycase. Answer was NO, with reasons, and the session spent the time on the two things that answer better.** Four targets exist (`firestarter` REST / `clitarget` CLI / `flightsim` render / `rung1` adopted) and a fifth, `pollbox`, was deliberately **retired** once it had bought its generality evidence (`f0f0d5dd`). The generality question is answered; the binding constraints are mechanism defects reproducible on targets already in hand, and another target only fragments an already-starved measurement N (`T6-N` needs 20 retained runs and has 5).
- **✅ THE SMOKE DENOMINATOR IS FIXED AT THE SOURCE, not just in the one consumer that worked around it.** `smoke_strict.py` now reports the SHEET'S own check count and `preflight.sh` prints `%d/%d` — the literal `/23` at `preflight.sh:488` was firestarter's size stamped onto every target. Verified against the real archive, which holds sheets of **12, 19 and 23** checks all previously recorded as `/23`. `survivability_report.py`'s workaround stays and its warning is rewritten: pre-2026-09-10 rows still lie, so do NOT simplify it to trust the stated denominator until they age out.
  - **A SECOND, LATENT defect in the same script, fixed with it: the expected-404 witness map is keyed by check NUMBER and was applied to every target's sheet.** Check `[7]` is *"GET /books absent"* on firestarter and *"a row spanning physical lines is well formed"* on clitarget. It never actually misfired in the archive (checked all 8 non-firestarter sheets, zero wrong discounts) — but a CLI `FAIL` line reads `got [2]`, unparsable by the three-digit status regex, so the witness reads as unobserved and the rule **withholds by default**: on a foreign suite it failed toward the strict side. Each rule now proves it is on its own sheet by the check's own label first.
- **🔴 THE REAL T4-DK RESIDUAL WAS NOT WHERE THE TICKET SAID, and the archived run says so directly.** The ticket pointed at making `SeedCoverageAnalyzer`'s 1-in-19 signal precise. Replay refutes that for the live specimen: run `2026-08-30_00-06-00`'s plan **did** bank *"A file containing a quoted field with an embedded newline is parsed as a single logical row"* — twice — and `MultiLineRowReader` **was** wired into `Cli`. Not a coverage failure, not a DI-wiring blindness.
  - **What actually closed the run:** the source-reading verifier answered `✗` for four criteria at `00:15:12` (gate refused, correctly), then `✓` for all seventeen at `00:16:16`, and the root completed at 17/19. **A model reading structurally-correct-but-wrong code was the only voice** — because `BehaviouralClosureVerifier` selected probes with an HTTP-verb-and-path regex, so on a target with no network surface its list is empty at EVERY closure. `CommandExerciseSynthesizer` existed and was wired only to `BootGate`. [[project-capability-outruns-routing]] again.
  - **✅ FIXED:** `core/build/ExercisableCriterionQuery` asks the exercisability question once for both target shapes; the verifier uses it and now passes the real spec text (the `""` was justified by a comment true of the request synthesizer and false of the command one). Suite **2948/0/5**.
  - **⚠ IT DOES NOT RECOVER THE FOUR CRITERIA THAT FAILED** — those are CONTENT claims and the synthesizer abstains on them by design, with a measurement behind it. Surface criteria are now probed where nothing was before; format synthesis is what would close the rest. Asserted in `ExercisableCriterionQueryTest` so the residual stays visible under a green suite.
  - **⚠ EXPECT A NEW COST ON NON-SERVING TARGETS:** closures there used to spend nothing at that line and now buy a package build and a boot (~5.6 s) when a surface criterion is present. The *"roughly 5 firings a run"* estimate in the verifier's javadoc came from a REST-only corpus — re-measure it, do not trust it.
  - **⚠ NOT FIELD-VERIFIED.** The check is a `clitarget` run whose `T4-CU` line stops saying *"none of its N criterion(s) can be executed"*. Fourth consecutive mechanism unproven for the same structural reason: each repairs a failure path, so a run that goes right cannot test it.
- **✅ FIELD-VERIFIED THE SAME SESSION — run `2026-09-10_12-33-26` (clitarget from seed, 13 min, `GOAL_FULFILLED`, 5 merges).** The closure check **spoke on a non-serving target for the first time**: **3 execution-based refutations against a structural baseline of 0**, naming real defects against the booted tool (`checkx` exited 1 where 3 was specified; wrong arity exited 1 where 5 was specified; the absent-path message never printed). `T2-FB` then cleared the banked criterion reads so the claim had to be re-earned. **2 abstentions, both CORRECT** — content criteria, this fix's known residual, confirmed from the field side. **No over-tightening**: converged in 13 min, final artifact has zero failing checks.
  - **The measured cost is real: `package_gate_runs` 4 → 8, `boot_gate_runs` 4 → 8** vs the archived baseline. Watch this if closure-edge time becomes a problem on command targets.
  - **🔴 19/19 IS NOT EVIDENCE FOR THE CHANGE.** Two runs on the PREVIOUS build also scored 19/19. The attributable set is the refutation/abstention/cost counts, never the score.
  - **✅ AND THE DENOMINATOR FIX IS FIELD-EXERCISED BY THE SAME RUN: `smoke_strict=19/19 discounted=none`.** On the old build that line read `19/23`. `discounted=none` also confirms the label gate kept the firestarter witness map silent on a CLI sheet.
  - **✅ THE THIRD HOME IS FIXED AND FIELD-PROVEN (`CriteriaExecutabilityReadout`).** It now asks `ExercisableCriterionQuery` like the probe path does. Its javadoc argued against redefining the figure twice in two days; **that objection was measured, not overridden** — over five archived runs both firestarter rows are unchanged (10, 28) while three command-target rows go 0 → 4/8/8, so no comparable row moves. **Swapping the predicate alone would have produced a NEGATIVE `namedButUnparsed()`** (0 endpoint mentions minus 4 executable), so `namingEndpoint` became `addressingSurface`, a superset of `executable` by construction. Field: `criteria_executable_pct=55 count=6` where the old build wrote 0. Suite 2955/0/5.
    - **⚠ Only ONE home of the invariant is left, and it is deliberately open**: `T4-CY`'s supplier via `SpecEndpointBehaviourQuery`. It INJECTS work items, so it is a steering change needing its transition measured first.
  - **🔴 PULLING THAT THREAD FOUND THE BIGGER ONE: FOUR OF SEVENTEEN FIELD WATCHES HAVE NEVER BEEN ABLE TO FIRE.** `t4cu-criterion-contradicted` reported `NO-OPPORTUNITY` on a run where its own fired pattern matched 6 times. It declares `"file": "app"`; `watch_report.FILE_MAP` knows `applog`; the lookup fell back to the key itself, resolved to a path that never exists, counted 0. Also affected: `t2eq-halt-edge-rescue`, `t2eq-packaging-item-retired`, `t4cu-probe-synthesis-yield`.
    - **This was never a command-target problem.** Re-evaluated with the key fixed, that watch reports **FIRED(47)** and **FIRED(155)** on two recent FIRESTARTER runs — the target the project measures most — where it had been reporting reassuring silence.
    - **The typo was the occasion; `FILE_MAP.get(key, key)` was the defect** — it turned a registry error into a zero count, and zero looks like good news. `count_matches` now raises and the reporter prints `REGISTRY-ERROR`; a known key whose file is absent stays a plain 0. Two tests pin it, one of which walks the real registry.
    - **⚠ TREAT EVERY PAST `NO-OPPORTUNITY` FROM THESE FOUR AS UNKNOWN, NOT AS EVIDENCE.** Any prior session that read one of them as "the mechanism had no chance" was reading a broken instrument. `T2-EQ`'s two are in that set.
  - **✅ AND `criterion_probe_runs` IS FIXED, superseding the note below within the hour:** the command path already emits a symmetrical marker (`T4-DD: exit-status criteria probed=…`), so both instruments now match `(behavioural|exit-status) criteria probed=`. Reads 8 where it read 0; firestarter runs unchanged at 7 and 8.
  - **⚠ NEW INSTRUMENT BLIND SPOT, found by this run: `criterion_probe_runs=0` while 8 boot-gate runs and 3 refutations happened.** The field greps a marker emitted only by the HTTP probing method; the command path emits nothing of the kind, so the counter is structurally 0 on any command target (firestarter reads 7-8). Same family as the smoke denominator. Filed on `T4-DK`, not fixed.
- **✅ THE FIX IS REPLAY-VERIFIED, not just green.** `scenario/ClosureEdgeBlindToNonServingTargetReplayTest` replays run `2026-08-30_00-06-00` at the closure-claim check. **Confirmed to FAIL on the pre-fix predicate** (both fix-asserting tests report Mockito's *"Wanted but not invoked"* — the gate never reached, which is the run's own behaviour) and pass after. Its fourth test asserts the CONTENT criteria are still NOT probed, so the residual cannot be buried by a green suite. Suite **2952/0/5**.
- **🔴 THE SAME INVARIANT HAS A SECOND HOME — measured, filed on `T4-DD`, deliberately NOT built.** `T4-CY`'s *"the specification states no probeable requirements"* has the same root cause as `T4-CU`'s silence: `GoalPlannerService.specDerivedRequirements()` is built entirely from `SpecEndpointBehaviourQuery`, which returns **0** for the clitarget seed while `SeedRequirements.statedVerifiableIn` returns **10** on the same text. **Firestarter is 6 against 7, which is exactly why this was never noticed** — the defect is invisible on the target the project measures most.
  - **Why it was not fixed in the same scope**, though [[feedback-ship-full-scope]] would normally say so: `T4-CY` INJECTS its findings as work items and each requirement buys a package build and a boot per interval, so widening it is a STEERING change with an unmeasured transition — and it would land on the same path as the closure-edge fix before that fix has had one field run, making the next run unattributable to either. Measure the transition on one CLI run first: requirements, probes, injections.
- **✅ THE CLAUSE SPLITTER IS DONE ON BOTH SIDES, and the sweep re-ran over 26 corpora (the original used one).** `CLAIMED_ENGAGEMENT = 0.6` SURVIVES: 0.5 and 0.6 both break zero assertions, 0.7 breaks 2, 0.8 breaks 4 — kept at 0.6 as incumbent. The bracket/quote SCAN now has one home; the GRANULARITY POLICY is a parameter, because the verifier needs whole obligations (a fragment walls a run) and the analyzer needs separable ones (a clause covering three endpoints can never be cleared).
  - **A third defect fell out of sharing the scan: a separator now only separates when whitespace follows**, so `GET /loans?memberId={id}` no longer ends a sentence at the `?`. That was the T6-S audit's own precision note on the VERIFIER side and it is now closed.
  - **⚠ TWO INTERMEDIATE DESIGNS WERE WRONG AND MEASURING CAUGHT BOTH** — sentence-scan alone made the Loans paragraph one 250-char clause; adding a comma separator fixed firestarter and broke clitarget, because **the comma is a list separator in one seed and a prose separator in the other**. Same lesson as the rest of the session, one layer down.

Prediction stated before launch and held exactly: 25 clauses against a baseline of 33**, and the rendered block carries whole sentences where the old build sent fragments. `criteria_executable_pct=13 count=3` confirms the readout fix on a command target too. Suite 2957/0/5.

Criterion 9 (*"a,\"b,c\",\"d\"\"e\" is parsed into one row with three fields"*) was verified ✓ by SOURCE READING while the smoke check for exactly that fails. It is a CONTENT criterion, which the synthesizer abstains on by design — **independent field confirmation of the residual `ExercisableCriterionQueryTest` pins**, on a new criterion. Format synthesis is the next real lever.

Not this time: all four failing checks trace to the seed's FIELD SYNTAX paragraph, and running the artifact on **the seed's own worked example** (*"the line `a,"b,c","d""e"` has THREE fields"*) gives *"expected 3 fields, found 2"*, `column y` → `b,c,de`, `column z` → nothing. It merges two fields and drops the escaped quote. **And it RUNS** — not the unbootable family.

    - **⚠ `T4-DT` FIELD RUN (`2026-09-11_00-58-42`, deadline at 17/19): the probe did NOT fire, and that is CORRECT — checked, not assumed.** That run's artifact parses fields correctly (`column col2` → `b,c`), so there was nothing to refute; failing checks were 10,11, the malformed-row message wording, a different class. **What it DOES establish is the risk the ticket flagged: NO FALSE WALLING** — live all run against a correct implementation and never a wrong refusal. Positive path still offline-only.
    - **✅ AND THE POSITIVE PATH IS PROVEN WITHOUT A LIVE RUN.** Both artifacts frozen as scenarios — `clitarget-field-split-broken` (prints `b,c,de`) and `clitarget-field-split-correct` (prints `b,c`) — each manifest carrying the observation AND the command that measured it. The verdict moved onto `Exercise.contradictedBy(output)` rather than widening `BootGate`'s API for a test. **Does NOT cover the process launch** — that is the harness's deliberate boundary; it was exercised by hand against both jars. Suite 2970/0/5.
    - **✅ THE ROUTING GAP IS CLOSED (2026-09-11).** Uncovered seed obligations now go to `closureClaimVerifier.refute` BEFORE `verifyCompletion`, and where the probe speaks **the model is not asked at all**. Refusal injects an actionable item stamped `GATE_CRITERIA` (marker-first), whose done-when is the **exercisable subset** — an unjudgeable obligation there would hold the item open forever, which is walling. 3 tests on the real completion edge, 2 confirmed failing with the routing disabled. Suite 2965/0/5. **⚠ New cost: on a command target every root closure attempt now buys a package+boot** (REST targets pay nothing — nothing exercisable). Not field-verified.

Natural completion is to offer them to the probe too; NOT built, because it changes the root closure path while T4-DT's own positive path is field-unproven.

Closes `T4-DK`'s content residual; `ExercisableCriterionQueryTest`'s abstention assertion is the acceptance criterion and should invert.

It is now permanent in `scripts/survivability_report.py`.
- **📋 WHAT SUPPRESSES MERGES, MECHANICALLY: the compile probe, and it does NOT distinguish good runs from bad.** Rejection reasons as a share of all rejections — collapsed **100% / 93% / 100%**, perfect **38% / 95%** — are dominated everywhere by *"Compile probe failed"*. **The loop proposes code that does not compile in every run; that is the system's baseline condition, not its failure mode.**

Longest merge drought: **60 and 42 cycles (ground) against 20 and 16 (healthy)**, where `RootConvergenceTracker.grinding()`'s javadoc already records **42 and 60 against 13 and 19** from a different run set. **Two independent measurements, the same numbers.** The answer is already largely built as T4-DQ slice 3's rewind-on-grind.
- **📋 `grinding()` needs 30 cycles of history (brake window 20 x 1.5) and 34% of runs never reach 30 — but that abstention is CORRECT**, because a run stopped at 12 cycles did not grind.
- **🔴 AND THAT RETIRES ONE OF T6-R's TWO NAMED CASES — MY OWN.** `2026-09-08_22-32-37` was cited throughout as the collapse to explain; it is **a 20-minute run I commissioned myself, killed by its deadline after 12 cycles**, scoring 0% because it never had time to build a controller. **The founding contrast paired a 71-minute perfect run against a truncated one.** The genuine grind population is **two runs**.
- **➡ THE BETTER-POSED QUESTION for whoever picks this up: within `deadline` runs, what separates the 6 that reached 100% from the 2 that collapsed?** Same harness treatment, n=16 instead of 5.
- **⚠ THIRD HETEROGENEITY FINDING OF THE SESSION**, after the AUTHORED bucket and the multi-target denominator: **"collapsed" was never one population** — 2 ground to death, 1 was cut off, 1 halted on an empty backlog, 1 ran long and landed at 39%. **Ask what a class CONTAINS before contrasting against it.**

## 2026-09-10 later (Opus 5) — T6-R ANSWERED, AND THE ANSWER IS NO

- **✅ `T6-R` CLOSED on a NEGATIVE result, which its DoD named as the likely and equally valuable outcome.** 26 features over the durable ledger and the graph; **none separates a converging run from a collapsing one beyond chance**, except `merges >= 6`, which is the tautology the ticket had already flagged (merges are how code enters the artifact the checks score). Instrument shipped: `scripts/survivability_report.py` + 12 tests.
- **➡ THE METHOD LESSON, and it is the one worth carrying: THREE WAYS A MEASUREMENT LIKE THIS LIES, all three hit here.**
  - **BASELINE.** 19 perfect against 5 collapsed means *"always say perfect"* already scores **79%**. Four features landed at exactly 79% — zero information — and an accuracy column alone would have sold them as 79% accurate. **Report lift over baseline, never accuracy.**
  - **MULTIPLE COMPARISONS.** Trying 11 features and reporting the best is not testing one. **The permutation test shuffles the OUTCOME and refits the whole search**: graph best 92% -> **p = 0.091, not significant**. Without it I would have reported a 92% discriminator.
  - **EXTREMES FLATTER.** Dropping the middle sharpens everything. **`score_max` has the strongest correlation found (rho +0.54) and leave-one-out accuracy of 64%, BELOW the 79% baseline** — it tracks the middle and carries nothing where the populations differ. **A correlation and a discriminator are different claims.**
- **🔴 THE PREMISE WAS PERIOD-DEPENDENT AND NOBODY KNEW.** *"Near-perfect or near-zero with little in between"* is FALSE over the corpus (median 83%) and TRUE only of the current build: **Aug 2026 — 37 runs, 25 in the middle; Sep 2026 — 10 runs, 8 perfect, 2 zero, ZERO in the middle.** **The middle vanished rather than thinned: the failure mode changed from partial success to all-or-nothing.** It also means the well-posed population is **ten runs**, which is why the causal question cannot be answered on this corpus.
- **📋 A SECOND NEGATIVE WORTH REMEMBERING: `crit_pass_rate` rho = +0.02.** **The share of the loop's own acceptance criteria that pass is UNRELATED to whether the artifact is any good.** That is the measured form of [[project-first-perfect-smoke-score]]'s question and an argument for keeping the smoke yardstick external.
- **📋 THE USER'S EARLIER CORRECTION PAID OFF AGAIN: ask the GRAPH, not the logs.** Logs rotate — 99 of 123 ledger rows have none, which is what confined the first pass to 15 runs. The graph holds **161 runs** and joins to 115 ledger rows on start time (UTC+2, 10-minute window). **Two data defects surfaced while using it:** `Merge` nodes are per FILE not per cycle (so files-per-attempt exceeds 1.0 and is not an acceptance rate), and one ledger key drew two graph runs because two JVMs started inside the join window.
- **➡ WHAT IS STILL OPEN AND MUST NOT BE READ AS ANSWERED: what SUPPRESSES merges.** T6-R asked what separates the populations and the answer is the thing definitionally downstream of the outcome. **Do not re-open on this corpus** — it needs a mechanism hypothesis tested by replay, or the accumulation of collapses that only time provides.
- **🔴 CORRECTED WITHIN THE HOUR, ON THE USER ASKING WHETHER `merges >= 6` WAS JUST THE TOYCASE. IT WAS — AND CHECKING IT FOUND A BUG THAT HAD MANUFACTURED THE ONE SIGNIFICANT RESULT.**
  - **`smoke_strict` states its denominator as 23 — firestarter's size — FOR EVERY TARGET.** A clitarget run passing 19 of 19 records `19/23`; a 12-check run passing 12 of 12 records `12/23`. Dividing by that literal ranked **perfect runs on smaller targets as 83% and 52%** and filed them in the MIDDLE — the population the whole analysis contrasted against. **Fixing it moved ledger permutation p from 0.011 to 0.326**, so the negative result is now TOTAL rather than partial.
  - **`merges >= 6` refuted by direct counterexample:** a 12-check target reached 100% with **4** merges. The threshold is an absolute count with no ratio to the target's own size.
  - **The graph half's p=0.028 winner is `crit_pass_rate >= 0.103`** — *"at least a tenth of criteria passed"*, the same tautology in another coat, with rho +0.09 across the range.
  - **➡ FOURTH MEASUREMENT TRAP, now in [[feedback-baseline-before-accuracy]]: A NORMALISATION IS A CLAIM ABOUT COMPARABILITY.** The bug survived a permutation test, twelve unit tests and a written-up commit **because every one of them ran downstream of it**. Regression tests added.
- **No run was commissioned**, per the ticket's own instruction: a field run cannot validate a corpus measurement and would return one sample against a question needing tens.

## 2026-09-09 (Opus 5) — A 23/23 RUN CARRYING 232 GRIND DIAGNOSTICS: THE SIGNATURE IS NOT THE OUTCOME

- **🏁 RUN `2026-09-08_23-52-13`: `PREFLIGHT: PASS terminal=GOAL_FULFILLED smoke=23/23 smoke_preserved=23/23 strict=23/23 discounted=none`, 71.3 min, 9 merges, 3 controllers, `preserved_source=loop-preserved`, peak at 52/52.** The current build reaches a perfect strict score at full length. Commissioned because a 20-minute run had scored 5/23 and the user asked whether the system had become unstable.
- **🔴 I CALLED THIS RUN A FAILURE 20 MINUTES BEFORE IT SCORED 23/23, AND THE ERROR IS THE DURABLE LESSON.** At 65 minutes it showed **232 `inference variable T has incompatible bounds` hits, 65 rollbacks, 7 merges** — I matched that against the documented grind family (prior instances: 274/91/173 hits) and reported it as the fifth member, "the mechanism is already visible". **It then finished perfect.** The 232 hits were the loop WORKING: proposing, failing, rolling back safely, converging.
  - **A DIAGNOSTIC COUNT IS NOT AN OUTCOME, and no count in the mid-run telemetry separated a run that recovers from one that does not.** The prior instances' hit counts genuinely bracket this run's, so the resemblance was real and still meant nothing ([[feedback-runs-are-variant-by-design]], [[feedback-activity-not-progress]] — a mechanism firing is not the same as harm).
  - **The ledger fact I had, correctly: runs with <=3 FINAL merges score badly (12/12).** That is a statement about COMPLETED runs. I applied it to a run in progress at 7 merges and called it worse than a run that ended on 3. **Mid-run merge rate does not predict the endpoint** — this run added only 4 merges between minute 4 and minute 65 and still closed at 9 with every check passing.
  - **➡ RULE: do not attribute an outcome to a run that has not terminated.** Report mid-run telemetry as telemetry. The terminal is cheap to wait for and is the only thing the ledger records.

But variance is chronic across the whole ledger, and it was far worse earlier (week of 07-27: **1 of 30** perfect, median 18). **The system is BIMODAL — roughly a third of full-length runs are perfect and a recurring minority collapse — not decaying.**
  - **⚠ DURATION DOES NOT PREDICT SCORE, and I claimed it did.** I first excused run A's 5/23 as "a 20-minute budget against a 45-90 minute norm". Measured: 15-25 min runs are **26%** perfect, 70+ min runs **33%** — indistinguishable. What predicts a bad score is FINAL merge count. Drop the duration excuse.

Caught by checking the field across the population instead of quoting one run's number ([[feedback-verify-population-before-claim]]).

Do not file this as a defect on the strength of the hit count.

But the fact is rendered into a PROMPT, and which cycle a prompt happens to carry it in is not when the fact became TRUE. **Corrected rule: a tree changes only by MERGE, so a finding with no merge preceding its first appearance was already true on the tree the run was handed.** Under that rule the filing run classifies INHERITED and the ticket is vindicated. **When a query disagrees with a run someone read by hand, doubt the query first.**

Suite **2931/0/5**, +7 tests.
  - **`ADOPT_PROJECT` is excluded deliberately** — an adopted tree has no goal yet, and rewriting someone else's source before anyone asked is an uninvited edit, not a repair. Pinned by a test rather than left to a reader.

Removing it broke `MergeAgentRegistrationRepairMergePathTest` at compile time — the right failure mode. **Had the field been left in place that test would have kept passing while exercising nothing**, which is the same trap as the dead constants found here this morning.

Closed by `StructuralTreeRepairWiringContractTest`, which asserts `@Component` under the scanned root plus an `@Autowired` field at each caller — **proven to bite by commenting out `@Component`, then restored**. Every existing test wires the collaborator by hand, so a green suite would have stayed green with it unwired.
- **⚠ THREE WATCHER/READING ERRORS OF MINE IN ONE VERIFICATION SESSION, all self-caught:** I read the target project's `BUILD FAILURE` (the loop's own compile churn) as autobuilder failing and had to re-arm a narrower monitor; I declared run B's JVM down while it was alive because my waiter used `pgrep -x java | head -1`, which matches **the compile JVMs the loop spawns** ([[project-run-lifecycle-runbook]]'s ninth variant, new form — always match the jar name); and I assumed `--autobuilder.run.budget-minutes` stops the JVM, when it caps the LOOP and leaves the process cycling, so a wait-for-exit loop would have hung forever. Preflight kills the JVM; a raw `java -jar` launch does not.
- **Nothing is committed.** Changed: `MergeAgent`, `AutobuilderService`, new `StructuralTreeRepair`, new `AutobuilderServiceInheritedTreeRepairTest`, new `StructuralTreeRepairWiringContractTest`, `MergeAgentRegistrationRepairSourceTest` moved to `core/build/StructuralTreeRepairSourceTest`, new `scripts/inherited_finding_report.py`, and the roadmap/overview edits for `T1-FW` and `T4-DR`. (`scripts/run_outcomes.json` was touched by preflight's own ledger during run A.)

## 2026-09-08 later (Opus 5) — T1-FV CLOSED: THE READER WAS NEVER MISSING, AND THE REJECTED HOME WAS THE WRONG DIRECTORY

- **✅ `T1-FV`'s CAUSE IS FIXED, not just its consequence.** `analysis/structure/RequestBodyContractQuery` reads what a handler DECLARES it accepts; `RequestBodySynthesizer` uses it for both halves — which type the components come from, and which of them can bind. **Ground truth on the filing run's own preserved artifact: the old entity-derived body draws 400, the declaration-filtered one draws the specified `404 {"message":"Book not found"}`.**
- **🔴 BOTH OF THE TICKET'S BLOCKING PREMISES HAD EXPIRED, and reading the code was what showed it.**
  - *"Nothing reads handler parameters"* — **false since T3-BX.** `WiringFactsAnalyzer:813` filters on the `@RequestBody` marker and resolves the parameter's declared type, to answer a different question with one consumer. **Capability built and unreached, not missing** ([[project-capability-outruns-routing]]). Before building a "missing" reader, grep for the reading.
  - *"Blocked on the 2026-08-26 correction"* — that correction rejected a handler-parameter reader **in `core/build`**, as a new web subsystem there. `analysis/structure/` already reads the artifact's web surface and `domain_decision_report.py` counts it BEHIND THE SEAM. **The objection was about placement and it had an answer nobody had looked for.**
- **📋 MEASURED, not argued: 70 preserved artifacts, 49 with a body-taking handler, 142 endpoint paths, ALL 142 declarations readable, 0 unreadable.** Running the synthesizer over them drops **96 components** — `id` 66, `returned` 19, `returnedAt` 5, `createdAt` 3, `loanedAt` 2, `status` 1. So 96 bodies now differ ([[feedback-verify-diff-is-substantive]]), and the archive contains BOTH triggers the ticket argued between (timestamps AND the boolean type mismatch).
- **🔴 IT SUBSUMES THE NAME-BASED STRIP THE TICKET HAD RULED OUT.** 66 of the 96 drops are `id` — removed because a numeric identifier cannot bind to a handler declared over strings, structurally, naming no field. The ticket forbade extending `withoutAssignedIdentifier`'s literal name list; reading the declaration reaches the same result for the right reason and generalises.
- **✅ CLOSED ON ALL FOUR DoD CLAUSES — but only after one attempt failed and its failure taught the population lesson TWICE in an hour.**
  - **The decline clause** (*"where the declaration cannot be read the synthesiser declines"*) was first implemented on the strength of **142 of 142 declarations readable over 70 preserved artifacts** — and **four existing tests went red and were right.** Those are FINAL trees; every one has controllers because it reached the end. The state the decline acts on is the MID-RUN tree, which that corpus contains none of. Reverted.
  - **Then measured on the right population and put back in.** `data/<target>/checkpoint-archive` holds **112 archived mid-run trees**. Restricted to body-carrying verbs and the paths the SPEC names: **281 derivations, 271 readable, 10 not — and all ten are trees whose endpoint is NOT SERVED YET** (nine have no controller at all). A success probe there fails whatever body it sends, so declining loses nothing and stops the synthesizer inventing a body for an absent endpoint.
  - **⚠ AND THE FIRST CUT OF THAT SCAN WAS ALSO WRONG — it said 1 of 102** because it generated a candidate path per model class, measuring `/apiresults` and other paths no criterion asserts. **Same error, opposite direction, within the hour: a population must be the one the code MEETS, not the one that is easy to enumerate.**
  - **The decline is scoped to `acceptableBody` only.** It promises a body the application will ACCEPT; `candidateBodies` only ever promised "no 5xx" and stays best-effort. Four model-only fixtures were given a controller — their subject (constraint satisfaction) is unchanged, and one would otherwise have kept passing VACUOUSLY.
  - **Generality was met by a second implementation that already existed:** `CommandExerciseSynthesizer` derives a CLI exercise from `CommandSurfaceQuery`'s declared surface and abstains when it is undeclared. It never reads a storage model, so this defect cannot arise there. **Built for T4-DD, independently, without knowing about this ticket.**
- **⚠ TWO WALKS OVER ONE DECLARATION NOW EXIST** (WiringFactsAnalyzer's in-memory one, this query's on-disk one). They share the vocabulary — moved to `AnnotationVocabularies` — but not the walk. **`T1-FW`'s pattern-grounding is the third consumer; unify the walk when it arrives**, not before, because unifying today risks a T3-BX regression for no measured gain.
  - **⚠ THIS IS NOT THE GENERALITY CLAUSE, and the two were asked about as if they were one.** Unifying the walks yields ONE reader of HTTP handlers instead of two — `T3-BX`'s question is an HTTP question as well. **Neither the shared vocabulary nor the shared walk moves generality by a line.** Worth knowing before someone closes a clause with a refactor that cannot satisfy it.
- **Seam accounting checked:** core clause-4 debt unchanged at 11 sites in 5 files; seam 23 -> 24. **Suite 2924/0/5**, both behavioural tests RED against the pre-fix code.
- **Both commits of the earlier half are in** (the GSD/`.planning` teardown, 846 deletions; and the T1-FW work). Nothing pushed.

## 2026-09-08 (Opus 5) — T1-FW RE-DERIVED; THE CAVEAT THAT SENT ME WAS STALE, THE SHIPPED HALF HAD THREE DEFECTS

- **➡ THE ONE THING TO CARRY FORWARD: `T1-FW` and `T1-FV` are ONE capability with two consumers.** The 37 pattern criteria abstain because grounding a reference that must RESOLVE needs the handler's own parameter declaration. Both ticket bodies already said "build the reader once, with two consumers"; what is new is that **there is no independent T1-FW slice left that recovers verification**, so selecting either ticket selects the same build. Work it THROUGH `T1-FV`.
- **🔴 A CAVEAT OUTLIVED ITS OWN RESOLUTION BY 13 DAYS AND COST A RE-DERIVATION.** The overview warned the fix "adds a fifth HTTP field to the record `T4-DD` wants dissolved -- re-derive before building". True of the FILED fix; the 2026-08-26 design read had already replaced it with a post-step on the exercise, and `Assertion` still has its original four components. **When a ticket's design read supersedes a caveat, the caveat is on the INDEX line and does not get updated by appending to the body.** Corrected.
- **🔴 THE SHIPPED HALF CARRIED THREE DEFECTS, ALL FOUND BY READING, NONE BY RUNNING.**
  - **The abstention logged at `DEBUG` and there is no `logging:` section in `application.yml`** -- so all 37 pattern criteria abstained invisibly in the field. **This is exactly what `T1-FU` fixed EARLIER THE SAME DAY, one method away in the same file.** A lesson applied to one abstention does not travel to its neighbour on its own; when closing a ticket about a class of defect, grep the file for siblings.
  - **`ABSENT_REFERENCE_STATUS` and `UNUSED_REFERENCE` had zero references** -- residue of the pattern-grounding build withdrawn on measurement. An unused private field fails nothing.
  - **The `statedInput` javadoc asserted "ONE HOME: `parse` consults it" and `parse` never called it** -- and `CriteriaExecutabilityReadout`'s javadoc promised "the figure the probe path will actually see" on the strength of that belief.
- **🔴 THE PROSE DEFECT WAS LOAD-BEARING -- two callers asked the READING question meaning the EXERCISE one.** `CriteriaExecutabilityReadout` counted pattern-input criteria as executable, and that reaches `criteria_executable_pct` in the run ledger; `BehaviouralClosureVerifier` let such a claim past the in-memory filter and bought a package build and a boot before probing nothing. **Neither produced a false failure** -- the synthesizer's abstention still held -- so it is overstatement and waste, not a return of the original defect, and I recorded it that way rather than booking a second T1 find.
- **✅ FIXED WITH ONE NAMED HOME: `BehaviouralCriterionParser.exercisable`.** `parse` keeps its contract as a READER (it is right that it reads a pattern and reports the endpoint), the synthesizer's abstention stays LIVE rather than becoming dead-by-construction, and the readout counts the shortfall under its own cause instead of blaming the wording. **The verifier test is RED against the pre-fix code** (Mockito names `verifyPackageable` as invoked). Suite **2914/0/5**.
- **⚠ `criteria_executable_pct` CHANGES MEANING AT THIS COMMIT** -- lower on runs with pattern-input criteria. Wrong, not redefined, but `criteria_ungroundable_input` is now emitted per run so a reader comparing rows across 2026-09-08 has the addend rather than an assurance.
- **⚠ `orientation_check.py` WAS FAILING ON ITS OWN SUBJECT.** It required the token `.planning/ROADMAP.md`, which the milestone cut deleted -- so removing the dead pointer from `MEMORY.md` PRODUCED a finding instead of clearing one. Retargeted to `meta_process.md`, 8 fixtures updated, and the live-index pin moved 97 -> 96 (that pin is what surfaced the index had moved at all). **A checker that names a surface outlives the surface.**
- **🔴 STILL UNCOMMITTED AND NEEDING A DECISION: the GSD/`.planning` teardown, 846 staged deletions.** The milestone was cut deliberately (its own closing line: "Next: the product queue"), but nothing is committed, so the deletion is one `git checkout` from being undone by accident. Nothing in this session was committed either.

## 2026-09-02 later (Opus 5) — T4-DQ SLICES 1-3 SHIPPED: THE LOOP CAN NOW GO BACK

- **✅ `T4-DQ` slices 1, 2 and 3 are in.** `core/checkpoint/` (bucket enum, `@StateHolder`, `CheckpointableState`, `StateManifest`, `CheckpointService`, `RewindService`, `RewindPolicy`, `StateFingerprint(+Service)`, `CacheMode`), `goal/GoalTransitionLog`, `client/LlmExchangeRecorder`. Wired at four places, each the ONE home that already existed: the post-snapshot point in `AutobuilderService`, the T5-I status chokepoint in `GoalPlannerService.persist()`, the convergence brake's halt edge, and `LLMClient.ask`. **Suite 2831/0/5 green**, `Neo4jGoalGraphServiceIT` green, charset guard clean. The as-built record with the full reasoning is appended to the ticket in `roadmap.md`; the durable lessons are in [[project-state-time-travel-design-note]].
- **🔴 THE STATE SURFACE WAS 40 HOLDERS, NOT 5 — and the design note's list was a starting point that read like an inventory.** A reflection scan over the compiled beans (Spring stereotype + a mutable, non-injected instance field) found 40; 12 are decision-bearing. **`AutobuilderService` alone holds eleven brake counters plus the pause flags — more decision-bearing state than the five named trackers combined**, and nothing had ever persisted any of it. This is why the deliverable is the registration contract and not the list: `StateHolderRegistrationContractTest` fails the build for an undeclared holder, **verified by removing one annotation (red, naming the class and its fields) and putting it back (green)**.
- **🟢 THE SHORT FIELD RUN EARNED ITS KEEP — TWO REAL DEFECTS IN NINE CYCLES, NEITHER VISIBLE TO A GREEN SUITE** ([[feedback-short-field-run-after-context-change]] holding exactly as written).
  - **The "NDJSON" transition log was not line-delimited.** The injected `ObjectMapper` has `INDENT_OUTPUT` on, so every event spread over ten lines. **Invisible within one JVM** — an in-memory counter carried the sequence — and fatal across a restart: the fold would have read zero events and re-created every goal. Fixed by writing through a compact writer and reading as concatenated JSON (recovers both shapes). **The regression test now builds its mapper from `JacksonConfig` itself**; a bare `new ObjectMapper()` in the first test is what let it through. Same defect, same fix, in `LlmExchangeRecorder`.
  - **One unresolvable holder lost the ENTIRE capture.** The bootstrap cycle runs from inside `AutobuilderService`'s own construction, so resolving it threw "currently in creation" and cycle 1's checkpoint recorded **zero** holders instead of eleven plus a named failure. `StateManifest` now resolves holders by bean NAME, each in its own try, and an incomplete checkpoint is not offered as a rewind target.
  - Also hardened after review, not from the field: the tree restore was delete-then-copy on the run's **only** artifact — now staged and swapped, with a test that a snapshot which staged empty leaves the live tree standing.
- **🔴 A PLAIN REWIND WOULD DETERMINISTICALLY REPEAT ITSELF, AND THE DESIGN DID NOT SAY SO.** Prompts go out at temperature 0: the same restored state asks the same question and gets the same answer. What breaks the repeat is that goals rewound out leave the live tree but stay in the graph's description corpus, which is what the planner's duplicate check reads — so the plan that led into the grind stays known and stays refused. **Both halves asserted in `Neo4jGoalGraphServiceIT`. Remember this before slice 4: backtracking needs something to DIFFER, and state restoration alone guarantees the opposite.**
- **🔴 CORRECTED THE TICKET'S OWN EVIDENCE.** `T4-DQ` cites run `2026-09-01_23-11-25` as "53 cycles, 25 rollbacks, 3 merges". Its behaviour trace holds **53 cycles, 5 merges and ZERO `merge_rolled_back` events**; `2026-08-31_05-47-22` likewise has 3 merges and no rollback events. The rollback lines in `autobuilder.log` come from this session's own `mvn test` runs appending to the live file ([[feedback-count-events-not-log-lines]]). **The true shape is starker than the cited one:** after their early merges the two runs landed nothing for **42 of 53 cycles (79%)** and **60 of 65 (92%)**.
- **The slice-3 trigger was MEASURED, and introduces no new constant.** It reuses the relative judgement the brake already makes (`ConvergenceTrend`: merges in the window against the run's own earlier rate) and adds a second response before the terminal one. Replayed over four runs (`RewindOnGrindReplayTest`): the first cycle at which a trailing 20-cycle window holds no merge is **24 of 53** and **25 of 65** for the ground runs — more than half of each run still ahead — and **never** for the two healthy ones. A late flurry (two merges in `23-11-25`'s last seven cycles) is why the decision belongs during the grind, not at the terminal.
- **The field run itself is not a quality signal:** 20-minute cap, 9 cycles, `terminal=deadline`, `smoke_preserved=5/23` off a reconstructed early snapshot. It was commissioned as an integration probe and answered that: 9 cycles produced 9 checkpoints, 12/12 holders captured with zero capture failures after the first, and the transition log recorded real `CHANGED` events alongside the startup `CREATED` baseline.
- **Left open deliberately:** slice 4 (branching/forking) not built; checkpoint cadence is every cycle, bounded at 40, and unmeasured against decision-points-only; and **the cache-mode question is now ASKABLE but unanswered** — `FAITHFUL` vs `FRESH` both work and nobody has run the same rewind twice to see whether the resolution cache is decision-bearing. That experiment is the design's own decision 3.

## 2026-09-02 (Opus 5) — THE FIELD RUN WAS THE WRONG INSTRUMENT, AND MY WATCHER WAS BLIND FOR AN HOUR

- **🔴 THE VERIFICATION LESSON, user-raised as "this is inefficient" and it is the durable one: DO NOT VERIFY A DETERMINISTIC PROPERTY WITH A RARE NATURAL EVENT.** `T1-FX`'s DoD demanded a run where the repair fires in the introducing merge's own cycle. The property is deterministic — does the hook's disk read see what this merge just wrote? — but the trigger occurs in **4 runs of 16**, so a 60-minute run is a 25% lottery ticket for something a temp directory settles in two seconds. Run `2026-09-01_23-11-25` returned **NO-OPPORTUNITY**, exactly at the base rate.
  - **✅ REPLACED WITH A MERGE-PATH TEST THAT IS RED AGAINST THE PRE-FIX CODE.** `MergeAgentRegistrationRepairMergePathTest` drives the real `mergeCompletedBranch` over a real `FileService` on a real directory, peers + injection site arriving in the merge under test, `previousOutput` deliberately EMPTY. **The first cut's weakness was that it MOCKED `readFolderContentsResolved` — the exact behaviour in question.** It also passes the ABSOLUTE `outputPath` production builds, not the relative `"output"` the older IT uses.
  - **The three field-only risks are retired by observation, not argument:** the analyzer is `@Autowired(required=false)` but non-null (`T3-CA` fired in `19-50-35`); `outputPath` to disk-read resolution is exercised in production by the sibling mover making the identical call (`01-47-47`). **Field DoD restated as a PASSIVE WATCH** — free to read if the fact ever recurs, no run commissioned.
- **🏁 RUN `2026-09-01_23-11-25` (firestarter, 59.2 min, `deadline`, `smoke=5/23`): NO-OPPORTUNITY for `T1-FX`, and NOT a regression.** 0 announces, 0 repairs, and **0 `could not be applied`** from the new tree read. The low score is the known catastrophic family: **third run with the `inference variable T has incompatible bounds` grind** (274 / 91 / 173 hits in `08-31_05-47-22` · `08-31_23-26-24` · this one), 3 merged cycles against 25 rollbacks in 53 cycles, **zero controllers in the artifact**, and a **byte-identical passing set** to the pre-change 5/23 run (`[7] [12] [14] [15] [22]`, all passing spuriously BECAUSE the endpoints are absent). **The two priors ran on builds without this change**, so the family is build-independent.
- **⚠ WATCHER BLINDNESS, EIGHTH VARIANT AND THE NASTIEST YET — ALIVE ALL HOUR, ZERO BYTES.** A `tail -F … | awk -f filter` monitor never reported the `PREFLIGHT:` terminal line; both processes were still running at the end. **`awk` here is `mawk`, which block-buffers its INPUT from a pipe (~4KB).** The launch log totalled **4515 bytes across the whole hour**, so everything past the first block sat unread forever. Replaying the identical pipeline against the finished file prints instantly — **the pipeline is correct and only the streaming case fails**, which is why every after-the-fact test passed.
  - **`fflush()` CANNOT FIX IT AND THE TOOL DOCUMENTATION RECOMMENDS IT.** `fflush()` flushes awk's OUTPUT; the record is never READ. Verified fixes: **`mawk -Winteractive`**, or `grep --line-buffered` ahead of awk. `stdbuf -oL` on `tail` does nothing — `tail | cat` streams fine, so tail was never the buffer.
  - **⚠ AND MY PRE-ARM CHECK PASSED WHILE MISSING IT.** I ran the standing absent-and-present check as `printf … | awk` — a closed stdin that flushes at EOF. **Exercised for CONTENT, never for STREAMING.** New standing rule in [[project-run-lifecycle-runbook]]: after arming a watcher, append a marker to the watched file and confirm the notification ARRIVES.
- **📋 `T5-BK` FILED — six `*IT.java` classes holding 20 passing tests never run in `mvn test`.** Surefire's defaults do not match `*IT.java` and no failsafe execution is configured. **Found only because adding a test moved the suite total by zero.** All 20 pass when named explicitly, so this is invisibility, not rot. Not fixed on the spot: three need Neo4j via Testcontainers, so defaulting them on makes `mvn test` require Docker — a policy call, not a silent tweak.
- **Suite 2772/0/5.** Still uncommitted on `main`: the `T1-FX` fix, two test classes, `T5-BK`, and the memory updates.

What its log DOES establish: the three types were merged for the first time at **18:51:20-21** and that was the last of four merges, so the shape would have defeated the hook. **Check whether the code under discussion existed in the run you are citing** — [[feedback-dont-evaluate-a-build-with-older-runs]] pointed the other way round.

  - **`T4-DB`** — all clauses met and the win explicitly NOT claimed: `[14]`/`[15]` fail in 11 of 16 rows in the Aug 1–5 era and 1 of 5 after Aug 22, but that boundary is also the model swap. What the ledger supports is that the failing FAMILY moved.
  - **`T2-FD`** — closed on its own DoD; the score question it deliberately separated stays a note for `T6-L`.
  - **`T2-EX` CLOSED WITH A NEGATIVE DIRECTION CALL: do not steer on the executable share.** 14 runs, range 31–69%, mean 55.4%. Perfect runs mean 60.4%, everything else 52.7%, unbuildable runs 53.8% and include a 69%. **The decisive case is this run: 23/23 on both trees at 51% — the lowest share of any perfect run.** A run scores full marks with half its criteria unverifiable; the premise does not hold as a constraint.
- **TWO NO-OPPORTUNITY READINGS RECORDED INSTEAD OF PASSIVE WAITING.** `T2-FE`'s override has fired **0 times across 85 retained logs** — and the reading is narrower than it looks, because the heuristic is silent on its ordinary path, so this measures firings, not reachability. `T3-BX` cannot fire on a tree that declares **no validation annotations at all**; this run's 400s come from a `GlobalExceptionHandler`, so the "decorative validation" premise describes a shape this target produces only sometimes.
- **OBSERVABILITY GAP FIXED — both peer rules were invisible in the field.** `T3-BW` (annotations) and `T3-BZ` (placement) reached the evolution prompt and nothing else: no log line, no behaviour event, and prompts are not logged. That is why the 08-23 watch keyed on a proxy and read NO-OPPORTUNITY. `WiringFactsAnalyzer.announce` now emits one INFO per FIRING carrying the facts, shared by both rules. **One line per firing, not per anomaly — count DISTINCT statements.**
- **`scripts/failure_family.py` CORRECTED — it named the wrong owning ticket, twice over.** A package nothing on the classpath provides is now `dependency-missing` rather than "a type the code never wrote" (`org.springframework.data.jpa.repository` → `JpaRepository` on run `00-07-06`), and classification is scoped to the LAST failing compilation — the first version reclassified a run off a `jakarta.persistence` diagnostic at log line 22,178 that the run repaired, when the failure that ended it sat at line 138,106. Both are the cascade-read-as-cause shape `T2-FA` describes, in an offline script. 21 tests.
- **⚠ `T6-L`'S BASELINE RESTARTS FROM TONIGHT — a ten-run window that SPANS A CODE CHANGE measures a mixture of builds, not a system** (user-raised, and it corrected a reading I had already written). The ledger's history stands as history — 79 runs, per-attempt all-time 48%, newer half 68%, last 12 64%, and 50 of 79 runs (63%) ever produced a scorable artifact — **but none of it is a rate for the current build.** Both zeros in the last ten predate the change that addresses them: `2026-08-24_00-07-06` failed on the undeclared-dependency shape and the classifier swap answering it shipped at 17:41 that day, AFTER the run. **The post-fix population is n=1.** Ten consecutive runs on an unchanged build is what the clause always asked for; do not quote the mixed window as where the loop stands.
- **⚠ THE EVALUATION RULE THIS SESSION LEARNED, user-stated: DO NOT EVALUATE A BUILD USING RUNS THAT PREDATE IT.** The history of the session doing the fixing is not measurements about the fixed system — that belongs to the NEXT session's runs. It bit twice in one evening: the `T6-L` ten-run window mixed pre- and post-fix builds, and a zero-run in it was caused by exactly the shape fixed hours later. **A window is only a rate when the build is constant across it.** Related but distinct from [[feedback-verify-population-before-claim]], which is about scanning the WHOLE population; this is about the population being the RIGHT one.
- **TWO DEFECTS FOUND BY ASKING WHY A ZERO-RUN STAYED ZERO, and the second is live.** `T2-FF` (filed, then DEFERRED the same hour): a method whose branches return different shapes emits `inference variable T has incompatible bounds`, which no extractor can read (`ContainerMismatchExtractor` requires the literal `cannot be converted to`), so the injector defers to the model — 12 distinct sites across 28 failing builds in the zero-run, present in 12 of 85 retained logs. **Its own gate refuted its causal claim within the hour**: the shape persisted into the last failing build in only 4 of 12 runs and NOT in the zero-run, and those four scored 23, 22, 18 and 5 — presence and persistence both fail to separate. What survives is a wasted-build COST claim, not an outcome one.
  - **`T1-FT` (filed AND FIXED): the analysis contract's own response-format placeholder was admitted to the goal tree as a subgoal.** `AnalysisResponseParser` took every non-empty dash line under `ProposedExecutableSubGoals:`, so a model echoing the format got `<next subgoal description>` in as work. In `2026-08-22_06-19-55` that node was the run's most frequent current subgoal — 11 renders, ~21 minutes of 55 — and closed **COMPLETED**, counting a goal that never meant anything towards completion. **Tonight's 23/23 run had one too** (SUBSUMED, so cheap) — the defect is live, and its cost ranges from negligible to a fifth of a run. Fixed in the parser: a proposal that is NOTHING BUT an angle-bracket placeholder is refused, while `ResponseEntity<Book>` still parses. 3 tests.
- **🔴 THE BIGGEST FINDING OF THE NIGHT, AND IT IS ABOUT THE INSTRUMENTS, NOT THE LOOP: two of the last four runs were misjudged by the harness rather than failed by the loop.**
  - **`T6-P` (proven, fixed):** `smoke23.sh` interpolated ids unquoted, so any artifact choosing UUID ids received malformed JSON and answered 400 to every `POST /loans`. Booted the artifact and asked both ways — suite's body **400**, same body with ids quoted **201**. Re-scored run `2026-08-24_19-46-29`: **13/23 → 20/23**. Numeric ids emit bare exactly as before, so the ledger stays comparable (verified: the 23/23 numeric artifact re-scores 23/23).
  - **`T1-FU` (filed, mechanism PINNED, fix written): a probe sequence longer than the request budget was TRUNCATED and then judged.** The model answers the bounded-count criterion CORRECTLY in nine requests — four DIFFERENT books, one member, four loans. `parse()` kept the first six, dropping the three loans that establish the precondition, so the probe asked a member holding ONE loan to be refused a second, got a correct **201**, and recorded a contradiction that blocks root closure **9-34 times per run**.
    - **Nothing downstream could catch it:** `isUsable` only checks the sequence ENDS on the asserted verb/path (a truncated tail still does), and `SETUP_REFUSED` only fires on a REJECTED setup (these all succeed).
    - **The artifact is correct** — booted two of them; one member + four different books gives `201·201·201·422` (and `200·200·200·422` on the HEAD run, whose separate defect is 200-instead-of-201 on create). Their smoke check for that rule passes.
    - **⚠ A TEST ASSERTED THE TRUNCATION AS INTENDED BEHAVIOUR** (`sequenceLengthIsCapped`), so every green suite protected the defect. Replaced with three tests, one carrying the nine-request field answer verbatim.
    - **NOT a regression from 2026-08-24:** a user-requested baseline run from a worktree at `8c375af` (08-23 03:07) reproduced the identical signature 20 minutes in — same blocks, same `-> 6 request(s)`, same answer byte-for-byte. **Five runs, four builds.** And because the answer is identical across builds (per-criterion cache + deterministic model), it fires the same way EVERY time the criterion is reached, not intermittently.
    - **⚠ I GUESSED THE MECHANISM WRONG TWICE FIRST, both recorded on the ticket.** "No setup check" — false, `SETUP_REFUSED` fires 579 times in one run. "`returnedAt` defeats the loan count" — false, tested. Both came from reading interleaved probe lines out of a shared log window; the answer came from the model's own logged output plus the code.

- **🔴 THE CLOSURE GATE IS THREE-QUARTERS NOISE, AND IT IS THREE INDEPENDENT DEFECTS — decomposed completely on run `2026-08-24_22-14-05`, every result tested against the booted artifact.**
  - `GET /loans?memberId={id}` ×2 → **`T1-FW`**: the probe drops the query string and requests bare `GET /loans`; the artifact correctly refuses a missing required parameter with 400. **Largest single contributor.** `normalizePath` strips the query on purpose — right for endpoint IDENTITY (5 callers), wrong as a REQUEST builder (1 caller).
  - `POST /loans → 422` → **`T1-FU`**: the model answers correctly in 9 requests, `parse()` keeps 6, the loans that establish the precondition are dropped.
  - `POST /loans → 404` → **`T1-FV`**: the body is derived from the ENTITY, so it carries `createdAt`/`returnedAt` the endpoint refuses.
  - `POST /books → 201` → **GENUINE.** The artifact answers 200 and its own smoke check `[1]` agrees.
  - **⚠ NO SINGLE FIX RESTORES `GOAL_FULFILLED`** — I said `T1-FU` was "the strongest candidate" and had to walk it back twice. Shipping one and watching the terminal state would read as a failed fix. Verify each on its own criterion against the booted artifact; expect an outcome change only from the full set.
  - **The method that found all three, after two failed guesses:** stop inferring from interleaved log windows, boot the artifact, and send the exact request the probe sent beside the request the criterion describes. Every one of the three fell out in a single comparison.
- **THE READING THIS FORCES ON THE SCORES:** 23/23 is **8 of 80 runs (10%)** all-time, not the norm I had been implying by quoting the good ones. Recent scorable runs sit 18–23. Nothing in tonight's measurements shows the loop getting worse; two of four recent runs were mis-scored or mis-judged by the apparatus.
- **METHOD THAT PAID OFF TWICE TODAY: measure the opportunity BEFORE believing a mechanism matters, even one you just wrote.** The coordinate-search branch and the failure-family fix both changed meaning after the corpus was scanned; and reading the code before building saved rewriting three probes that already existed ([[project-capability-outruns-routing]]).

## 2026-08-26 (Opus 5, user-directed) — THE ARCHITECTURAL CORRECTION THAT REDIRECTED THE SESSION, AND TWO T4-DD ITEMS BUILT

- **🔴 THE USER'S CORRECTION IS THE HEADLINE, and it was right against the code.** I recommended `T1-FW` + `T1-FV` as the next work. The reply: *"they read exactly like they'd hardcode further webservice logic into Autobuilder, while we concluded in T4-DD that we need the exact opposite."* Checked against the source rather than the ticket prose, and it is worse than the tickets admit: `BehaviouralCriterionParser.Assertion` is literally `record(criterionText, verb, path, expectedStatus)` — **the exact `verb+path+status` record T4-DD's CLI run named as the missing outcome abstraction** — with the verbs hardcoded as a regex alternation and "a status" defined as 100–599. `T1-FW`'s prescribed fix (*"carry the component in a SECOND field"*) adds a fifth HTTP field to it, **failing T1-FW's own last DoD clause** (*"expressible for a target whose input component is not an HTTP query"*). `T1-FV`'s *"read the interface's own declaration"* is a handler-parameter reader, a new web subsystem in `core/build`. **Neither should be built as filed.**
  - **The reframe it produced:** T1-FW/T1-FV (what is an exercise?), T4-DD item 2 (what is an observed outcome?), and T4-DD item 3 (what does "done" look like?) are **one missing abstraction with three faces**, not three tickets ([[feedback-one-invariant-one-home]]).
  - **⚠ THE LESSON FOR TICKET-READING: a DoD clause promising generality is prose, not architecture.** Both tickets open with a domain-free INVARIANT and then prescribe a fix that violates it. Read the fix against the code, not the framing.
- **✅ ITEM 3 BUILT AND FIELD-PROVEN — the gate now asks which COMPLETION MODEL applies BEFORE it reads an exit status.** `BootGate.launchAndObserve` asked the served-surface question on the clean-exit branch and **never on the non-zero branch**, so a terminating artifact was reported as one that crashed at startup. The asymmetry was already visible in the test file (`cleanExit_whenSpecNamesNoEndpoints_abstains` existed; its non-zero sibling did not). Also neutralised the injected imperative (`GoalPlannerService:3108`) and fixed the harness half (`PREFLIGHT_SMOKE_MODE=terminating`; measured `boot-failed` -> `SMOKE: 12/12` on the same artifact).
  - **Run `2026-08-26_02-20-47`, fresh target: `terminal=GOAL_FULFILLED smoke=12/12 smoke_preserved=12/12`, 4 merges, 2.8 min, and the `start and serve` injection fired 41 times on the previous CLI run and 0 on this one.**
  - **⚠ THE MERGES AND DURATION ARE NOT A RATE** — one run against one run. **41 -> 0 is the claim that stands**, because it counts the mechanism that changed.
  - **⚠ AND THE `OK` IS AN ABSTENTION, NOT A VERIFICATION** — `criteria_executable_pct=0`, `criterion_probe_runs=0`. A correct artifact, and nothing in the loop checked it. Removing a brake that fires wrongly leaves no brake; that is what item 2 is for.
- **✅ ITEM 2 BUILT — and the corpus made it far smaller than the design note assumed.** Reading the 61 criteria the first CLI run authored showed **the invocation is written INTO the criterion**, exactly the way an endpoint is: *"Running `<launch> unknown` prints … and exits with code 3"*. **So no surface discovery and no model were needed** — it is a parser, `CommandCriterionParser`, plus a read-invoke-compare loop in `BootGate`. Suite **2573/0/5**.
  - **The abstain rules came from the corpus, and two would otherwise have manufactured contradictions against a correct tool:** a disjunction (*"exits 0 or 2"*) yields only ONE matched status; a negation (*"(never 5)"*) names a status the tool must NOT produce. Arguments the criterion DESCRIBES rather than gives are declined too — inventing a fixture is `T1-FW`'s own failure.
  - **⚠ NOT FIELD-VERIFIED** (shipped after the run) and **fixture-needing criteria still abstain** — the CLI analogue of the HTTP setup sequence is the next slice.
- **METHOD THAT PAID OFF TWICE: read the real corpus before designing.** The seam looked like a subsystem until the 61 authored criteria were read; and both of item 2's non-obvious abstain rules are verbatim field text, not anticipated shapes. Same lesson as [[project-capability-outruns-routing]] one level up.
- **⚠ Everything above is UNCOMMITTED** (7 files). `clitarget/` was reset to its seed for the run; the pre-reset tree is backed up in the session scratchpad.
