- **`T4-DD` items 3 and 2 are built and field-verified.** Item 2 now reads the declared command surface, synthesises exercises, builds a content fixture from the specification's own worked example, and observes output as well as exit status. **Field runs probe 3–10 criteria where every earlier CLI run probed none.** Suite 2597/0/5. Commits `0ab0313a`, `f3782b2e`, `7113c31d`, `ae922288`.
- **🔴 I BUILT THIS PROJECT'S OWN RECURRING DEFECT AND THE FIELD CAUGHT IT WITHIN ONE RUN.** `groundedMessage` substituted a fixture PATH into `<n>` of *"line `<n>`: expected `<h>` fields, found `<f>`"* and refused closure six times on an artifact that answered the criterion correctly. **Two causes worth carrying forward: (1) the doc comment stated the correct rule and the code did something else — a comment is not a guard; (2) my own three-artifact verification compared EXIT STATUSES only, so the message half was unverified while I reported the result as validation.** Fixed (more than one placeholder ⇒ no message check), re-verified with the message half included, and confirmed at zero occurrences in the next field run.
- **🔴 THE REMAINING FALSE COMPLETION IS A CRITERIA-COVERAGE PROBLEM, NOT A VERIFICATION ONE — and this is the handover point.** Run `2026-08-26_15-11-51` fulfilled at 17/19; the two failures are about rows spanning physical lines inside a quoted field. It authored **9 criteria, all about the argument surface, none about the input format**. The gate executed everything it could and correctly found nothing. **Measured: the 17/19 artifact probed against the richer 11-02 corpus ALSO yields 0 contradictions — no run has ever authored a criterion for that requirement**, though the specification states it plainly. That is `T4-DA`'s question reached from the verification side. Not started.
- **The harder CLI specification and its yardstick are the durable assets here**: `clitarget/bootstrap/seed.txt` (quoted fields, doubled quotes, physical-line numbering) and `scripts/smoke_cli_quoted.sh`, calibrated reference 19/19 vs naive 11/19 with all losses on the quoting axis. Both spec/yardstick pairs remain runnable.
- **⚠ THE LEDGER IS BLIND TO THE NEW CHANNEL** — summaries report `criteria_executable_pct=0` and `criterion_probe_runs=0` while ten criteria were probed, because those fields count the HTTP path only. Fix before quoting any rate from them.

## 2026-08-27 — WHY THE 23/23 RUNS STOPPED: THE YARDSTICK GRADES A STATUS THE SPECIFICATION NEVER STATES

- **🔴 THE ANSWER TO "what happened to the 23/23 smokes", and it is not variance and not a regression.** Today's REST runs score 18/23 failing `{1,8,13,17,18}` — **all one defect, creates answering 200 where the suite wants 201**. The same family failed on `2026-08-24_22-14-05` (`{1,13,17,18}`) **before any of this session's work**, so it predates every change.
  - **THE SPECIFICATION NEVER STATES A SUCCESS STATUS.** The seed says *"POST /books (create; title required, max 120 chars; isbn required…)"* and nothing about 201. Audited every one of the 23 checks against it: **everything the yardstick asserts is spec-stated EXCEPT the success statuses** — 201 on create, 200 on read and on a successful return. The 200s are effectively universal; **201-vs-200 on a create is a genuine convention choice, and it is exactly the five failing checks.**
  - **THE FULL CAUSAL CHAIN, every link verified in the run's own artifacts:** the spec omits the status → the loop authors *"POST /books returns HTTP 201 with the created book body on valid input"* → `stampCriteriaBinding` classifies it **AUTHORED** (correctly — it is not grounded in the spec; 8 criteria were stamped AUTHORED in that run) → `specBoundBehaviouralCriteria()` **excludes AUTHORED criteria from the completion gate** (also correctly — that exclusion is what stops the loop blocking on requirements it invented, the very failure `T1-FW`'s 404 case demonstrates) → **so the create's status is never verified in-loop** → the external yardstick asserts 201 and removes five checks. **Whether a run scores 23/23 depends on whether the model happened to pick the conventional status.**
  - **⚠ AND THE LOOP HEDGED AROUND ITS OWN DEFECT:** one authored criterion reads *"POST /members … returns HTTP 201 **or 200** with the created member object"* — a requirement its own wrong behaviour satisfies.
  - **The machinery to catch this already exists and is not the problem.** Creates have been probeable since 2026-08-23 (`FieldConstraintDiscovery` supplies a body the artifact's own validation accepts, and `BootGate` reads a refusal as abstention). Valid create bodies were going out as probe SETUPS in the same run. The only missing link is that the requirement is not spec-bound.
- **⚠ TWO CONFOUNDS IN TODAY'S COMPARISON WERE MINE, and they matter more than the defect.** The three 23/23 runs each ran **~90 minutes, 35–46 merges, 75–77 cycles**; today's ran at a **60-minute cap** and managed **9–10 merges, 14–23 cycles** — a quarter of the work. Cycles were also ~3.4x slower (4.1 vs 1.2 min/cycle), plausibly the cost of more probing (`criteria_executable_pct` 55% → 86%). **A 60-minute run does not measure against 90-minute runs.** [[feedback-dont-evaluate-a-build-with-older-runs]]
- **⚠ AND A REAL RISK THIS SESSION INTRODUCED: `T1-FW` removed 190 false closure blocks, and those blocks were accidentally keeping the loop working.** Run `2026-08-26_21-36-31` fulfilled at **18.4 minutes with 9 merges** and shipped 18/23 — all 18 executable criteria satisfied while five real checks failed. The fix is correct (the blocks were false, proven against a booted artifact) but nothing replaced the pressure they applied. **Removing a false brake reveals that the true brake is missing.**
- **23/23 was never the norm: 8 of 80 runs all-time.** The recent cluster is three runs on 08-23/08-24 plus the `T6-Q` A/B series, and that series ran from FIXED pre-change worktrees, so it measures neither build.

Run state ignored for all three targets; only `bootstrap/seed.txt` is tracked. **The graph is what saved this run's record** when the directory looked lost ([[project-run-lifecycle-runbook]]): `lineage-76eda78f`.

Selection was already solved by `T4-CK`; what was missing is the convention plus ignore rules. Three targets exist now. **`flightsim` deliberately has NO yardstick** — a render loop has no scriptable observation channel, and writing one is the sharpest available test of `T4-DD`'s observation seam. Do not fake one.

Its open design question is also already written down: where the GOAL comes from on a cold read.

What carries the domain is the evolution prompt's worked EXAMPLES — `package com.example.demo;`, a web-framework controller, a `spring-boot-starter-parent` pom — which on a `com.flightsim` LWJGL target is a worked answer to a different question.

## 2026-08-31 (user-directed A/B) — `T3-BZ` IS IMPLICATED BY RUNS AT p=0.007, AND MY REASON FOR DISMISSING RUNS WAS THE ERROR

- **🔴 THE RESULT: with `T3-BZ` 1 perfect of 5 (23,21,15,6,5 — mean 14.0); without it 8 perfect of 8 (mean 23.0).** Fisher one-sided **p=0.007**; exact permutation test on the 9-point mean gap **p=0.007** (two tests, the second using full scores rather than a threshold I picked). The 8 comprise 7 control runs plus the `T2-FH`-only arm.
- **`T2-FH` IS CLEAN AND `T3-BZ` IS NOT — established by bisect, not by argument.** Cherry-picking each commit onto main separately: `T2-FH`-only scored **23/23** (`2026-08-31_12-32-04`), `T3-BZ`-only scored **6/23, 23/23, 15/23**. The two commits touch disjoint files and cherry-pick cleanly, so the split is well-formed.
- **⚠ THE METHOD ERROR THAT COST THE MOST, user-caught twice: I POOLED 61 RUNS ACROSS WEEKS OF DIFFERENT BUILDS** and concluded the branch runs were unremarkable (21/23 at the 65th percentile). Restricted to the runs since 2026-08-27, where the build is near-constant, controls are **7/7 perfect** and the effect is obvious. **This is [[feedback-dont-evaluate-a-build-with-older-runs]], which I had quoted at the user an hour earlier and then violated.** A wide window does not add power — it adds variance from builds that are not the one under test.
- **⚠ THE SECOND ERROR, and the one worth generalising: "the changed code provably never executed, therefore it cannot matter."** Two `T3-BZ` runs had **zero moves** and scored **5/23 and 23/23** — the full outcome range from identical executed code. **"No mechanism I can find" is not "no mechanism exists"**, and I let the first stand in for the second for several hours while the correlation strengthened.
- **SIX CANDIDATE MECHANISMS ELIMINATED BY TEST, not by argument:** the relocation itself (the 5/23 run moved nothing) · the analyzer refactor (**byte-identical facts on all 20 archived trees**, main's class compiled separately and A/B'd) · silent hook exceptions (zero catch-block warnings in any run) · starting-state asymmetry (both arms clean dirs + reset the goal stack at startup) · machine load (LLM latency 7.43 s vs 7.78 s, cycle times 77 s vs 76 s) · resource pressure (**zero** LLM timeouts and zero OOM/GC errors in any arm; the circuit breaker fired only on controls, which all scored 23/23).
- **✅ MECHANISM FOUND, FIXED, AND THE FIX FIRED IN THE FIELD — the mover repaired the SOURCE TREE and left the GOAL TREE asserting the vacated path.** Run `2026-08-31_15-02-34` kept rendering the banked criterion *"A Java class `Book` exists at `src/main/java/com/example/library/domain/Book.java`"* after the move that falsified it, so the loop was steered by, and checked against, a statement its own repair had invalidated.
  - **The grouping that revealed it was ACTED vs NOT ACTED, not code-present vs code-absent.** Mover acted: 21, 6, 15 (**0/3 perfect**). Mover did not act (incl. 7 controls, `T2-FH`-only, no-hook, and two zero-move `T3-BZ` runs): **10/11 perfect**, p=0.011. Merely HAVING the code is harmless — which is why the no-hook arm and a zero-move run both scored 23/23, and why every mechanism I tested at the I/O and state level came back clean.
  - **✅ FIXED + FIELD-CONFIRMED.** `RequirementPhrasingNormalizer.rewriteRelocatedReferences` (path + derived FQN, boundary-guarded so `a.b.Book` never eats `a.b.BookRepository`) · `GoalPlannerService.rewriteRelocatedFileReferences` walks EVERY goal (criteria banked on COMPLETED goals are still read at the closure edge) · called from the `MergeAgent` hook after a successful move. **12 tests, 6 of them RED against a deliberate stub before the fix**; suite **2768/0/5**. Field run `2026-08-31_19-50-35`: move + *"re-pointed 1 goal(s)"*, **0** references to the vacated path left in the persisted GoalStack, and **`SMOKE: 23/23` — the first perfect score from any run in which the mover acted.**
  - **⚠ THAT CONFIRMATION IS n=1** (p=0.25 against the pre-fix moved group on its own). What it establishes is that the fix FIRES and cleans the goal tree; that it RESTORES the outcome needs one or two more runs in which the mover acts.
  - **🔴 THE EFFECT WEAKENED AS DATA ACCUMULATED, AND THE HONEST READING IS NOW 'MARGINAL, POSSIBLY NOTHING'.** p for moved-vs-not-moved went **0.005 -> 0.011 -> 0.022** as the no-move population grew; every additional run moved it the wrong way, which is the signature of a false positive being diluted rather than a real effect being confirmed. **TWO runs now score 5/23 with NO move at all** (`2026-08-31_05-47-22` full branch, `2026-08-31_23-26-24` T3-BZ+fix), and **both carry the same `inference variable T has incompatible bounds` grind (`T2-FF`)** — catastrophic runs happen on any build for reasons neither capability touches. Post-fix moved runs are 2/2 perfect (p=0.10), which is consistent with the fix helping AND with there never having been much to fix.
- **🔴 THE VERIFICATION WAS RUN ON A BUILD NOBODY WOULD MERGE — user-caught, and it invalidates the framing rather than a number.** Bisecting to isolate blame was right; then I kept verifying the fix on the **`T3-BZ`-only** arm because it matched the damaged baseline. **The mergeable artifact (main + `T3-BZ` + `T2-FH` + the fix) had ZERO runs**, and **none of the four `GOAL_FULFILLED` runs carried both capabilities**. An arm that isolates a variable is not the thing that ships; a verification series has to run what would merge.
- **⚠ `pkill -f <pattern>` SELF-KILL, FOURTH VARIANT, COMMITTED BY ME HOURS AFTER WRITING THE RUNBOOK ENTRY ABOUT IT.** `pkill -f verify_series.sh` matched my own command line and killed the shell (exit 144). The rule is in [[project-run-lifecycle-runbook]] and I still typed it. **Kill by PID; to inspect, split the literal (`verify_""series`) so the checking command cannot match itself.**
- **⚠ THE REPLAY THAT SHIPPED THE CAPABILITY COULD NOT HAVE CAUGHT THIS — a lesson bigger than the ticket.** Nine-tree replay asserts properties of the TREE; the damage was in the goal tree. **Any repair that writes to the workspace needs a BOOKKEEPING assertion, not only a "still compiles / still boots" one.**
  - **Path-keyed surfaces audited:** criteria+descriptions (was stale, fixed) · evaluation score-history-by-file (already self-healing via `T1-FS`, fails open) · mutation snapshots (read/rejection-marking, not a resurrection path; no recurrence observed in 3 field moves) · `FixAttemptLedger` (keyed by rule+token). **`T4-BL`'s `PackageConsolidationService` redirects a proposed WRITE rather than moving an existing file, so banked criteria stay true — reasoned, not tested; worth a ticket.**
- **📋 SUPERSEDED: the mechanism was open at the time of writing.** The only untested `T3-BZ` path is the merge hook's per-merge whole-tree read + JavaParser parse, which is the only code that runs when no move occurs. A **no-hook arm** (the full commit with the single call site commented out) is running as `2026-08-31_17-29-13`. 23/23 means the tree read is the culprit and the fix is narrow; below 23 means the only remaining difference from control is provably-identical analyzer output plus two unreferenced classes, which would be strange enough to re-examine the control baseline itself.
- **✅ `T2-FH`'S TRIGGER WAS OBSERVED IN THE FIELD FOR THE FIRST TIME — on the arm that could not act on it.** Run `2026-08-31_13-23-46` (T3-BZ-only, so the OLD brake) halted on `root-convergence-stall` at **53 of 90 minutes**, and its 20-cycle window held **exactly one merge, in the final cycle** — the merge that triggered the mover 27 s earlier. `T2-FH` would have extended (budget allowed ~22 min for another window against 37 remaining). **It is also the marginal case: one merge in twenty cycles is the bare minimum the rule accepts, i.e. the toothlessness risk the ticket names.**
- **⚠ TWO HARNESS DEFECTS FOUND WHILE MEASURING.** (1) **`scripts/run_outcomes.json` was CORRUPT** — a stray `R ` at line 2178 made the durable corpus unparseable; introduced by tonight's runs, absent from the committed version, and every corpus analysis (`T6-L`, `T6-M`) would have failed or silently skipped. Repaired, 109 rows parse. (2) **preflight records no git sha in the run dir**, so a run cannot be attributed to a build — the precondition for exactly this kind of comparison. Both worth tickets.

- **THE CHAIN, read from run `2026-08-30_00-06-00`'s own log instead of from another run.** The criteria gate refused closure correctly at 00:15:12 and injected a work item carrying the four unmet requirements. At 00:16:00 a merge compiled and the COMPILE_OK cleanup retired that item as SUBSUMED, 48 seconds later, with none of its work done. At 00:16:17 nothing ACTIVE remained, the ledger read clear, and the root completed at 17/19.
- **ONE MISLABEL, THREE WRONG DECISIONS.** The T4-BP-3 item wears `[BUILD_FIX]` only because the five closure-edge injection templates share a description prefix. `T1-DR` retired it; the closure ledger skipped its criteria as build-status facts; the grounding stamp marked them AUTHORED. **It is the one gate whose criteria ARE the specification's own words.**
- **✅ FIXED — all three sites now discriminate on the ORIGINATING GATE, not the tag** (`BlockerTypes.GATE_CRITERIA`; COMPILE_OK retires only unstamped plain compile fixes). **This generalises `T2-EQ`, which wrote the same carve-out for the package gate alone** — boot- and test-gate items were being retired by an observation that says nothing about them too. `scenario/CriteriaWorkItemDissolvedByCompileReplayTest` replays the archived chain, **verified to fail on the pre-fix predicate** (item SUBSUMED, root COMPLETED) and pass after. Suite **2727/0/5**. **Uncommitted — no commit authorization given.**
- **✅ FIELD-CHECKED IN TWO RUNS ON THE FIXED BUILD, AND NOTHING REGRESSED.** `2026-08-30_18-08-10` (clitarget) `GOAL_FULFILLED smoke=19/19` — the criteria gate blocked 4 times, unconfirmed count fell 4 -> 10 -> 3 -> 3, **0 `T1-DR` retirements**, root closed only after the gate stopped blocking. `2026-08-30_18-44-37` (firestarter) `honest-halt:root-convergence-stall smoke=boot-failed`, **`closure_attempts=0` so the changed code had no opportunity**; matches pre-change run `2026-08-28_13-58-46` on every compared field, and `T1-DR` retirements were **0 in all four firestarter runs including two on the OLD build** — narrowing that retirement removed nothing this target was using.
- **⚠ DO NOT QUOTE 19/19 AS THE EVIDENCE** — `2026-08-29_23-08-29` scored 19/19 on the previous build. The attributable observation is narrow: 0 compile-triggered retirements where the failure had 1, and 4 surviving work items where it had 0.
- **⚠ TWO INSTRUMENT NOTES.** The `t4dk-closure-by-dissolution` watch reported `COUNT(1)` on the GOOD run — it matches any ledger line mentioning subsumptions, and clitarget's 7 were all `REPLACED / superseded by replanning`, a different mechanism. And an earlier clitarget attempt (`01-05-42`) is **void**: the box OOM'd, the kernel killed Docker Desktop and Neo4j with it, the run wrote 5.9 GB of stack traces and scored `deadline 17/19` having never reached a closure edge. **Restart the Docker Desktop `neo4j` container, not the system-daemon one — the latter binds 7687 to a different host port and a different volume.**
- **🔴 THE DECISIVE LINK IS STILL OPEN, AND THE FIX DOES NOT CLOSE IT.** At 00:16:16 the completion verifier re-ran over 17 requirements (including the seed clauses `T4-DK`'s top-up adds) and answered **✓ to all 17**, the failing pair included. What the fix buys is that the run cannot CLOSE while the work item stands — honest halt or real repair instead of false success.
- **THE ARTIFACT DEFECT WAS ONE LINE, WHICH IS EXACTLY WHY A SOURCE-READING VERIFIER PASSED IT.** `MultiLineRowReader.readAll` re-tests `countQuotes(line)` without reassigning `line`, so one odd-quote line swallows the rest of the file into a single row. Wiring above it is correct. **The architecture reads right; only execution sees the loop variable.**
- **🔴 AND NOTHING EXECUTED HERE: `criteria_executable_pct=0` (31 authored, 0 probes).** `BehaviouralClosureVerifier` admits only criteria the HTTP parser can turn into a request, so every CLI closure logs *"none can be executed"*. Stdout-content criteria need an INPUT FIXTURE and nothing synthesises one — **`T4-DD` probe synthesis is the honest fix, and it is the next thing worth building on this line.**

- **🔴 THE LIVE DEFECT, reproduced on the CURRENT build: a false completion with NO subsumption involved.** Run `2026-08-30_00-06-00` (clitarget) closed **`GOAL_FULFILLED` at 17/19**, failing checks **6,7** — the multi-line quoted-field pair the seed states twice — with the criterion present in the tree (22 renders), **0 folds, 0 reopens**. Not a regression from this session: checks 6/7 are unrelated to what was changed, and the identical failing SET closed at 17/19 on `2026-08-26_15-11-51` beforehand. **This is `T4-DK`'s core, and it is where the next session should start.**
- **TWO PATHS TO A FALSE COMPLETION ARE NOW DISTINGUISHED, and I had been treating them as one.** (1) *fold-then-drop* — a correct subgoal folded into a COMPLETED goal, criteria dropped; **fixed, still unproven** (0 folds in 3 runs). (2) *no fold at all* — **live, reproduced today**. Everything built this session addresses (1); the one that fires is (2).
- **✅ FIELD-CONFIRMED (the only one): the command-arity fix.** `CommandExerciseSynthesizer` substituted a DIFFERENT command when the criterion's named command had the wrong arity (`column` arity 2 → filter dropped it → fallback picked `check`). **13 closure blocks → 0** on the post-fix run, no new blocks. Same invariant as `T1-FW` but on a target with **no network surface**, which is evidence `T1-FW` should be re-derived as the general rule, not patched as HTTP.
- **⚠ THREE MECHANISMS REMAIN FIELD-UNPROVEN — `T4-DO`, `T1-FV` retry, `T4-DK` carry-criteria — all for one structural reason: each repairs a FAILURE path, and verification needs a run that fails that way.** **Decision: replay, not live runs** — the standing rule already says corpus replay over small-N live batches, and I spent much of this session arguing past it.
- **⚠ THE REPLAY TOOL IS NOT `FrozenCycleObserver`** — read it: signal-layer only (deliberation mode, fitness ranking, eligibility), agent-emitted state explicitly held constant. **The right precedent is `PlannerAmputationReplayTest`** (+ `Cycle1779492075276ReplayTest`): bespoke scenario tests replaying one archived failure, and it already asserts *a GIVEN_UP goal returning to ACTIVE* — the exact shape the reopen chain needs. **Next step: one scenario test in that style.**
- **`T4-DK` flipped `[x]` → `[~]`** so its appended findings are reachable from the live queue. **`M-E` filed** (meta axis): `find_warnings` is one-directional, so a `[x]` ticket admitting its own open half is invisible forever; a grep found 17 candidates but mixes genuine residuals with references to OTHER tickets — **audit body-by-body, do not quote 17**.
- **A charset guard now blocks stray characters at write time** (`scripts/charset_guard.py`, PostToolUse hook). Blocklist by inventory — em dashes/box-drawing/arrows are legitimate style; `jq` is NOT installed, hooks use python3.
- **⚠ THE METHOD LESSON OF THE NIGHT: four of six diagnoses needed correcting, and every correction came from reading code or checking provenance, never from another run.** Pooled builds, pooled yardsticks, a target whose own file calls its score meaningless, a pre-fix run, an unread mechanism, and a defect-grouping that committed the error it named ([[feedback-dont-group-defects-by-vocabulary]]). **Check what a number is OF before it means anything.**

## 2026-08-28 (LATEST — T4-D QUEUE FINISHED, AND SELF-VERIFICATION FOUND 6 DEFECTS IN THE SAME SESSION'S WORK)

- **🔴 RUN `2026-08-28_21-47-39` RESOLVED — `terminal=deadline`, `smoke=23/23` AND `smoke_preserved=23/23` (both `_strict`, `discounted=none`), 64 cycles, 10 merges, 74 min. A 23/23 ARTIFACT THAT WAS REFUSED CLOSURE ON A FALSE CONTRADICTION.** `closure_attempts=1`, and that single attempt was blocked by exactly ONE criterion (`probed=35 skipped=24 contradicted=1`).
  - **PREDICTION 2 HELD:** the closure gate received only confirmable requirements — the wall was a well-formed criterion, NOT an unparsed fragment. The 08-28 extractor fixes are field-confirmed. (Prediction 1 = correct abstention, prediction 3 = confirmed live at 17/0 → 5/9; both already recorded.)
  - **⚠ BUT THE REFUSAL ITSELF IS FALSE, AND `T1-FV` IS NOW PROVEN IN THE FIELD AS A CLOSURE BLOCKER — not "rare" as previously recorded.** Blocked criterion: *"POST /loans with a non-existent bookId or memberId returns HTTP 404 with JSON body {"message": "<reason>"}"*. Booted the artifact and sent both bodies:
    - probe's body `{"bookId":999999999,"memberId":999999999,"returned":true}` → **400**
    - criterion's body `{"bookId":999999999,"memberId":999999999}` → **404 `{"message":"Book not found"}`** — exactly what the criterion demands, message field included.
    - **The artifact is CORRECT. The probe manufactured the failure.**
  - **MECHANISM PINNED, and it is narrower than the filed ticket assumes.** `RequestBodySynthesizer.findModelSource` resolves path segment `loans` → the ENTITY `Loan.java`, so the body carries every entity field — including `returned` (boolean). The handler is `@RequestBody Map<String, Long>`, so a boolean cannot deserialize into `Long` → 400. **It is a TYPE mismatch on an invented field, not unknown-property rejection.**
  - **🔴 THE ACTUAL DEFECT IS A ONE-CONDITION SCOPE ERROR IN AN EXISTING GUARD, NOT A MISSING SUBSYSTEM.** `BootGate.java:800-806` already abstains when a derived body is refused — but the guard reads `assertion.expectedStatus() < 300`, so it protects SUCCESS criteria only. Its own comment (*"no derivation can be sure it satisfies validation it never discovered"*) applies identically to an ERROR-status criterion, whose body comes from the same synthesizer. A 404 criterion answered 400 falls straight through to `failures`. **Same invariant, one home, half the coverage** ([[feedback-one-invariant-one-home]]).
  - **⚠ DO NOT WIDEN THE GUARD UNCONDITIONALLY** — that would discard genuine wrong-error-code findings (404-expected/400-observed is sometimes real). The discriminator that worked by hand is domain-free and free to run: **re-send with only the fields the criterion text names; if the answer becomes the expected status, the body was the problem.** Here the criterion names `bookId` and `memberId`; `returned` is invented. This is NOT the handler-parameter reader the 2026-08-26 correction rejected.

Deliberately not the blanket widening, which would discard genuine wrong-error-code findings. **⚠ NOT FIELD-VERIFIED through BootGate's own path** — same gap that left T4-DO unreachable; the next firestarter run is the check. DoD unmet: the first request still carries every entity-derived component, which still wants the interface reader the 2026-08-26 correction put out of scope. Suite **2720/0/5**.

## 2026-08-28 (Opus 5, user-directed) — T4-DF BUILT AND PROVEN; THE DISAMBIGUATION RULE IS STRUCTURAL, AND A GREEN SUITE HID A DEAD RESOLVER

- **✅ `T4-DF` CLOSED — member-level classpath discovery exists, with an actuator.** `ClasspathMemberDeclarationService` (capability) + `MisplacedMemberResolver` (`@Order(20)`, rewrites the call and adds the import), a package view on `JdkPlatformTypeIndex`, and a receiver FQN + bare-type flag on `MissingMemberExtractor.Entry`. **Suite 2641/0/5.** Uncommitted.
- **🔴 THE ROUTING WAS EMPTY, NOT WEAK.** `SymbolImportResolver` declines member-shaped tokens outright (`!Character.isUpperCase` → empty, T4-BM step 6b), so no deterministic resolver ever fired on a missing method and it fell to the LLM build-fix path — whose only move is adding a dependency. [[project-capability-outruns-routing]]: trace to the link that does not fire, before writing code.
- **🔴 THE DISAMBIGUATION RULE IS THE FINDING, and the ticket did not anticipate it.** A package has several claimants — `glBindVertexArray` is declared by `ARBVertexArrayObject`, `GL30` and `GL30C` — so abstain-on-ambiguity would have killed every GL case. The separator is **the receiver being a SUPERTYPE of the declaring type** (`GL20 extends GL15 extends … extends GL11`): the code addressed a base and the member lives further down its own hierarchy. Domain-free, and it resolves every ambiguous corpus case to exactly one answer. **Prefer a structural relation over a naming one whenever both would fit** — a "shares the `GL` prefix" tie-break would have worked on this corpus and meant nothing.
- **🔴 12 GREEN UNIT TESTS, AND THE RESOLVER PROPOSED NOTHING END-TO-END.** Driving the real production classes over real `javac` stderr returned **NO PROPOSAL**: `javac` names an IMPORTED type by simple name (`location: class GL11`) while the Maven field log carried the FQN, and **both shapes occur in the same run's logs**. My fixtures had only the second, so the code read a null package and abstained silently. Fixed by recovering the package from the project's own imports; 3 regression tests. Folded into [[feedback-verify-diff-is-substantive]].
- **THE SUBSTANTIVE PROOF:** the run's own converged `WorldObject.java`, with the field's misplacement reintroduced, goes **5 errors → 0**, and the rewritten source is **byte-identical to what the run itself reached after ~20 further cycles**. The service proposes the answer the loop spent the rest of its life finding.
- **⚠ A FILED SUB-DEFECT WAS A MISREADING, AND BUILDING IT WOULD HAVE BROKEN THE POM.** "The same dependency is added 4x" — those are the main artifact plus three `<classifier>natives-{linux,macos,windows}</classifier>` rows, four legitimately distinct dependencies. The count came from grepping `<artifactId>` without classifier awareness ([[feedback-count-events-not-log-lines]]). `PomDependencyEditor.containsDependency` keys on groupId+artifactId only, so the proposed "already declared?" check would have **suppressed the three correct natives rows**. Retracted in the ticket. **Read the artifact before building a fix filed from a grep.**
- **⚠ NOT FIELD-VERIFIED — the next run is the DoD's last clause.** `MissingMemberExtractor.Entry` gained fields that flow into `BlockerExtractor`, so [[feedback-short-field-run-after-context-change]] applies. Watch for `misplaced-member:<member>` at INFO.
- **⚠ `roadmap-overview.md` IS STALE** — a 2026-08-02 snapshot that never listed `T4-DF`/`T4-DG`/`T2-FG`. `roadmap.md` is authoritative and its bookkeeping is clean (0 warnings, 469 headers). Regenerate the index before trusting it for triage.

Silence is the specified answer. **The firing path is still unproven in the field**; this run never reached the vertex-array code that produced the earlier run's 12 cases.

"Consulted and correctly abstained" was therefore indistinguishable from "never reached", and I had to re-derive the answer offline by replaying the archived stderr through the real classes. **Fixed same session — all abstention paths now log at INFO.** I broke the standing marker rule inside the very scope that cites it; check it against the code, not the intention.

## 2026-08-25 (Opus 5, autonomous) — THE CLOSURE GATE IS THREE-QUARTERS FALSE, AND AN A/B SERIES IS IN FLIGHT

- **✅ `T6-Q` ANSWERED: NO MEASUREMENTS THAT 2026-08-24's WORK REGRESSED THE LOOP.** Paired A/B, arms `8c375af` (A, pre-today) vs `9cef43a` (B, today), N and rule fixed BEFORE the runs. **Pairs: +4, 0, 0, +1** — 2 of 4 favouring A, mean **+1.25**, against a rule needing ≥3 of 4 and mean ≥8. After pair 3 the first condition was already unreachable, so the result was determined early and pair 4 confirmed it. **Arm means A 22.75 · B 21.50; across all 8 runs mean 22.12, range 19–23, 5 of 8 at 23/23.**
  - **Pair 1's 19/23 was the outlier, not the signal.** Arm B then scored 23/23 three times. A single pair could not have told us this; the pre-registered rule is what made it decidable rather than arguable.
  - **⚠ THE NULL IS NOT SAFETY** — at sd ≈ 31 this screened for a LARGE regression only. Written down before the runs precisely so it could not be dropped once convenient.
  - **The incidental finding is bigger than the result: 5 of 6 arm runs scored 23/23**, both arms halting identically (30–50 merges). **The loop produces correct artifacts consistently and is told it has not.**

- **🔴 THE NON-REST TARGET RAN, AND IT REWRITES `T4-DD` (2026-08-25).** Built `clitarget/` (a delimited-text CLI: no network surface, argv in, exit status out) + `scripts/smoke_cli.sh`, calibrated both ways first (12/12 reference, 10/12 with two injected defects). The harness needed no change — `T4-CK` had already added `PREFLIGHT_TARGET`/`PREFLIGHT_SMOKE`.
  - **THE LOOP BUILT A FULLY CORRECT CLI TOOL: 12/12 at merge 12, still 12/12 at merge 37**, three source files, on a target kind it has never seen. Planner decomposition, criteria authoring (*"exits 5"*), evolution, merge, compile gates and the T3-BY preserve machinery ALL transferred.
  - **The design note's premise was wrong in an instructive way.** It ranked `GoalPlannerService` (66 HTTP hits) as the sharpest signal; hit counts measure MENTIONS. Measured against a real non-REST target, the reasoning transferred and **three things leaked, in increasing depth: (1) hardcoded strings** (`"[BUILD_FIX] Make the packaged application start and serve the specified endpoint"`, injected on a target with no network surface and demanded at closure 41 times); **(2) a missing outcome abstraction** — `verb + path + status` cannot express `exits 5`, so every criterion abstained; **(3) AN ASSUMED LIVENESS MODEL** — "starts and keeps running" vs "runs and terminates", which is in what the gate ASKS and cannot be fixed by moving code.
  - **Item 3 costs the RUN, not just accuracy: 25 of 37 merges (68%) were churn on a finished artefact.** The loop completed the target in twelve minutes and was denied closure for forty-five more. **Fix item 3 first.**
  - **⚠ AND THE HARNESS CARRIES IT TOO:** the run scored `smoke=boot-failed` while the artefact measured 12/12 by hand — `build_boot_smoke` waits for a `Started` banner and never invokes the yardstick, which it would have called with a URL. **A non-REST target therefore records a FALSE ZERO in the ledger regardless of correctness.**
  - **⚠ THE LOOP SUCCEEDED WHILE FLYING BLIND** — its verifier could execute no criteria, so nothing checked the work in-loop. Encouraging about the loop, not about the verification chain. **Next experiment: a harder CLI specification where the obvious implementation is wrong**, which should separate them. One target, one run, one seed.
- **🔴 THE MAIN RESULT: three INDEPENDENT probe defects make correct artifacts look broken, and the closure gate is three-quarters noise.** Decomposed on run `2026-08-24_22-14-05`, every result tested against the booted artifact:
  - **`T1-FW` — the probe DROPS THE QUERY STRING.** A criterion about `GET /loans?memberId={id}` is tested by requesting bare `GET /loans`; the artifact correctly refuses a missing required parameter with 400. **Present in 10 of 14 archived runs, 27 distinct contradictions.** `normalizePath` strips the query on purpose — right for endpoint IDENTITY (5 callers), wrong as a REQUEST builder (1 caller). **⚠ The obvious fix breaks `isUsable`** (it compares `pathMatches(assertion.path(), last.path())`); carry the query in a SECOND field, leave `path()` alone.
  - **`T1-FU` — an over-budget sequence is TRUNCATED and then judged.** The model answers the bounded-count criterion CORRECTLY in nine requests (4 different books, 1 member, 4 loans); `parse()` kept the first six, dropping the loans that establish the precondition. **34 of 55 syntheses were over budget.** **Fix SHIPPED and suite-verified (2558/0/5)**: over budget now abstains. **⚠ The existing test asserted the truncation as INTENDED, so every green suite protected the defect.**
  - **`T1-FV` — the request body is derived from the ENTITY**, so it carries `createdAt`/`returnedAt` the endpoint rejects (any ONE field is enough). Real but RARE: 5 of 145 contradictions, and it does NOT reproduce on the baseline artifact.
  - **The single sharpest illustration:** the baseline run scored a perfect **23/23** and was still refused closure **33 times**, all naming the 422 criterion. A flawless artifact denied `GOAL_FULFILLED` by a false contradiction.
- **`scripts/probe_truth.sh <jar>` IS THE INSTRUMENT FOR ALL THREE** — boots an artifact and sends the PROBE's request beside the CRITERION's. Run it before AND after any fix; a fix that moves its own row and leaves the others alone is the measurements. **A green suite is not** (see `T1-FU`).
- **⚠ EVERY WRONG CLAIM I MADE TONIGHT CAME FROM MINING LOGS WITH REGEXES; EVERY CLAIM FROM BOOTING THE ARTIFACT HELD.** A non-greedy match hid multi-criterion closure blocks and made three defects look like one; a quantifier undercounted `T1-FW` by 5×; two mechanism guesses came from interleaved probe lines. All produced PLAUSIBLE numbers. **Prefer the booted artifact over log mining wherever both are possible.**
- **`T6-P` — the YARDSTICK was sending malformed JSON** to any artifact choosing UUID ids (unquoted id interpolation), worth **7 checks** on one run (13/23 → 20/23, proven by booting). Fixed so numeric ids emit bare exactly as before; the 23/23 numeric artifact re-scores 23/23.
- **Closed on measurements:** `T3-BY` (7/7 loop-preserved), `T4-CY` (third leg live twice), `T4-DB`, `T2-FD`, `T2-EX` (NEGATIVE call — 23/23 reached at 51% executable criteria), `T5-BJ` (6,057 → **97** DbHits, 62×). **Built:** `T3-CA` (calibration cut two false positives; exactly ONE firing across 12 real trees), `T1-FT`, `T6-N`'s prefix window, `T3-BW`/`T3-BZ` markers (T3-BZ then fired **38×** on a real anomaly — first field results).
- **⚠ SIXTH `pgrep -f` VARIANT, AND IT DEADLOCKED THE EXPERIMENT.** A waiter whose own command line contained its pattern matched itself forever, and the series driver matched THAT shell. **A liveness predicate must never be a string the checking command contains** — match by pid. See [[project-run-lifecycle-runbook]].


## 2026-08-23 (Opus 5, user-directed) — THE RUN'S TAIL SUBTRACTS, PROMPT GROWTH IS ALL ACCUMULATOR, AND I MEASURED THE WRONG POPULATION TWICE

- **Ending at the cap is the most common single outcome, but it is NOT the universal one — and I got this wrong twice before checking the right population.** Over the **74-run T6-E ledger** (`scripts/run_outcomes.json`, the durable record): `deadline` **41 (55%)** · `GOAL_FULFILLED` **11 (15%)** · `jvm-died` 9 · `honest-halt:*` 13 (root-convergence-stall 6, llm-circuit-breaker 4, empty-backlog-injection-limit 2, bare 1). So the cap is the plurality terminal state and worth attacking, but fulfilment is a real recurring outcome — consistent with [[project-goal-fulfilled-is-real-happy-path]], which already said so.
  - **⚠ I FIRST CLAIMED "5 of 6 runs deadline-cut, 0 fulfilled", THEN "6 of 7".** Both were measured over `/tmp/preflight_runs/`, which only holds the last handful of runs because /tmp gets cleared — a 7-run recency window, not the population. **`scripts/run_outcomes.json` is the population; /tmp is a cache.** Second population error of the same night on the same block ([[feedback-verify-population-before-claim]]). What the recent window does show honestly is a worse-than-baseline stretch: 0 fulfilled in the last 7, against a 15% base rate.
- **⚠ THE T6-E LEDGER HAS A FIELD DEFECT, AND IT MATTERS BECAUSE T6-L/T6-M CONSUME IT.** `smoke_failed_checks` is populated from the **PRESERVED** tree while `smoke`/`smoke_pass` come from the **FINAL** tree. Verified on all 3 of 18 rows where the two trees' failure COUNTS differ, and hidden in the other 15 because the counts happened to agree: `2026-08-23_02-12-34` says 18/23 (5 failures) but lists 2 — `{1,8}`, exactly the preserved tree's 21/23 · `2026-08-23_01-15-20` says 18/23 but lists 9, exactly its preserved 14/23 · `2026-08-04_08-29-58` says **23/23, zero failures, and still lists 4**, exactly its preserved 19/23. **Any failure-family classification keyed on `smoke_failed_checks` is attributing the preserved tree's failures to the final tree's score.** Worth a ticket before T6-M's classification is trusted.
- **⚠ THE RUN DECELERATES — MODESTLY — AND IT IS NOT THE REPAIR LOOP.** Measured over the FULL run `2026-08-23_02-12-34` (74 completed cycles): first third **58 s/cycle**, middle **79 s**, last third **74 s** — a ~1.3x slowdown that PLATEAUS and partly recovers, not a compounding one. Mean LLM call latency by 16-cycle band has the same shape: 3.54 → 4.59 → 5.47 → 5.51 → 4.47 s. **The load-bearing claim: LLM calls per cycle stay FLAT throughout (12.1 / 10.1 / 11.4 / 9.9 / 13.5).** A repair loop would add calls; none appears. What changes is per-call cost, not call count — so the previous session's `Book` placement repair loop is not the throughput story (that finding stands on its own compile-failure measurements).
  - **⚠ CORRECTED FROM MY OWN MID-RUN MEASUREMENT, SAME NIGHT.** At t+64 min I reported 58 → 97 s/cycle and a 3.09 → 6.02 s latency doubling, and wrote it here. Both were artifacts of measuring a partial run whose then-final third was its slowest patch; the run recovered afterwards. **Do not measure a run's trend before the run ends, and use segment means, not first-vs-last samples, on a volatile series.** [[feedback-verify-population-before-claim]]
- **Prompt growth is ENTIRELY accumulator-driven — the legitimate content blocks SHRINK.** 70% of wall time sits inside `LLMClient` (2577 s of 3699 s attributed over 548 calls). Evaluation prompt across 50 renders, segment MEANS: first third 3810 → last third **4686 tokens = 1.23x** (the series is volatile — min 1369, max 9127 — which is exactly what made the endpoint comparison misleading). Per-block means, first third → last third: SCORE HISTORY BY FILE 210→**544** · PROJECT STATE KEYS 143→**301** · RECENT SCORE DELTAS 119→**270** are the only monotonic growers. Meanwhile CURRENT FILE CONTENT 1321→**869** and STRUCTURAL DIFF SUMMARY 587→**263** both SHRINK, and ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA is flat at ~147. The model is `llama-server -c 32768` local at ~134% CPU, so prefill cost tracks prompt length.
- **This is the quantified argument for T4-CL** (per-run-disposable-assumptions audit, ranked #3 in the 2026-08-23 reorder on the grounds that T1-FS was found by accident "from you reading a prompt", which is not a search strategy). Three accumulating blocks — score history + recent deltas + project state keys — are **1115 tokens, 24% of the last-third evaluation prompt**, they are the ONLY blocks that grow, and they are all the same class T1-FS was one instance of.
- **⚠ Two limits on that argument.** Only the EVALUATION prompt was measured — 50 renders of ~548 calls, one of five agents; the other four are unmeasured and could be better or worse. And the prize is bounded: the whole prompt grows only 1.23x across a run, so reclaiming the accumulators buys back part of a modest deceleration, not a converging run. Stays two-factor with env/model slowness and run variance; see [[feedback-calibrate-not-flip]]. (An earlier version of this bullet cautioned that CURRENT FILE CONTENT was the largest grower and its growth legitimate — that was the same endpoint artifact; measured on means it shrinks.)
- **⚠ T1-FS'S CLOSURE FIGURE WAS MEASURED 3 MINUTES INTO A RUN AND DOES NOT HOLD ACROSS ONE.** `9a3461d` claims "2 paths, 0 that do not exist" — that was the run's FIRST evaluation prompt, when one file existed on disk. Across 36 rendered blocks it settles at **19 paths / 2 phantom**, plateauing from block 15 onward. **The 97% reduction claim HOLDS and is the real result**: 269 paths/17,205 tokens → 19/525, and path count is now bounded by the project's file set rather than by run length. The phantom count does not.
  - **The phantom mechanism is still live, at 2 paths instead of 259.** `BusinessRuleViolationException.java` and `EntityNotFoundException.java` were staged to `mutations/cycle-1787444599094/` and scored 0.8 each; that cycle was REJECTED (compile probe failure, `Merged: false`) and neither file ever reached `output/`. Per-file scoring runs at evaluation time, BEFORE the accept/reject decision, and `ContextLineageTracker:69` carries `scoreHistoryByFile` forward unconditionally — so a rejected mutation's files enter score history permanently.
  - Residual growth vector, noted not filed: path count is bounded, the per-file score ARRAYS are not (`LoanController.java` at 14 entries; block tokens creep 457→525 over 21 blocks). Harmless at 90 min, compounds over a long run.
- All figures above are FULL-RUN (76 cycles, 50 evaluation prompts). The run was left to reach its 90-min cap rather than cut short, so it stays length-comparable with the other six for T6-L's post-T1-FS baseline — and that turned out to matter for more than comparability: the mid-run numbers I first recorded were wrong.
- **FINAL STATE of `2026-08-23_02-12-34`** — `PREFLIGHT: PASS mode=live terminal=deadline`, 90.6 min (02:13:13-03:43:52), **76 cycles / 43 merges / 8 preserves / 0 health failures / 0 terminal markers**, 0 watches needing attention. `smoke=18/23` · `smoke_preserved=21/23` · both `_strict` identical, `discounted=none` · `preserved_source=loop-preserved`. Archived to `~/.autobuilder/run-archive/2026-08-23_02-12-34`.
- **⚠ THE FINAL ARTIFACT IS A STRICT REGRESSION ON THE TREE THE LOOP ITSELF PRESERVED — and the failing sets are NESTED, so this is readable** ([[project-smoke-score-is-chained]] says compare SETS, never totals). Preserved fails **{1, 8}**; final fails **{1, 8, 13, 17, 18}** — a strict superset. **All five failures are ONE defect**: a POST that creates a resource returns **200 instead of 201**. The preserved tree had all three `POST /loans` returning 201 correctly; the final tree broke them. Preserve was taken at `cycle-1787448836103` (03:35 CEST, `COMPILE_OK`, verified, 68 snapshots considered); the final tree is 8 minutes younger and worse. **The last stretch of the run had NEGATIVE yield, and T4-CA's preserve is what saved the better artifact.** This is the concrete answer to "does it matter that runs end at the cap": the tail of a run is not merely slow, it can subtract.
- **THREE TICKETS WORKED THE SAME NIGHT (suite 2521/0/5, nothing committed).**
  - **`T4-DB` — the create probe, and the ingredient that made it honest.** The 201 abstention was never a missing SHAPE; it was one missing ingredient, a body the application will accept. `FieldConstraintDiscovery` reads the artifact's own hand-written validation (`length() > N`, a `Pattern.compile` constant resolved to the identifier it is matched against, inline `matches("…")`); `RegexSampleGenerator` samples a narrow subset and **returns empty for alternation, negated classes and back-references** rather than approximate; `BootGate.Unobserved.BODY_REFUSED` makes a 4xx to the ASSERTED request an abstention, so a strict correct artifact can never be scored a defect. **Proven on the real failing artifact by booting its own jar: the OLD placeholder body → 400 refused (hence silent), the NEW one → 200 accepted — and 200 against a criterion demanding 201 is exactly what all five failing smoke checks report.** Scope stated: creates on a non-parameterized path; `POST /loans` correctly abstains because its references resolve to nothing on a fresh boot.
  - **`T3-BZ` slice 1 — the placement fact, wired, with a calibrated watch.** `peerPlacementAnomalies` derives the peer group from the project (same-package peers that name exactly one project type nominate it; the nominees' packages are compared), reuses `PEER_GROUP_MIN_OTHERS = 2`, and is silent on a tie, a non-strict majority, or fewer than three peers. On the real artifact it reports **exactly one** anomaly and it is `Book`. A test asserts the message contains no `entity`/`should`/`convention`. **The relocation decision is now written down as the ticket demanded: slice 1 does NOT move the class, and slice 2 is gated on the watch** — fact firing all run with no relocation justifies the mover; a relocation after being told means slice 1 sufficed.
  - **`T4-CL` — read, not worked, and the reorder that sent me there was wrong.** Both its halves are shipped and its own body hands the wider audit to `T4-CJ` slice 2 (~87 graph reads, one judgement each). Recorded on the ticket rather than silently re-pointed.
  - **`T1-FS` residual closed** — see the correction above; both file-keyed blocks now drop paths absent from `previousOutput()`, failing open on an empty map.
- **✅ THE THREE-RUN SERIES ANSWERED THE OCCURRENCE QUESTION AND REFUSED THE OUTCOME ONE.** Rule fixed before the runs; result **3 of 3** — every run independently produced `POST /loans → 201, but the running application answered 500`, caught by a probe that abstained a day earlier. Scores: **23/23 · 23/23 · artifact-build-failed** (run 3's target imported a package absent from its own pom; framework health 0 events, 0 exceptions from the new classes). **Two perfect runs are NOT support for the change** — the post-swap base rate is 4/10 = 40%, two of them predating everything here. The all-time 9.1% is the wrong denominator and would have made this look like a result.
- **THE DETERMINISTIC REPLAY IS THE REAL MEASUREMENT, and I only reached for it because the user asked whether to run more.** Over 8 preserved trees / 770 criteria: **74 body-carrying 2xx criteria all abstained before; 61 (82%) probeable now**, coverage 71.4% → 86.3%. Zero variance, no model calls, N no live batch could reach. **Reflex to keep: when a question is about COVERAGE or any property of a fixed corpus, replay answers it; live runs are only for occurrence.** [[feedback-replay-over-batches]]
- **⚠ THREE MONITORS IN ONE NIGHT, THREE VERSIONS OF ONE DEFECT — measuring the ARTIFACT OF LOGGING instead of the event.** (1) A liveness probe grepping `/proc/*/cmdline` for a pattern its own command line contained, so it could never see the run end. (2) A cumulative `BODY_REFUSED` tally read as a rate — it was flat at 1 per sweep. (3) A cumulative grep of contradictions reading **14** where the distinct-assertion count was **1**, because contradictions are re-rendered into records and prompts. Same root cause as the 2026-08-23 `Book` mention count (112 raw → 39 real). **Design rule: an instrument must count the EVENT — dedupe by identity, or read the producer's own per-sweep field — and must be calibrated against a corpus where it MUST be silent before it is trusted.** [[feedback-count-events-not-log-lines]]
- **⚠ A CALIBRATION LESSON FROM THE WATCH, worth more than the watch.** My first `fired` pattern for `t3bz-…` matched any write under a `/model/` or `/domain/` directory and scored **FIRED(36) on a run predating the mechanism**. The shipped pattern (a non-empty `Deleted Files:`, since relocating a type must delete the old file) scores `NO-OPPORTUNITY` on that same run. **Calibrate a new watch against a run where it MUST be silent before trusting it** — the same discipline as [[feedback-verify-population-before-claim]], one level down.
- **⚠ THE RUN-WATCH MONITOR COULD NOT SEE THE RUN END — pgrep self-match, recurring.** Its liveness probe greps every `/proc/*/cmdline` for `autobuilder-.*SNAPSHOT.jar`, but the monitor's OWN bash command line contains that literal pattern, so `alive` was permanently 1: it never broke its loop, never printed `run ended`, and kept emitting ticks with frozen grep counts from a dead run's static log (merged=43 at 03:45, JVM gone since 03:43:52). Already recorded as a trap in [[project-run-lifecycle-runbook]] and it bit again. **Fix: match the JVM by the pid preflight prints (`kill -0 "$pid"`), never by a pattern the watcher's own command line contains.** Violates [[feedback-monitor-every-terminal]] in the worst way — silence and ticks looked identical to a healthy run.

## 2026-08-22 (Opus 5) — MODEL SWAP TO qwen3.8, AND ITS REASONING IS A COST TO BE TURNED OFF

- **The local model is now `qwen3.8`** (Ollama `qwen3.8:latest`, dense 27.3B Q4_K_M, `family=qwen35`, capabilities completion/tools/**thinking**/vision, num_ctx 32768 = the configured `context-window-tokens`). Replaces `qwen3-coder:30b` in `application.yml`. Ollama resolves the bare `qwen3.8` to `:latest`.
- **A/B on the T5-AF labelled hollow/real corpus** (`CompletionVerifierPrecisionEvalTest`, 5 cases × hollow+real, temperature 0, single pass — **N=10 per arm, directional not statistical**):
  - `qwen3-coder:30b` — flat blob **precision 1.00 / recall 0.40**, with T4-BS wiring facts **1.00 / 0.60**.
  - `qwen3.8` reasoning at its default — **0.83 / 1.00** both arms (one false alarm on computed-pricing-real).
  - `qwen3.8` with reasoning suppressed — **1.00 / 1.00 both arms, TP=5 FP=0 TN=5 FN=0**.
  - **Recall is the metric that matters here** — a missed hollow artifact is a false completion (three recorded), a false alarm is one wasted cycle.
- **⚠ THINKING MADE IT WORSE AND MUCH SLOWER.** The single FP appeared only with reasoning on. Warm-model latency on a ~9.3k-token verifier prompt, prefix cache defeated per call: qwen3.8 **19-21 s** (~700-850 hidden reasoning tokens) vs qwen3.8 no-think **1.8 s** vs qwen3-coder:30b **4.6 s**. So suppressing reasoning is both more accurate and ~2.5× faster than the model it replaces; leaving it on is ~4× slower.
- **NEW KNOB `llm.api.reasoning-effort`** (blank default = send nothing, behaviour-preserving): optional `reasoning_effort` on `ChatRequest`, `@JsonInclude(NON_NULL)` so non-reasoning endpoints are untouched; read by `LLMClient`; overridable in the eval test via `LLM_REASONING_EFFORT`. Hidden reasoning tokens also eat `response-reserve-tokens` (4096).
- **Ollama puts reasoning in a separate `reasoning` field on the OpenAI-compatible endpoint, NOT inside `content`** — no `<think>` leakage into any parser, and the RestTemplate's own ObjectMapper already ignores unknown properties, so no DTO change was needed to survive the swap.
- **⚠ T4-BS wiring facts showed ZERO measured contribution under qwen3.8** (both arms identical in both conditions) vs +1 TP under qwen3-coder. Read as **ceiling saturation on a 5-case corpus, not refutation** — there is no headroom left for an arm to differ. A harder corpus is needed before touching that mechanism.
- **✅ FIELD DoD MET, AND THE FIRST RUN SCORED 23/23 ON BOTH TREES** — run `2026-08-22_00-23-46` (deadline, 88.4 min, 74 cycles, 24 merges): `smoke=23/23` · `smoke_strict=23/23 discounted=none` · `smoke_preserved=23/23` · `smoke_preserved_strict=23/23 discounted=none`, `preserved_source=reconstructed:cycle-1787356300342` (74/74, peaked on the last cycle), 0 watches needing attention, 1/11 features outside the healthy envelope. **First row in the 68-run ledger where all four numbers are 23/23** — the 2026-08-04 perfect run's preserved tree was 19/23.
- **⚠ DO NOT ATTRIBUTE THE SCORE TO THE MODEL — N=1 under known run variance.** What the run DOES establish at N=515 is a per-call property, not a run outcome: **515 LLM responses, 0 empty, 0 parse failures, 0 circuit events, 0 timeouts.** qwen3.8's output shape fits every parser under live load. `CriteriaCheck lines parsed: 5 of 5 expected` throughout. Median cycle 75 s, `score_delta=+0`, `criteria_executable_pct=59`.
- **⚠ THE PREFLIGHT MODEL PROBE BLOCKED THE FIRST LAUNCH — a gate testing something the app never does.** `probe_model` sent `max_tokens: 5` with no reasoning budget and read only `content`; on a reasoning model all five tokens went to hidden reasoning, `content` was empty, and G2 reported `model-unreachable` against a healthy endpoint (Ollama up, 275 ms once fixed). Fixed in `scripts/preflight.sh` `probe_model`: reads the deployed `reasoning-effort` from `application.yml` and sends it, counts `content` OR `reasoning` as an answer, budget 5→16. **Also note the 15 s `model-slow` gate would have blocked EVERY launch with reasoning left on** (19-21 s per real call).
- **⚠ MY OWN RUN-WATCH FILTERS WERE WRONG TWICE, BOTH THE SAME MISTAKE — inventing patterns instead of reusing preflight's.** A guessed merge pattern reported `merges=0` while 5 cycles had merged (real marker: `CycleMergeTracker - Marked cycle <id> as merged`, one line per event); a case-insensitive `Goal fulfilled` matched the substring in **Sub**`goal fulfilled` and raised a false terminal alarm on a 40-minute-stale line. **Copy preflight's watcher patterns verbatim** (`Result: GOAL_FULFILLED|root .* marked COMPLETED|Root goal exhausted|root .* marked GIVEN_UP` + the pause/brake set), case-sensitive. Related: [[feedback-count-events-not-log-lines]], [[feedback-monitor-every-terminal]].
- **Mid-run the loop stalled ~15 min at 7 merges** on duplicate classes split across `demo` and `library` packages, then broke out on its own — it named the root cause, injected a dedup subgoal, and merges resumed and accelerated (7→8→9→10→…→24). Diagnosis machinery worked; no intervention.
- **TWO MORE EVAL SURFACES A/B'd (2026-08-22, both models, reasoning suppressed on qwen3.8):**
  - **T5-AG `EvaluationPromptWordingEvalTest`** — the PRODUCTION `EvaluationProtocolContract` + `EvaluationResponseParser`, a different surface from the verifier's own prompt. **Acceptance state: both models 10/10** (every hollow REJECTED, every real ACCEPTED). **Criteria marks: qwen3.8 10/10, qwen3-coder 9/10** (`invalid-checkout-4xx|real` marked ✗ at score 0.67).
  - **⚠ THE REAL FINDING IS DECISION MARGIN, NOT ACCURACY.** Against `heuristic-threshold: 0.4` — qwen3-coder's hollow scores are 0.00/0.25/0.25/0.35/0.35, so **two hollow artifacts sit 0.05 below the accept line**; qwen3.8's are 0.12/0.15/0.15/0.12/0.15, **0.25-0.28 below**. Real-artifact scores 0.67-0.92 vs 0.85-0.95. Separation 0.32 vs 0.70. A 0.05 margin is one noise excursion away from accepting a hollow artifact — exactly the recorded false-completion failure mode.
  - **T2-EP `PromptCompactionLlmEvalTest`** — **no discrimination, both models identical**: `surface1/healthy` old=NOOP new=NOOP, `surface1/rollback` old=APPLIED new=APPLIED, `surface2/repetition` valid=true. Confirms qwen3.8 preserves the compaction property; it is a gate on prompt changes, not a model yardstick. Don't re-run it to compare models.
  - **⚠ BOTH EVALS DRAW ON THE SAME 5 `HollowVsRealFixtures` SCENARIOS.** The 20 decision points are 5 scenarios probed two ways, NOT 20 independent items. **The corpus is the binding limit on every accuracy claim about this swap** — a harder/larger labelled corpus is the highest-value next instrument.
  - Both eval tests now take `LLM_REASONING_EFFORT` (same pattern as the verifier eval), so an A/B runs the deployed config rather than the model's default.
- **⚠ THE SMOKE SCORE CANNOT SETTLE A MODEL SWAP — and my first baseline for it was WRONG.** I computed "42 scored runs, mean 17.2, stdev 4.5" by regexing `\d+/23` out of the ledger's `smoke` field, which **silently dropped 26 runs whose artifact did not build or boot** — 39%% of the corpus, and failures rather than missing data. Scored honestly (build/boot failure = 0, same rule applied to both arms) the real baseline is **n=67, mean 10.4, median 11, stdev 9.0, 26/67 zeros, 3 runs at 23/23**. **Never regex a score field without first counting the rows that do not match it.**
- **⚠ 39%% OF ALL RUNS EVER RECORDED END WITH AN ARTIFACT THAT WILL NOT BUILD OR BOOT** (26/67 pre-swap, 2/5 post-swap). That is the largest single fact in the ledger and it was invisible while the mean was computed over survivors only. Likely worth its own ticket.
- Detecting even a +3 mean shift against stdev 9.0 needs ~140 runs per arm. The ledger also spans a month of loop code changes, so the full history is not a clean model baseline; the recency-matched comparison is the last 12 pre-swap runs (mean 16.9). Use the per-item harnesses; see [[feedback-replay-over-batches]].
- **FIVE-RUN LIVE SERIES (2026-08-22 02:54-07:16, 90 min each, independent draws — `reset-on-start=true`/`resume-from-preserved=false`, so every run re-scaffolds from zero): qwen3.8 scores 23, 0, 5, 23, 0 → mean 10.2 vs baseline 10.4. Difference -0.2, ~0.0 SE. INDISTINGUISHABLE.** Against the recency-matched last-12 baseline (16.9) it looks worse, at ~1.3 SE — also not significant. **The live runs do NOT support "qwen3.8 is better"; the per-item evals do. Not a contradiction — the evals measure judgement on a labelled corpus, the runs measure an end-to-end outcome swamped by variance.**
  - 2 of the 5 were all-four-perfect 23/23 (runs `00-23-46`, `04-48-23`) — 2 of the only 5 perfect runs ever — and 2 were zeros. Same config, same target, same model.
  - **⚠ A 6th run was LOST to preflight's G2 slow gate: `model-slow:18559ms` was a COLD LOAD** (my own qwen3-coder eval had just evicted qwen3.8), not the busy queue the gate exists to catch. **Second false-negative shape in that same gate in one night.** Mitigated without touching the script (a running series reads it — [[feedback-never-edit-a-running-script]]) by a keep-warm pinger, 1 token/2 min, because the app's own calls reset Ollama's expiry to the 5-min default and the between-run scoring window is longer than that. **The gate still needs fixing: it cannot distinguish load-from-disk from a backlogged queue.**
  - Use `smoke` (final tree) as the primary axis and `smoke_preserved` as a separate secondary; run `02-54-35` was build-failed final with 23/23 preserved, and quoting the preserved number as the headline would be picking the flattering measurement.
- **✅ DRIFT MEASURED OVER THE WHOLE LEDGER — rare, catastrophic, and NOT the main failure.** `drift_tail_cycles` is stored on 64 of 72 rows, so this needed no surviving run_dirs. **4 of 64 runs (6%) ended after their peak; all 4 scored 0** (tails 1, 3, 12, and **64** cycles — `2026-08-05_22-14-13` spent 64 cycles past a 22/23 peak). **Decomposition of the 28 zero-scoring runs: 4 drifted off a working artifact, 24 NEVER REACHED ONE.** So preserve/restore-at-halt would rescue ~6% of runs; **86% of failures are a generation problem, not a preservation one.** Fixing drift is worth doing and is not the headline.
- **✅ NEW INSTRUMENT `scripts/convergence_report.py`** (+ `test_convergence_report.py`, 17 stdlib tests, no maven/model) — bands runs by outcome, splits failures into drifted vs never-there, and ranks per-run features by Cohen's d between failed and strong runs. Reports COVERAGE per feature (the ledger is sparse) and tags each EARLY (observable mid-run, so a live brake could key on it) or LATE (explains, cannot warn). Prints `CONVERGENCE: runs= failed= drifted= never-there= top-early=` as its last line.
  - **⚠ FINDING: every top separator is EARLY, and they all say the same thing — FAILING RUNS REPEAT THEMSELVES AND MERGE LESS.** `repeats_refused` d=1.17 (7.50 in failed vs 1.93 in strong), `merges_per_cycle` d=1.01 (0.28 vs 0.44), `injector_files_created` d=0.93 (coverage only 16), `repeat_proposals`/`repeat_rate` d=0.85, `merges` d=0.75. Matches the field observation: `04-25-58` halted while looping BUILD_FIX on one file.
  - **⚠ THE INSTRUMENT CANNOT YET SHOW THESE PREDICT EARLY.** The ledger stores END-OF-RUN TOTALS, not time series, so this proves "failed runs repeated more *in total*", NOT "at cycle 20 you could tell". Part of it is near-tautological (a run that never merges cannot produce a working artifact). **Closing that gap needs per-cycle data from `behaviour.ndjson`, and only 5 run_dirs survive `/tmp` clearing — so capture per-cycle features from now on or the question stays open.**
- **STATE:** suite **2499/0/0/5** green. **Committed on branch `qwen3.8-swap` (4 commits, NOT pushed, NOT merged to main)** — model swap + `reasoning-effort` knob; the two preflight G2 fixes; `convergence_report.py` + 26 tests; ledger/memory records. Working tree clean. **`main` still has the old model**, so merge before the next run series or it will run qwen3-coder.

- **🔴 THE HONEST RESULT ON THE DAY: THREE MECHANISMS FIELD-PROVEN, ZERO OUTCOME MOVEMENT.** Every block built today demonstrably changes what the model writes next — it creates the types it is told to create (T1-FR), adds the members it is told to add (T1-FQ), and fixed the call site it was told to fix (T2-FD). ## 2026-08-02 EARLY (Opus 5, autonomous) — A STATED OUTCOME TARGET, THE GAP MEASURED, AND TWO REFUTATIONS THAT SAVE THE OBVIOUS BUILD

**The user asked what percentage baseline to aim for, then said go. `T6-L` is the answer as a tracked ticket.**

- **✅ THE METRIC CHOICE IS THE FINDING: measure per RUN ATTEMPTED, not per scored run.** Mean-of-scored is survivorship bias — the OLDER ledger half shows a *better* median (21/23 vs 15/23) purely because only **31%** of its runs were scorable, against **92%** in the last 12. Counting a non-building artifact as 0 is the only form that cannot be gamed by failures leaving the denominator. **All-time 37% · newer half 48% · last 12 runs 62%.**
- **✅ TARGET SET: 75% per attempt** (≈17.3/23, scorability ≥90%) — one chain-link away, not aspirational. Ladder: 85%, then reproducible 23/23 (reached twice, so the ceiling is real).
- **✅ THE GAP IS MEASURED AND NAMED.** Built `scripts/smoke_chain_report.py` (RAW vs **ORIGINATED** — failed while every dependency was observed AND passed) and `scripts/rescore_preserved.sh` (backfills sheets by booting preserved trees; a measurement, not a reconstruction; refuses to run beside a live autobuilder). **23 runs re-scored, 25 with sheets.**
  - **⚠ DIRECT is a firm floor; UNLOCKS are an upper bound.** Fixing check 1 lets 6/13/15/16 be measured honestly — it does not make them correct. **Choose work by DIRECT points.**
  - On direct points: **loan referential 404 + limits (14,15,19,22) = 1.76 pts/run, then input validation (2,3,4,9,10) = 1.36 pts/run.** Those two alone take **62% → 75%** with no reliance on unlocks.
  - **An interim read over 12 runs had the top three in the WRONG ORDER** and is recorded as such — half a corpus promoted the creates and demoted the loans family. Full table in [[project-smoke-score-is-chained]].
- **🔴 TWO REFUTATIONS, EACH KILLING A PLAUSIBLE NEXT BUILD:**
  - **"Make the failing check probeable" does NOT help.** `[19]` leads and the loop cannot see it, so probe coverage looks like the lever. But `T4-DB` slice 1 made `[14]`/`[15]` probeable on 07-30 17:43, and across that boundary both went **64% → 18%** — the same drop as `[19]`, which gained no probe — while control `[10]` went **43% → 64%**. A common cause in the loans family, not a probe effect.
  - **Throughput does not explain it either:** loans pass **50%** under 40 cycles, **48%** at 40+.
  - **Suggestive and deliberately NOT acted on:** validation strength vs loans strength **r = −0.41, n=25** (weak-validation runs pass 75% of loans, strong-validation 42%). Fits `T4-DB`'s "attention follows the probes", but one arm is n=5 and confounded with overall score. Needs a within-run attention measurement.
- **⚠ THE TARGET IS OURS, NOT THE LOOP'S.** smoke23 is the independent referee — the whole false-completion history is why. Nothing from T6-L may reach a prompt or a goal.
- **Single most striking datum:** one run scored **22/23 and its only failure was check 10** (`POST /members` no name → 400).
